# The rulebook

Every decision a mesh run makes on its own is made by a written rule. Each rule has a stable ID,
and each decision the run records in its "why" record names the rule behind it
(`contracts/decision_record.py`). This file is the one home for those rules.

Rulebook version: **135**

How the book is kept honest:

- A rule's ID never changes and is never reused. A rule that stops applying is marked `retired`,
  not deleted.
- **In force** means the code makes this decision today. **Planned** means the ID is reserved
  for a later PR (named in brackets) and no code may cite it yet.
- `tests/unit/hygiene/test_rulebook.py` checks, on every PR, that every rule ID the code cites is
  in force here, that every in-force rule here is cited by the code, and that the version above
  matches `RULES_VERSION` in `contracts/decision_record.py`.
- Changing what a rule decides means changing this book in the same PR and raising the version.

Each decision in the record also says where it came from:

| Source | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `rule` | The code decided by the rule, from measured or declared facts. |
| `user` | The user decided (named an engine, or confirmed one). The rule says why that stands. |
| `ai-lever` | The AI made a judgement call from a declared list (the FEA builder's AI-fea-n), and the rules made it exact inside their limits. |
| `guard-clock` | A guard clock tripped (TIME-3), or one of the clocks still left for the CAD repair work decided (TIME-6). Since D3 no other clock decides anything. |

---

## ENG: which engine meshes the run

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| ENG-1 | in force | **Only engines that can take the case are ranked.** An engine is a candidate only if its declared capability covers the flow, the file's form, the number of separate passages and the layers asked for. |
| ENG-2 | in force | **Home turf first.** An engine that declares the measured shape class its home (`EngineSpec.home_classes`: VMTK for tubes and branching passages) comes first, unless its own turf rows mark the case a weak spot or outside. Two home engines rank between themselves by ENG-4. |
| ENG-3 | in force | **The lab record overrides home turf only when it clearly wins:** the challenger has at least 20 runs on at least 8 different shapes of this class, no quality fault, and a 95% lower bound above the home engine's 95% upper bound. A home engine whose own record of 20+ runs here shows a quality fault also loses first place. A home engine never tried on the class keeps it ("home turf unproven"). |
| ENG-4 | in force | **The best lab record on shapes like this one comes first.** The run's geometry is measured, and engines are ranked by the Wilson 95% lower bound of their pass rate on its shape class (`engines/fitness.py`), each run short of 20 counted as a miss, so 7 of 8 never beats 14 of 19. The table is dev-side runs only, versioned (`docs/engines/fitness-changelog.md`). An engine with a quality fault ranks after the sound ones; an engine whose declared turf puts the shape outside is never recommended. Ties go to fewer broken meshes, then fewer bad cells, then the declared turf, layers and ladder order. Time never breaks a tie. |
| ENG-5 | in force | **The fallback ladder walks the same ranking**, after the approved engine, minus the engines already tried and the engines that would refuse the case. With nothing measured, the same rules rank the engines on their record over the whole flow, never a fixed order. |
| ENG-6 | in force | **The user's pick stands.** An engine the user named is never switched on its own; another engine is only offered. A dispute keeps the engine of the mesh it disputes. |
| ENG-7 | in force | **An engine that reads the upload's own format comes first**, measured or not: a conversion loses what the file describes (an ECXML's parts, materials and powers). |
| ENG-8 | in force | **Nothing measured, nothing named: the declared default.** When the run's geometry could not be measured, a run nobody chose an engine for takes the engine the declarations name for its flow (or the flow-only order). |
| ENG-9 | in force | **The user confirmed an engine the measured ranking did not put first** (for example, the ranking was not ready while the user was asked). The confirmed engine stands. This is a gap D1 closes: the proposal should always be the ranked one. |
| ENG-10 | in force | **What the user typed steers the engine** (rulebook 107, `engines/cfd_requirements.steer`, `pipeline/engine_select.engine_for_asks`). Before the first attempt of a flow run whose engine the system picked (recommended and confirmed, or picked with nobody asking), the notes are read (the same reading the builder makes next) and each engine valid for the run - it admits the request and meshes its passages (ENG-1, ENG-5's `able_engines`), and its own declared turf does not mark the case weak or outside (ENG-2) - scores the asks it has a setting for: an ask with a number the user typed counts 2, a vague one ("finer around the bend") 1. What each engine has no setting for is declared once in `cfd_requirements` (`FIRST_LAYER_ENGINES`, `NO_LAYER_ENGINES`, `STACK_ENGINES`, `CELL_GROWTH_ENGINES`, `PLACE_SIZE_ENGINES`, `NO_SETTING`, and a control's NotHere lane), the same fields its applier tells as not applied. The run moves only when another engine scores strictly more; ties go to the engine that delivers an absolute layer height over the whole wall first when one is asked (CFDREQ-10's `LAYER_HEIGHT_DELIVERS`), then the ladder's order. The move is told in one plain line on the run's engine line and kept with the result ("Meshed with snappyHexMesh, because it can set the first layer for the y+ you asked for, which VMTK has no setting for."), with what the new engine cannot set either; the result's rows say each ask applied or not, as before. Recorded as stage `engine_for_asks` (inputs: who chose, the order, the scores) and replayed by `decision_record.engine_for_asks`. Never an engine the user named (ENG-6: the result says what it could not apply), never a dispute, and nothing typed changes nothing. CFDREQ-10 (the card's reading before the run) uses the same pick. |

## SW: whether a run moves to another engine after a failed attempt

Decided by `pipeline/engine_fallback.decide`. The run moves only on measured facts, never on a
review. The order the run moves in is ENG-5.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| SW-1 | in force | **Move on: the mesh came out broken** (checkMesh's fatal defects). The next engine in the ladder runs at once. |
| SW-2 | in force | **Move on: the engine could not build a mesh** (it crashed, stopped early or refused the input). |
| SW-3 | in force | **Move on: the same measured failure repeated** on this engine. |
| SW-4 | in force | **Move on: the shared attempts go to the untried engine** when a retry here would leave it no attempt of its own. |
| SW-5 | in force | **Stay: a measured check failed, and this engine's own levers are tried first**, because a retry here still leaves every untried engine an attempt. |
| SW-6 | in force | **Stay: a retry here fits** and still leaves every untried engine an attempt. |
| SW-7 | in force | **Stay: a review never moves the run.** The mesh passed every measured check. |
| SW-8 | in force | **Stay: the engine is not ours to change** (the user named it, or a dispute; see ENG-6). |
| SW-9 | in force | **Stay: another engine cannot change this failure** (the request, the approval or a region split), or the mesh is sound and only short of one of our own bars: its own resolution ladder answers it (LAD-cfd-1), never a switch. |
| SW-10 | in force | **Stay: no untried engine delivers the approved mesh** (same boundaries, units, cell kind and layers). |
| SW-11 | in force | **Stay: the untried engines would refuse** this many separate passages in one job. |
| SW-12 | in force | **Stay: no regular attempt is left.** |
| SW-13 | retired (D3) | **Stay: too little time left** for the next engine's run. Retired: no clock decides a switch any more (TIME-1). A next engine that runs past the run's guards is a guard trip (TIME-3). |
| SW-14 | in force | **Stay: the fallback ladder is switched off** by a setting. |
| SW-15 | in force | **No decision: the last attempt did not fail.** |
| SW-16 | in force | **Move on, to another kind of mesh: the system's own pick made no mesh, or a broken one** (a time-out, a crash, a stop before meshing, broken cells), and no untried engine delivers the same kind. The next engine in the ranking takes the run when the only differences are the mesh's file format and cell type: never fewer layers, a worse wall fit, a lost requirement or a setting. The user is told what changes, as it happens and beside the delivered mesh. Never for an engine the user named (ENG-6). |
| SW-17 | in force | **Move on: the mesh was built, a check refused it, and another pass on this engine could not change that** (LAD-cfd-8, `pipeline/engine_fallback.GATE_LEVERS`). The check is named in the record. An untried engine that delivers the same approved mesh takes the run; SW-2 stays for an engine that built nothing. |

## DEL: which valid mesh a run delivers

When a run built more than one valid mesh, `application/review_delivery.rank_key` ranks them by a
fixed order of measured facts. The first fact that separates the winner from the runner-up is the
rule. An invalid mesh is never delivered.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| DEL-0 | in force | **The only valid mesh** the run built is the one delivered. |
| DEL-1 | in force | **A mesh the review confirmed is the wrong problem ranks last** (a cited, inspected wrong-problem finding, not a concern). Shadow period only: once `VERDICT_BY_LIMITS` is on, a review ranks nothing and this position never separates two meshes. |
| DEL-2 | in force | **A mesh that passes every fixed quality limit beats one that does not.** |
| DEL-3 | in force | **Fewer failed quality checks wins.** |
| DEL-4 | in force | **Meeting more of the approved plan wins** (layers on the wall, wake refinement, stated sizes). |
| DEL-5 | in force | **Better worst cells win**, compared to the whole degree of non-orthogonality. |
| DEL-6 | in force | **On a measured tie, the mesh whose review passed wins.** Shadow period only: once `VERDICT_BY_LIMITS` is on, a review ranks nothing and a tie on everything measured goes to the earlier attempt (DEL-10). |
| DEL-7 | in force | **Lower worst non-orthogonality wins** (exact value). |
| DEL-8 | in force | **Lower worst skewness wins.** |
| DEL-9 | in force | **More wall covered by prism layers wins.** |
| DEL-10 | in force | **The earlier attempt wins** when everything measured is the same. |

## VER: pass, delivered with concerns, or fail

Decided by `contracts/decision_record.verdict` from measured numbers alone, read off the delivered
mesh by `engines/verdict_limits.py`. Every finished run's why record carries one `verdict` entry:
the outcome, the VER rule, and every row below with its measured number, its limit and its
source, so the verdict replays from the record. Nothing the AI reviewer wrote is an input.

**The AI reviewer only adds notes.** It still looks at the pictures. Its points are shown to the
customer under "Reviewer notes", each one labelled "Reviewer note". It cannot change a status,
start a rebuild, a bonus attempt or a retry, rank one mesh over another, or fail a job as the
wrong problem. A user's own dispute ("rebuild it") is the user's decision and still rebuilds.

**Shadow first.** The setting `VERDICT_BY_LIMITS` (off by default) switches the limits verdict on.
While it is off, the review-led verdict still decides, and the limits verdict is worked out on
every run beside it: one `verdict shadow:` log line (limits outcome, rule, review-led outcome,
whether they agree, the limits missed) and the `verdict` entry in the why record.

**On for dev.** The deploy target turns it on for shared dev and every personal environment
(`verdict_by_limits` in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, sent to the workers as template metadata,
so a fleet deploy applies it). Prod keeps the default until dev has run with it.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| VER-1 | in force | **Fail: no valid mesh.** The executor's gates refused the last attempt, or the mesh's own numbers fail one of the engine's gating rows (its `criteria.py` rows marked gating: checkMesh fatal defects, wall captured, ports captured, the quality floors, regions and interfaces). An invalid mesh is never delivered. Invalid is what checkMesh v2412 itself fails on validity, read the same way in every OpenFOAM engine (`engines/checkmesh_validity.py`): negative-volume cells, wrongly oriented faces, open cells, empty patches on a 3D mesh, zero-area faces, faces 90 degrees or more out of square (checkMesh's non-orthogonality errors), faces out of upper-triangular order, unused points, duplicates, points out of range and broken topology; skewness, aspect ratio, determinants and weights stay quality. Two zero-area faces and two faces at 118.7 degrees on a cfMesh manifold (board batch 3), five and five on a cfMesh cooling pipe (batch 2), had been read as no defect and delivered with concerns; the cfMesh rescue had even kept the manifold's broken mesh as its best. The resolution floor, the share of skewed faces and the one-job cell limit are not validity: on a sound mesh they are notes (LAD-cfd-3, LAD-cfd-4). |
| VER-2 | in force | **Delivered with concerns:** the mesh is valid but misses at least one production limit (table below). Each one is listed as measured vs limit. |
| VER-3 | in force | **Clean pass:** valid, and every production limit that applies is met (a limit whose number was not measured is listed as unmeasured and decides nothing). |
| VER-4 | in force | **Wrong problem, by measured checks only.** The run failed at a gate that measures one of the ways a mesh can be a different problem than the one confirmed (table below). The gate's own measured account is what the user reads. |
| VER-5 | in force | **A review never reruns or fails a run once the limits decide.** When a mesh passed every gate, its review ends the run whatever it said, and also when it did not conclude (the model was busy, down, or gave no answer). No review rerun, no rebuild, no failure: the measured limits decide. A crash after the review is rescued the same way, whatever the review said. A user's own dispute keeps its routing. |
| VER-6 | in force | **The recorded review verdict is the measured one once the limits decide.** A review that ran on a mesh every gate passed records PASS (job row and final result) when the measured limits deliver it, pass or pass with notes. The model's own call is kept beside its notes, labelled. Job fb44fed7 recorded the model's FAIL on a valid FEA mesh. |
| VER-7 | in force | **A reviewer point a measurement settles is not an open point.** When a review axis is about a property a measured row measures exactly (layer coverage, cell quality, regions, interfaces, passage resolution, FEA element validity and detail, the FEA face accounting), and that row was measured and met, the point is kept with the measurement that settles it, and is not shown as a note to act on. A point no measurement speaks to stays a note. |
| VER-8 | in force | **The mesh on disk is the case written last, never the one whose name sorts last** (`engines/surface_deliverable._patch_files`, read by the CAD check, the delivered surface and the port areas). Every snappy pass writes its boundary to VTK/<its case>_<time>/; a pass the size brake stopped leaves its own beside the delivered one, all at time 0. The boundary read is the newest case's (by when its files were written), at its latest time. RM3 on 470350f3: two stopped passes (far field only) sorted after the delivered case by name, and the CAD check read no body off a 6.05 M-cell production-grade mesh (RM5 before it: "100% of your CAD not reached"). |
| VER-9 | in force | **A change asked on a delivered mesh is a requirement of a new run, never a review of the old one** (rulebook 95, `application/change_request.py`, the route `api/v1/simulation._change_request`). The person marks spots on the finished mesh ("Mark region"), types what they want and sends it (`POST /simulation/{job_id}/dispute`). Words that ask for a change of the mesh - a number, a size, finer or coarser, layers, a count, a quality word (`change_request.is_change`, read in code as the plan step tells a requirement, NOTE-18) - start a NEW JOB of the same part on the same conversation at once: the finished run's own dispatch payload (the confirmed setup, the unit, the engine, the saved settings as read then, every earlier note), with the words added as one more of the user's notes carrying the faces the marks tie (VER-10, NOTE-17's one rule), and the change in the review brief. The reviewer does not decide whether the change happens; it reviews the new mesh afterwards, as any mesh. Before (25 Nov, live FEA solid): "make the edges around this hole finer, nowhere else" with two marks went to the reviewer as a re-inspection of the OLD mesh, which said "The mesh meets your brief.", and the FEA rebuild re-planned the same mesh because the words never reached the plan. The note it writes is stamped with the finished run (`change_of`, `contracts/user_notes.RULE_CHANGE_OF`, rulebook 98), so "finer" or "coarser" with no number at the marked faces is against the size that run had there, not the mesh round them (PLAN-fea-36); the reply says the step in plain words when it is one ("Got it: finer on Face 100 only, about half the size the last mesh had there, and nothing else changes. Starting a new run; the last one took 3 min.", `change_request.size_step`, the fact `size_change_there` for VER-12's model) - the cost is not said, as the product cannot work it out at that moment without reading the notes again with the model and planning the part (`fea_plan_options.requirements_cost` needs the check's record, the part's surface and both readings). A flow mesh refines by its own levels at the marked faces and the result says what it did (VER-12's flow line); no step is said for it. |
| VER-10 | in force | **Each mark is tied to the CAD face(s) under it, in the upload's own frame and unit, and "only there" holds** (rulebook 95, `change_request.resolve_marks`, `engines/mesh_requirements.says_whole_mesh`). A mark's point is the delivered mesh's (metres, the part's frame: `contracts/mesh_units`); it is read on the upload's face map the stage's click uses (`application/face_pick.current`, `cad/face_map.face_at`) with the map's unit ratio. The face right under it is always meant; another face within the marked circle's radius (the viewer's span over `VIEWER_FLAG_SPAN_FACTOR`) is meant too when the whole face is about the circle's size (at most 4 times its diameter: the hole's wall, a fillet inside it), never a large face that only passes under it. A point just off the skin (a shell mid-surface) takes the nearest face inside the circle. At most 20 faces. Words that say only there ("nowhere else", "just that", "only", "specifically") beat a whole-mesh word, so the words stay on the picked faces (NOTE-17's `picked_for`) and nothing else in the plan changes. |
| VER-11 | in force | **Words that only doubt the mesh keep the reviewer's second look, at marks it can show** (rulebook 95, `change_request.is_change`, `agents/reviewer/context.py`, `contracts/human_flags.mark_radius`). "This looks bad, look again", "is this ok?", or marks with no words: the reviewer re-inspects the same mesh at the marks (the dispute run, as before). Each mark is handed over with its call (`go_to_coordinates` in metres) and the radius of the circle drawn; a viewer patch name the review session cannot isolate (an FEA mesh's `Face_k` against Gmsh's groups) is told as where the mark is, with "without patch_name", never as a name to isolate - before, every view of the marks was refused and the review never saw them. |
| VER-12 | in force | **The reply to a change request is written by the model, under the plan step's guard; a mark off the part is said** (rulebook 95, `agents/intake/change_reply.py`, `change_request.plain_reply`). The intake's own model (NOTE-19's `call_intake_model`, its voice and language blocks, its own block registry `CHANGE_REPLY_PROMPT_BLOCKS`) writes what it understood, where (the faces), that nothing else changes, that a new run is starting, and the last run's time when known. The guard is NOTE-19's `plan_reply.check` plus: a new run said, never a verdict on the old mesh ("meets your brief", "looks fine"), marks off the part said. Refused, failed or slower than 6 s: "Got it: I'll make that change on Face 12 · round Ø15 mm only, and nothing else changes. Starting a new run; the last one took 29 min.". Marks that tie no face are left out and said; when no mark ties a face nothing starts and the reply asks for the spot on the part itself; words naming one place with marks on several separate spots go on every spot marked, said; on a flow mesh whose mesher cannot size one face (all but snappyHexMesh, NOTE-17) the reply says so and the result tells what it did. |
| VER-13 | in force | **Separate passages meshed side by side are not a fault, and openings left unmeshed are told** (rulebook 95; `engines/verdict_limits.inputs_for`, `engines/manifest.write_manifest`, `cad/fluid_passages.passages_side_by_side`). A case meshed as several separate passages (INS-39: mergeMeshes puts them side by side, and a passage cut in two is refused) is held to as many connected cell regions as the passages the plan meshed, not to one: the manifest records `passages_side_by_side` from the case's passages record, and the regions row's limit is that number. Regions equal to it read as met, in the words that the part has that many separate flow volumes; more regions than passages is still a miss. Every opening the staging left unmeshed (INS-40) is a row of its own (`openings_not_meshed`, op none) in the words the user was told before meshing - a concern, never silently met. |
| VER-14 | in force | **The CAD check holds an internal mesh to the fluid's own boundary, never to the part's whole surface with the lids on** (rulebook 99; `engines/cad_fidelity.both_sides`, `cad_reference`). A staged surface in which three or more triangles share an edge holds both sides of the wall: at each lid's rim the bore, the part's end face and the lid meet (cfMesh's geom.stl on a pipe is the part's surface plus the lids). It is not the fluid's boundary, so the mesh is held instead to the fluid boundary the shared staging closes from the run's file and openings (the one snappy's runs are held to); when that cannot be staged, the surface is used as one that bounds the metal too (the wall is held to it, the share not reached is not judged). The bar does not move: a mesh that misses part of the fluid's boundary is still a concern or a rebuild. Batch 7: cfMesh meshes of abc 00188110 and 00189603 read "49% / 27% of your CAD surface is not reached" against 199 / 349 cm2 of reference round 100 / 174 cm2 of fluid wall; against the fluid boundary they miss 0.0% with the fluid volume within 0.11%. |
| VER-15 | in force | **The CAD check holds an internal mesh to the fluid's side of the surface its engine was staged with, before any re-staging** (rulebook 103; `engines/cad_fidelity.wetted_side`, `cad_reference`, `WETTED_SIDE_SOURCE`, `_drawn`). When the staged surface is the part's surface with the lids on (VER-14) or only its parts are on disk, the parts and the lids on the confirmed openings are drawn into the outside-skin voxel grid (half the mesh's wall cell); the free space a lid closes off from the outside air - the first free voxel off either side of each lid triangle that is not the air - is the fluid, and every staged face within two voxels of it is the reference: the bore, its walls and the lids, never the outside of the part. A wall thinner than the band keeps its outer face too, which lies within a cell of the bore, so it is never read as missed. The volume is not judged on it (not a closed surface). A lid with outside air on both sides (a passage that leaks) cannot tell the fluid: then the fluid boundary staged afresh from the metre surface (VER-14) is next, staged once per file and openings in a process (it was staged again in every attempt's check: abc 00145189, 209 s and 225 s). Why first: the re-staging takes the surface path, whose lids are not the B-rep's the engine was staged with, and closed another region on batch 7: abc 00157383, its inlet lid 2 mm inside the bore on a 57 mm loop, not the confirmed 61.07 mm mouth the mesh's inlet matches (28.96 cm2 against 29.29), reading the mesh's inlet as 24% of its wall off the CAD; abc 00131564, the part's metal (230.5 cm2, 23.0 cm3) against the 59.8 cm3 fluid the engine meshed, refusing a sound cfMesh mesh as a rebuild. The bar does not move: half the passage missed on the fluid's side is still a rebuild (test). |
| VER-16 | in force | **No unjudged pass: a share not reached that could not be judged is a concern, said as that** (rulebook 103; `engines/cad_fidelity.measure` `cad_to_mesh_not_judged`, `concerns`). When an internal mesh is held to a staged surface that bounds the metal too - no closed fluid boundary, no lid that closes the fluid off (VER-15), no re-staging - its wall is still held to it, and whether it reaches all of the fluid's boundary is not judged: that measure reads as a concern, in the words "Whether the mesh reaches all of your CAD surface was not judged: the fluid's side of the wall could not be told from your file and its openings, so this check is not counted as met", never as met. It never refuses a mesh and never skips a rescue (TIME-15 reads only a measured share). Batch 7: abc 00157702 passed the CAD check with the share not reached unjudged (its re-staging failed: "the openings confirmed on the picture do not close the fluid in"). |
| VER-17 | in force | **A second look or a change on a delivered mesh works whichever worker takes it, and a mesh no longer kept is told, never our failure** (rulebook 118; `application/review_seed.py`, `application/artifact_uploader._build_plan`, `application/job_service.seed_dispute_run` / `refuse_dispute`, `api/v1/simulation._finished_parent` / `_change_request`, `application/change_request.UPLOAD_GONE` / `ChangeFacts.marks_lost`). A delivered attempt sits only on the disk of the worker that collected it (a big mesh made on a VM, RUN-10, is sent home to that worker and the exchange is emptied). Dev, 26 Nov: job 6bce9dfd (641,000 elements, three workers) was marked and sent; another worker took it, found no workspace and the run failed in 1.3 s as "Something went wrong on our side". Now the delivery also stores a review seed (`jobs/<job>/review_seed.tar.gz`, an internal `viewer_data` row `review_seed`, never offered for download, optional): the attempt's manifest, request and brief, the files the manifest names for the reviewer (its surface and volume, up to 1 GiB each) and every other file up to 32 MiB, at most 2 GiB in all; never archives (the bundle and solver zips are downloads already) and never the record of files a big result kept in the exchange bucket (emptied after delivery, RUN-7). Deterministic, so a repeated delivery stores the same bytes. A second look (VER-11) on a worker without the attempt restores it from the seed to the same place under its own workspace root, the manifest's paths pointed at the copy; a store that cannot be reached is ours and said so. With no seed anywhere (delivered before this rule, or removed after the retention period) the run ends told: "I can't look at that mesh again: it is no longer kept, so there is nothing to check. Tell me what you want changed there ... and I'll start a new run with it." (`job_service.DISPUTE_MESH_GONE`), and the route says the same before starting one when a worker has marked the mesh purged and no seed is kept. A change (VER-9) needs nothing of the old attempt (the upload, the payload, the face map and the sizes are all durable), so a mesh no longer kept never stops it; the upload gone is said before any run starts ("The file this run was made from is no longer kept... Upload the file again and ask for the change there."); the upload's face list gone, the change goes from the words and the reply says the marks could not be used (`marks_not_used`, VER-12's guard). |
| VER-18 | in force | **A second look rebuilds with everything the run was asked, finer at the marks, and says so** (rulebook 123; `application/change_request.review_payload` / `marks_of` / `_plain_review`, `api/v1/simulation._second_look`, `engines/marked_spots.py`, `cad/part_words.with_picks`, `engines/fea_part_sizes.finer_words`, `agents/intake/change_reply.check`). Dev, 26 Nov (8b79fdb2 -> 31e51296): one mark on a 12 mm hole and no words went to the second look, which passed; the rebuild was a fresh payload of the intake fields alone, so the three typed requirements (3 mm overall, 2 mm on a picked face, finer at the holes) were gone and it meshed at the saved 6 mm; the reply was empty and the result said nothing of the spot. Now: the second look's run is the finished run's own dispatch payload (every saved setting as applied, typed note, picked face and count), with the dispute and each mark's faces (`user_dispute.marks`, tied as VER-10 ties them). After the second look the rebuild makes the mesh finer at every mark on the part, as "finer" on a change is (VER-9, VER-19: half the size the last mesh had there, what is already finer keeps its size; each face re-checked on the run's surface), and the builder still has the look's findings; nothing else changes. The FEA solid and shell routes size the marks; a flow mesh is rebuilt from the findings alone, and said. The result says, per mark: "From your marks: spot 1, on Face 66 · round Ø12 mm - the second look found nothing past a limit there (...); the last mesh had about 1.5 mm there, this one has about 0.75 mm.", and a mark nothing was made finer at says so (off the part, a face no longer on the model). The reply is never empty: written by the intake's model under VER-12's guard (it must say the second look and the rebuild, never a verdict), the code sentence its fallback ("I'll take a second look at the spot you marked on Face 66 · round Ø12 mm. If nothing there is past a limit, I'll rebuild with a finer mesh at that spot and keep everything else you asked for. The last run took 9 min."); the response names each mark's faces and the last run's time; an accept says it rebuilds nothing. |
| VER-19 | in force | **"The size the last mesh had there" is the size the last plan really held there** (rulebook 123; `engines/fea_part_sizes.resolve_scales` / `_changed_area` / `floor_words`, `engines/fea_requirements.whole_sizes` `floor_m`). Dev, 26 Nov: "3mm global cell size" and "make the mesh finer in/around the holes" put the 12 mm holes at about 1.5 mm; "make the mesh finer around this hole only" on the delivered mesh read the hole's last size as 3 mm (the rules' zones held to the overall size only) and built 1.5 mm again, while the note said 3 -> 1.5. Now an area asked finer or coarser on a delivered mesh is sized last, against every other size the plan holds (the parts' and the whole mesh's, the other areas' asks at their own sizes, picked faces' typed sizes, kept areas at the rules' sizes): the finest zone on each marked face, else its part's size, so the new size is half what was really built there (0.75 mm) and the note says the true before and after. A shortest element size the user set (their settings or notes, not a flexible one) is a floor: a target under it is built at it and said ("..., held at your smallest element size of 0.5 mm"); a spot already at or under it keeps its size, said. |

### VER-2 production limits, per engine and purpose

| Limit | Engines | Applies to | Limit value | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Worst face non-orthogonality | snappy, cfMesh, multi-region | every flow | at most 70 degrees | checkMesh warns above 70 |
| Worst face non-orthogonality | VMTK | every flow | at most 70 degrees | VMTK's own quality record, measured as checkMesh measures it (`engines/vmtk/vmtk_runner.check_mesh`); the same bar as the other flow meshers |
| Worst face non-orthogonality | gmsh | external and internal flow (not FEA) | at most 85 degrees | gmsh's own gating bar, so a mesh past it never validates |
| Share of skewed faces | snappy, cfMesh | every flow | at most 0.05% | checkMesh's skewness bars as a share of all faces; a production limit on a sound mesh, never a validity bar (cfMesh often does not report it) |
| Wall covered by prism layers | snappy, cfMesh, multi-region, VMTK | when the approved plan builds layers | at least 80% | lab history: below it wall shear and y+ are unreliable on a material share of the wall |
| Layers built short of the layers asked | cfMesh | when the approved plan builds layers | none short | the layer census (layers in the meshDict vs the request) |
| Connected cell regions | snappy, cfMesh, gmsh, VMTK | every flow | at most 1, or the number of separate passages the plan meshed side by side (VER-13) | checkMesh's region count: a second region is cut off from the flow; separate passages are separate flow volumes by the CAD's own truth |
| Openings in a cavity no inlet opens into, not meshed | every engine | when INS-40 left a passage unmeshed | none | INS-40's told openings, in the words the user was told before meshing |
| Cells across the narrowest passage | snappy, cfMesh, gmsh, VMTK | internal flow | at least the resolution floor (12) | the resolution floor's own reading; narrow side passages it set aside are listed here |
| Stated requirements met | every engine | every flow | no near-miss | the executor's measured requirement checks (`requirement_caveats`) |
| A believable size for what it is | every engine | every flow | no size concern | the unit-plausibility check on the delivered body |
| Follows the CAD it was built from | snappy, cfMesh, gmsh | external and internal flow | no measure missed | `engines/cad_fidelity.py`: the share of the mesh's wall more than one cell off the CAD (2%), the share of the CAD more than two local cells from the mesh (1%), the volume against the CAD's (3% internal, 5% an external body), each opening against its confirmed area (0.9 to 1.1); a measure that could not be read is unmeasured. A mesh past the rebuild bars is refused by the `cad_fidelity` gate (VER-4) |
| The engine's own declared advisory lists | snap-grid | every flow | empty | `engines/snapgrid/criteria.py` (thin layers short, resolution short) |
| Worst scaled Jacobian, over the whole curved element | every FEA adapter (gmsh today) | a mesh built from an FEA plan | at least 0.2 for straight (4-node) tetrahedra; at least 0.01 for curved (10-node) ones | Cubit/Verdict's acceptable range for straight tetrahedra. A curved element's figure reads its curving as well as its shape, and none of the version-1 solvers checks it (they check validity), so a curved mesh is noted only under 0.01 (Areen, 22 Oct) |
| Worst Jacobian ratio across an element's nodes | every FEA adapter | a mesh built from an FEA plan | at most 30 | Ansys MAPDL's corner-node warning |
| Worst aspect ratio (1 is a perfect element) | every FEA adapter | a mesh built from an FEA plan | at most 20 | Ansys MAPDL's aspect-ratio warning |
| Angles between the faces of an element | every FEA adapter | a mesh built from an FEA plan | between 5 and 170 degrees | Hexera's internal gate (design section 9) |
| Mesh zones short of the FEA plan's rules | every FEA adapter | a mesh built from an FEA plan | none | `engines/fea_checks.py`: elements per quarter-turn and through the wall at each hot spot |

The FEA rows are the strictest limit among the version-1 solvers (Abaqus/CalculiX, Nastran, Ansys;
`engines/fea_quality.LIMITS`, read by `engines/verdict_limits.fea_limits`), measured on the solver's
own element (`engines/fea_quality.py`). For an FEA mesh, VER-1 also refuses any inverted element
(a Jacobian not above zero anywhere in the curved element) and any degenerate one: a VALID MESH is the only thing that fails an FEA run (Areen, 21 Oct). The rows above, the quality note floor (SICN 0.1 for straight elements, 0.01 for curved ones) and the elements through each wall are notes on the delivery, never a failure.

The table is `engines/verdict_limits.PRODUCTION_LIMITS`. A limit names engines and flows, never a
file, a part or a model. Changing a number here means changing the table, this book and the
version in the same PR.

### VER-4 measured checks

| Way the mesh is the wrong problem | Measured by | Today |
|---|---|---|
| The wrong side of the body was meshed | `port_areas`, `region_contract` | gates |
| Part of the confirmed geometry is missing | `file_fidelity`, `cad_fidelity` | gate (snap-grid; snappy, cfMesh and gmsh flow meshes: over 8% of the CAD more than two local cells from the mesh, or the volume over 25% off) |
| The wrong scale | the believable-size row of VER-2 | a concern, not a failure |
| The flow setup contradicts what was confirmed | `domain_extent`, `port_areas` | gates |
| A boundary contradicts what was confirmed | `patch_contract`, `boundary_types`, `port_areas` | gates |
| No flow path from inlet to outlet | none yet | no measured check: a review claim is a note, and the shadow log lists it |
| The meshed surface differs from the CAD | `file_fidelity`, `cad_fidelity` | gate (snap-grid; snappy, cfMesh and gmsh flow meshes: over 15% of the wall more than one cell off the CAD with its 95th percentile over two cells). `CAD_FIDELITY_RETRY_ENABLED=false` makes every miss a concern only |
| Disconnected junk regions | `regions_split` (multi-region); the regions row of VER-2 | gate; a concern on one-region engines |
| A stated requirement is absent | the requirement rows of VER-2 | a concern |

The table is `contracts/decision_record.WRONG_PROBLEM_CHECKS`.

## TIME: planned work decides, the clock only guards

A run must come out the same on a quiet machine and on a busy one. So **planned work** decides
what a run does: cells, iterations, triangles, samples, ladder steps and attempts. These are the
same on every machine. A clock is kept only as a **guard**, so a hung step cannot hold a machine
forever. The numbers live in `contracts/work_limits.py`. Every guard's planned work and size goes
into the run's why record as a `work_limit` entry, and every trip as a `guard` entry. Both replay
from their recorded inputs.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| TIME-1 | in force | **Planned work decides, never the clock.** A step runs its planned work to the end. Whether a step or a ladder rung runs, how coarse a pass is, which engine runs next and what the verdict is never depend on how long anything took. |
| TIME-2 | in force | **Each guard is 3 times the planned time.** Planned time is the planned work divided by the step's slow rate (the 10th percentile the lab measured: snappy 1,500, cfMesh 1,200, VMTK 4,000 cells a second). A guard is never shorter than the step's fixed cap before D3, so nothing that finished before is cut now. It is never longer than the ceiling the job around it allows. An engine with no lab rate keeps its old cap. A run watched for progress (RUN-5: snappy, cfMesh, VMTK, multi-region snappy, gmsh, snap-grid) has its machine's wall as its guard, and inside it is stopped only when it makes no progress for the stall window; that stop is a guard trip too. |
| TIME-3 | in force | **A guard that trips is an infrastructure failure.** The attempt ends and nothing on disk is judged. The same attempt is run again once, unchanged - except a trip that says the step would do the same work again (TIME-13). A second trip ends the run as "ran out of time". A trip never picks a lever, skips a step, makes a pass coarser, picks or switches an engine, or changes a verdict. The exceptions are TIME-8 (a trip once the attempt already holds a valid mesh keeps that mesh) and TIME-14 (a trip inside one passage's rescue keeps that passage as built). |
| TIME-4 | in force | **A wait that feeds a decision waits for the answer.** This covers the measured engine order, the intake's recommendation and the geometry check's naming. Their guards are long (15, 5 and 9 minutes). A trip counts as that step failing, the same path as any error in it. It is never a different answer chosen because time ran out. |
| TIME-5 | in force | **The job's own clocks are guards only.** These are the builder's 3,600 s per attempt and 9,000 s in total, the pipeline's 21,600 s, the reviewer's 1,800 s and the Cloud Run task's 4 hours. A trip ends the attempt or the run as ours ("ran out of time"). The builder's and the pipeline's trips are recorded as guard trips; the reviewer's ends the review as one that did not conclude (our failure, never a verdict). (The snappy planner's 1,800 s setting is read by nothing.) |
| TIME-6 | in force | **The CAD repair steps still decide by the clock: inspection (25 s) and repair (120 s).** If inspection runs out, the repair status is "inconclusive". If repair runs out, the original file is meshed. These steps belong to the repair work (#201). They are recorded as `time_limited` with source `guard-clock`, and they are left for that work to bound by face count. |
| TIME-7 | in force | **A repair step on a finished mesh is planned work too.** The cfMesh rescue's optimiser (`improveMeshQuality`) runs a number of loops planned from the mesh's cells: 40 million cell-loops in all, never more than 20 loops and never fewer than 10 (cfMesh's own default). So a 2 M cell mesh gets 20 loops and a 4 M cell mesh 10; the rescue asked 50 before, whatever the mesh. On 8 dev-side meshes (29 Oct), 6 untangled in 2 or 3 loops (50-140 s), and on the 2 tangled ones the bad faces stalled at 3-19 from loop 2-5 while each loop cost more (1.4 M cells: 498 s for 20 loops, 1,263 s for 29). It also stops itself, read from its own count, when it is not untangling: at its 3rd untangling loop its bad faces are still at least a tenth of its 1st loop's, and at least 1,000 (on every dev-side mesh it untangled, loop 3 was under 3% of loop 1; the benchmark's microfluidics spiral read 52,518, 35,250, 52,544, at 6 minutes a loop). What it left is not kept, and the rescue goes on with the best mesh measured so far. Its guard is 3 times that work at its slow rate (5,000 cell-loops a second: a loop's cost follows the tangled region); the protective re-mesh is guarded like a cfMesh run of the same cells; neither guard is ever past what is left of the run's. Before (full benchmark, 29 Oct), the optimiser ran its 50 loops whatever the mesh, under the run's 4-hour guard: on tangled 2.5-4 M cell meshes it ran 2.5 to 3.7 hours and tripped, and 4 files main delivered came back as "ran out of time". The numbers were set on dev-side files only. |
| TIME-8 | in force | **A trip never throws a valid mesh away.** When a guard trips after the attempt already holds a valid mesh, that mesh is kept, with a note, and the attempt goes on to its checks as usual. It is never replayed or ended as "ran out of time". Valid means measured with no fatal defect: in the cfMesh rescue, the mesh as built or a better candidate measured before the step that tripped; in snappy, an earlier pass's sound mesh kept aside; for the passage flow reading and the passage measure, the mesh they read (which is checked without the reading, as before the reading existed). That reading is never taken beside a mesh with a fatal defect, which fails its gate whatever the flow does. With nothing valid in hand, TIME-3 holds. The trip is still recorded (rule TIME-8, with what was kept), so the run's record shows the guard fired. |
| TIME-9 | in force | **The upload's clean check stops at 2 minutes** (rulebook 76, `application/geometry_check.CLEAN_CHECK_LIMIT_S`, `unfinished_verdict`, `CLEAN_CHECK_UNFINISHED`). The clean check (`cad/validity`) runs beside the scout in a child process; it is the one check at upload that decides by the clock, like TIME-6. A check not finished 120 s after it started is stopped, the scout and the upload go on, and the verdict is "not checked" with the words "The check of this file did not finish in 2 minutes, so it is not known whether it is clean. The mesh goes ahead." - shown on the stage as "Not checked" and added to the check's notes. It is never "clean" and never "not clean". A check whose child stops with no answer inside the limit (a kernel crash, a kill) gets the same verdict and words. Neither is run again in the scout's own process, where nothing bounds it; only a check that could not be started beside the scout is judged there. Before, it had no limit of its own: a 9.8 MB IGES of 231 spline faces kept a worker core busy for 13+ minutes stitching 8,415 edges (dev, Nov 2026); read with B-splines kept whole (`cad/iges_read`) the same file checks in seconds. Test: `tests/unit/application/test_clean_check_limit.py`. |
| TIME-10 | in force | **Two readings of a file with no solid stop by the clock before meshing starts** (rulebook 77). Like TIME-9 they decide by the clock; each runs in a process of its own (started only when that work is reached, so a small file pays nothing) and is stopped with everything it started. The mid-surface extraction's own use of the volume maker is not bounded by this. (1) The volume maker (`midsurface._volumes_of`, through `_volumes_bounded`, run only when the sewing leaves a shell that is no valid solid or faces in no body) of the second reading of the file's loose faces in `fea_driver.solids_from_shells` (SHELL-15j, `_solidified`: the faces read as the mid-surface extraction reads them) stops at 300 s (`SOLIDIFY_LIMIT_S`, 3 times the slowest second reading measured that made a solid: 104 s on a public impeller). The plain stitch's reading (MESH-1, always made first, in seconds) then stands, exactly as when the second reading fails, and the run says "Joining your file's faces the slower, finer way did not finish in 5 minutes, so they were joined by plain stitching alone. The mesh goes ahead." Before, it had no limit: its volume maker (`midsurface._volumes_of`) ran 930 s on a public 1,423-face IGES assembly and then failed, while the plain stitch had closed all 1,423 faces into 2 solids in 1.5 s. (2) The closed-skin check (SURF-14, `surface_run.closed_skin_check`) stops its mid-surface extraction at 300 s (`CLOSED_SKIN_LIMIT_S`). The file is then not read as a skin: it goes on to the shell run as the solid its surfaces close round, and the run says "The check of whether your file's surfaces are only a skin, with no wall inside, did not finish in 5 minutes, so they are meshed as the solid they close round. The mesh goes ahead." Before, it waited for the extraction's own 1,800 s limit: 1,968 s on a public 1,220-face IGES cover before any meshing started, and the shell run then extracts again with its own limit. Both lines are in the decision record under TIME-10. Test: `tests/unit/engines/test_solidify_limit.py`. |
| TIME-11 | in force | **The FEA mesh sizing the check runs after the stage is shown always ends, and says how** (rulebook 95, PLAN-fea-22; `application/fea_check.sizing_now`, `application/geometry_check.time_boxed`, `sizing_deadline_s`, `engines/fea_plan_options.bounded`). The sizing's marker on the check carries its own end (`until`: its 1,800 s clock, the 120 s the task keeps for storing it and the time box's 120 s). Every reader of the check (the stage, the chat's answer about the mesh, the retry) reads a marker still saying "sizing" as not sized when that end has passed ("Sizing the mesh did not finish in 30 minutes"), or when its worker is gone (not for a sizing sized away from the naming task, TIME-21): the naming task that runs it ended more than 30 s ago with the marker still there, or its heartbeat has been quiet for 300 s ("The worker sizing the mesh stopped before it finished"). The stage then says that, with ": the run sizes it and takes the recommended mesh, without waiting for you."; the chat's answer says the sizes come when the run starts, never "on the stage in a moment". A sizing its own clock stopped says the same 30 minutes (it said "could not be sized in time"). Nothing is stored by the read; a worker that was only slow still lands its result. So that nothing outlives its clock: the sizing child is started with `--limit-s` (its clock and 60 s) and ends itself then, so a child whose task was killed never keeps sizing; a wait on a child that is interrupted (a task's soft limit) stops the child with it (`sandbox/safe_exec.run_guarded`); the sizing child runs at a lower CPU priority (nice 10), so the worker's other slot and the chat's work on the same machine get the cores first; a naming that gives its slot back while its scout runs is queued again with the same limits it was queued with (the naming's own plus the sizing's - it got the naming's own alone, which stopped its sizing part way); and the joins measure (`cad/fea_joins`, PLAN-fea-18, 600 s) reads its budget before every face pair, not only between pairs of parts - a pair it runs out in is left unmeasured like the pairs never reached (the fuse's answer stands for them). Before (dev, Nov 2026): on a 3-part STEP assembly the stage said "Sizing the mesh..." for more than 27 minutes with no end and nothing said. Test: `tests/unit/application/test_fea_sizing_ends.py`; the timing harness `devtools/time_fea_sizing.py` times every stage of the sizing on any STEP file. |
| TIME-12 | in force | **The FEA sizing counts only what its answer needs, and the answer is the same to the bit** (rulebook 95, PLAN-fea-8, PLAN-fea-22, PLAN-fea-18; `engines/fea_size_field.SizeField.histogram` / `sizes`, `engines/fea_driver._histogram` / `could_fit`, `engines/fea_plan_options.options_and_plans`, `cad/fea_joins._cell_index` / `_far_apart`). The geometry does not change between the sizing's passes, only the zones, so: (1) A COUNT IS NEVER MADE TWICE. A size field keeps what its counts found; asked again alike it answers from that, a finished count answers for any stop it never came near, and a recount with more cubes (PLAN-fea-8's MAX_CUBES_CEILING) goes on from where a count stopped on its cube budget instead of counting again from the first cube - every level before the stop is the same to the bit. A field lives for its pass only (one field holds about as much memory as one of its 8 counting processes: 0.5 GB on a public coil), so passes do not share one. (2) A PASS THAT ONLY ASKS WHETHER A PLAN FITS (PLAN-fea-8's search for a kind's floor, its step finer, the give-back) stops counting once the zones alone pass LIMIT_AIM of its limit: the estimate is never less than that, so the plan cannot fit; a plan that fits never reaches the stop, so its histogram, estimate and plan are the same; a plan that does not fit and is kept (every kind at its minimums) is counted in full once, as before. (3) THE QUICKER MESH IS DECIDED BEFORE IT IS PAID FOR (PLAN-fea-22): it is planned only when the full-rules mesh needs a bigger machine (as before) and the plan with every kind at its validity minimum, counted only as far as it takes to tell, fits the standard machine; when it does not, the quicker mesh would not be offered, so it is not planned, and the stage says the same words. (4) THE JOINS' FACE PAIRS (PLAN-fea-18): the near face pairs come from a nearest-point tree, as before; a pair whose boxes lie further apart than the gap limit and twice the touch tolerance is not measured point by point (no distance between them can be within either), and a face's sample cells are found by one stable sort (they were found by a unique over rows, half the measure on a public hinge), in the same order. (5) THE FIELD IS MEASURED A TILE OF POINTS AT A TIME (a customer assembly with 1,764 hot spots spent 25 s on each of its ~20 counts, every point against every zone group's box): the points are sorted into tiles of about TILE_POINTS, each tile keeps the largest size its points still need, and a group is looked at only in the tiles where the floor its box gives the tile's box is under that; elsewhere no point of the tile passes the test each point always had (every step of the floor is monotone, so it is never more than any point's floor), and inside the tiles kept each point is tested and measured as before - each point is measured against the same groups, so the sizes, the histogram, the plan and its estimate are the same to the bit. The counting processes' reply threads are started once per pool, not once per level. On a public 3-part fixture with 1,800 small holes (tests/fea_fixtures.drilled_plates, the harness on 4 cores) the sizing went 412 s -> 196 s (its counts 341 s -> 163 s), with the same plan, estimate and words. On public fixtures (the harness, a busy laptop): a 6-part coil the size choice offers two meshes for, sizing 1,452 s -> 1,095 s (the full-rules plan 580 s -> about 200 s); a single part whose quicker mesh is not offered, 12.6 s -> 3.1 s; a 7-part hinge's joins 99 s -> 58 s in one process, a 20-part chair's 82 s -> 64 s, the coil's 115 s -> 84 s - with the same plans, estimates, words and joints. (6) WHAT ONE PASS FOUND ABOUT THE GEOMETRY IS NOT FOUND AGAIN IN THE NEXT (a customer assembly, 95k surface triangles, profiled inside its counting processes: rebuilding the field in every child for every pass was a third of their work, the capped corners' edge distances the largest single part of the rest): within one sizing the signed distance to the whole surface is kept per cube (named by its octree level and integer place on the grid its count started from; a cube's centre is made by the same arithmetic in every count, so a kept distance is the float measuring it again gives; at most SIGNED_KEEP_CUBES cubes, past which a cube is measured as before); each face's triangles, the segments two faces share and a capped corner's sampled edges (its segments and spacing alike) are found once and handed to the next pass, and the counting processes are sent the surface once, then only each pass's zones; an assembly part's own size is not measured at a point a finer zone already holds to that size or under (the part's size is never under its own anywhere, so it could change neither the size nor the need there); and a capped corner's edge tree is searched only EDGE_MARGIN_M past where its size could still be under what the point needs (beyond, the size is at least that margin x (growth - 1) over the need, far past rounding, so the min is the same); and a thin spot's balls are measured before its face, the face only where the balls alone leave the size under the need (its size is never under what the balls give). Each gives the same floats: the sizes, the histogram, the plan and its estimate are the same to the bit. Tests: `tests/unit/engines/test_fea_sizing_faster.py`, `tests/unit/engines/test_fea_size_field_tiles.py`, `tests/unit/engines/test_fea_signed_kept.py`. |
| TIME-13 | in force | **A step its own guard stopped is not run again on the same plan** (rulebook 84; `engines/cfmesh/rescue._trip` `same_on_replay`, `contracts/work_limits.after_trip`, `TIME_SAME_ON_REPLAY`, `pipeline/graph._after_guard_trip`). A cfMesh rescue step stopped by its own guard (3 times its planned work, not cut short by what was left of the run's guard) works on the same mesh with the same planned work when the attempt is replayed, so its trip says `same_on_replay` and the run ends at once as ran out of time (TIME-3's one replay is not spent; an earlier sound mesh is still delivered, LAD-cfd-3). abc 99768 (c2e11_p33a): passage 1's optimiser on 26,407 cells (6,347 faces pointing the wrong way) was planned 105.6 s, guarded 316 s, tripped, was run again unchanged and tripped again - about 12 minutes spent on a replay that could not end otherwise. The optimiser's planned rate itself (TIME-7: cells x loops at 5,000 cell-loops/s) is NOT recalibrated here: every finished measured run is the same part (99768 merged mesh, 227k cells, ~18.5k wrong-way faces, 1,006-2,063 s for 20 loops, i.e. 2,200-4,500 cell-loops/s), too narrow to fit a rate on the bad faces. |
| TIME-14 | in force | **A guard trip inside one passage's rescue belongs to that step, not the attempt** (rulebook 85; `engines/cfmesh/rescue.rescue` `step_only`, `engines/cfmesh/native.rescue_passage`, `_note_passage_trip`, `passage_trip_words`, `contracts/work_limits.TIME_PASSAGE_STEP`, `NOT_ATTEMPT_ENDING`). When a step of INS-31's rescue of one passage (before it is merged) is stopped by its guard with nothing valid in hand, the passage is merged as built, the trip is recorded (rule TIME-14, still saying `same_on_replay` when its own guard stopped it, TIME-13) and the attempt goes on: the mesh it finishes is judged like any other (gate, fidelity, verdict), and the run's rescue note tells the user, in words, which passage's rescue did not finish. A trip of the whole mesh's own rescue still ends the attempt (TIME-3 / TIME-13). abc 99768 (c2e13_p34d): passage 1's optimiser tripped at 21:20; the attempt went on, its whole-mesh rescue finished a mesh in 2,115 s (worst cell 85.4 degrees), and the executor threw it away for the earlier passage trip ("an infrastructure stop, nothing judged"); the wrap fallback then left an incomplete polyMesh at 53.3 min. |
| TIME-15 | in force | **The CAD check runs on the mesh as built before a long rescue; a mesh beyond any rescue is not rescued** (rulebook 88; `engines/cfmesh/rescue.rescue` `fidelity`, `planned_rescue_seconds`, `beyond_rescue`, `FIDELITY_FIRST_MIN_S`, `FIDELITY_FIRST_MISSED_SHARE`, `fidelity_first_refusal`; `engines/cfmesh/native.as_built_fidelity`; `agents/builder/tools/meshing` `fidelity_first`; `pipeline/executor.refusal_stands_over_mesh`). Before a cfMesh rescue of an internal-flow mesh (the whole mesh, or one passage before it is merged, INS-31) whose planned work - the protective re-mesh and the optimiser at their slow rates (TIME-7, TIME-19) - is at least a step's least guard (300 s), the CAD check's own measure (`engines/cad_fidelity.measure`) is taken on the mesh as built. When it misses more than half of the CAD surface the flow reaches (LAD-cfd-8's not-the-part bar, past which no refused mesh is ever delivered; the check refuses past 8%), the rescue is not run: a rescue moves points and re-fits layers on the same cells, and would have to bring back over 42% of the CAD surface. A passage is then merged as built; the whole mesh ends the attempt in the CAD check's own words (gate cad_fidelity), standing over the mesh on disk; no retry or other engine changes it (LAD-cfd-8), the wrap rung follows as for any CAD-check refusal (LAD-cfd-13). A check that cannot measure never skips a rescue. abc 99768 (c2e14_p35b): 30 min of rescue (rescue_s 1,821) on a mesh the check then refused at 55.0% not reached. |
| TIME-16 | in force | **The passage measure beside a mesh is stopped at its guard, whatever call it is in** (rulebook 94; `engines/passage.passage_of_polymesh`, `_bounded`, `_measure_polymesh`, `MEASURE_CHILD_RULE`). The measure runs in a forked child process that the run stops when its guard (TIME-2, `measure_guard`) runs out; the trip keeps the mesh, checked without the measure (TIME-8), as before. The SIGALRM guard could not stop a VTK call (Python sees the signal only when the call returns) nor a measure in a worker thread, where it never arms: abc 63401's 11.4 M-cell merged mesh sat 2 hours on one core in the measure under a 3,600 s guard. Without fork (not Linux) the measure runs in the process under the old guard. Each sub-step's seconds are logged (boundary read, interior point, decimation, chords, cells across), and a measure stopped at its guard logs the Python frames it was in (faulthandler on SIGUSR1 before the kill), so the slow step is named. The quadric decimation that gives the chords a surface of about 60,000 points (`coarse_copy`, `DECIMATE_BOUND_S` 300 s) runs in its own bounded child; past 300 s the chords use vertex clustering (`_clustered_arrays`, vtkQuadricClustering, bins of about sqrt(area / 60,000)) instead. abc 63401's merged 717k-point wall sat over 58 minutes inside vtkQuadricDecimation (the stack the trip logged); a 750k-point voxel surface decimates in 13 s. Clustering is linear in the triangles, but merges walls closer than a bin: a passage narrower than a bin is read wider at the chords (the cells-across figure is still taken at every real boundary point). Accuracy: none lost when the measure finishes inside its guard; past it there is no passage measure, as before. |
| TIME-17 | in force | **The staged VMTK wall's triangles follow its area, never the CAD's tessellation** (`engines/vmtk/rims._halve_long_edges`). The wall is cut so no edge is longer than its bound by halving edges round after round, which keeps each triangle's shape. A needle - two edges past the bound, the third within it, and thin (longest edge squared over twice its area above 20) - is taken out of the halving the round it appears: its two long edges get exactly the points the halving puts on them (2^k equal pieces, shared with whatever is on the other side, so the wall stays conforming and every rim keeps its points), and the two chains are zipped from the apex into a ladder, each quad cut along its shorter diagonal. A needle of 2^k pieces a side is 2^(k+1) - 1 triangles; halved, it was 3^k. Why: OCP tessellates a long straight bore as slivers the whole length. abc 00168427 (a 4 m pipe, 56 mm bore) came as 284 triangles and left the halving as 2,868,474 at an 11.2 mm bound (an 11 mm wall needs about 14,000), short edges down to microns; the radius field took 712 of the 868 s of staging on the laptop, 99 minutes on the board, and with 00160152 and 00184527 the run met the 250-minute wall with no mesh. Laddered: 159,908 (vmtk's own remesh makes about 100,000 of it at its target edge). |
| TIME-18 | in force | **VMTK's staging is planned work, and a wall that cannot fit the run moves it on** (`engines/vmtk/staging_work.py`, `rims.TooManyTriangles`). Each triangle the staging hands VMTK costs it, at its slow rate, 6.5 ms of surface work - the radius field the staging reads on it and vmtk's own remesh and centerlines, which run on the staged wall before any cell is filled (board, 22 Nov: 2.3 ms a triangle on abc 00046345, 1.8 on 00178394, 6.4 on the stabilizing rod). The staging and that work get one hour of a run (a quarter of the 4-hour mesh job, so the next engine has the rest to deliver), so the staged wall is at most 553,846 triangles. The edge bound counts what each round will make before it makes it and stops at that count; the bounded wall is priced before the radius field reads it. Past it, the staging stops before the work starts, as the staging's own refusal (deterministic, before meshing), in plain words ("VMTK would have to prepare at least N wall triangles from the M of your surface, about H hours of work, past the 1 hour a run gives it..."), and the run moves on to the next engine at once (NOT_BUILT, SW). The planned work is recorded with every staging (`vmtk_staging.json` `staging_work`). Why: abc 00168427, 00160152, 00184527 and 00046344 staged 3.0-3.6 M triangles, spent 81 to 134 minutes staging, and met the 250-minute wall with no mesh; 00088953 spent 100 minutes staging 574,866. |
| TIME-19 | in force | **The cfMesh rescue runs its cheaper lever first and never optimises a mesh the best so far already beats** (`engines/cfmesh/rescue.remesh_first`, `RESCUE_ORDER_RULE`). The order is decided from planned work at the slow rates, never the clock: the protective re-mesh is one cfMesh run of the cells, the optimiser its planned loops over them, so the re-mesh runs first, and a re-mesh that comes out off the edge is kept with no optimiser run at all. The optimiser then takes the candidates still near the edge, best first, and skips one the best so far beats (recorded as `skipped`, rule TIME-19). abc 99768 (c2e8_p30a): the optimiser ran 2,729 s into its guard on a re-mesh of 171 degrees with negative volumes, beside an optimised mesh of 87 degrees with no fatal defect; the rescue took 4,574 s. INS-30 holds: every candidate still keeps every opening the mesh as built held. |
| TIME-20 | in force | **cartesianMesh is stopped when its untangling is not converging, read from its own counts** (`engines/cfmesh/native.untangle_not_converging`, `UntangleWatch`, `UNTANGLE_RULE`). cfMesh untangles in passes of at most 50 iterations and prints its bad faces at each. Once two passes of the run have ended with bad faces left, a pass still at 1,000 bad faces or more that, at the geometric rate of its last 10 iterations, would need more iterations than it has left is stopped; the builder records ENGINE_CRASHED with untangle_not_converging and the run moves to the next engine, as VMTK's layers do. Never the clock: the same log stops at the same iteration on any machine. static_mixer_004 (c2_res5t): 4,531 s of 5,514 s in 108 passes, the last rising to 64,438 bad faces and ending at 2,107; the mesh was refused anyway. |
| TIME-21 | in force | **A big FEA sizing runs on a machine of its own, only when the part needs it** (rulebook 130, PLAN-fea-22, TIME-11, TIME-12, RUN-4, RUN-10; `application/big_sizing.predicted_worker_s` / `send_away` / `watch` / `at_run_start`, `application/geometry_check.size_fea_plan` / `store_plan_fact`, `contracts/fea_sizing_remote`, `adapters/mesh_execution/fea_sizing_vm`, `engines/fea_plan_options.pack` / `run_packed`, `runtime/mesh_runner.run_sizing`). Before the stage's sizing starts, its time on the 4-vCPU geometry worker is predicted from what the check measured: 0.56 s x (hot spots + surface triangles / 2,000); a measurement kept from before the triangles were counted is predicted from its hot spots. Over 240 s, the sizing is sent to a VM through the big-machine route: the check's canonical file and its unit packed, the speed machine (c2d-standard-32) asked for first, then its fallback (n2-highmem-16), never the Cloud Run job; the VM runs the mesh image's own sizing (`engine` fea_sizing) under the check's clock less 300 s for its boot, image pull and hand-back. THERE ITS COUNTING PROCESSES FOLLOW THE MACHINE (`engines/fea_size_field.machine_workers`, passed to the sizing child as `--workers-cap`): its CPU quota (as the snappy ranks and the cfMesh threads read it), at most as many as 90% of its memory holds after 4.5 GB for the sizing itself (4 GB measured after the measure on drilled_plates(30), plus the kept signed distance at its 20 M-cube cap, 0.32 GB) at 2.5 GB each (2 GB, the largest counting process measured - the 1.7 M-triangle grooved shaft - and a quarter more; the lab's parts took about 0.5-0.6 GB each), and at most 32: 32 on c2d-standard-32 (128 GB would hold 44), 16 on n2-highmem-16, never fewer than the worker's own. The worker keeps its cap of 8 (`MAX_WORKERS`). The answer is the same to the bit whatever the count (TIME-12; drilled_plates(20) on 4 and on 8, drilled_plates(4) on 4 and on 16). What more processes buy is bounded by the sizing's serial share - the measure, the assembly's joins and the parent's own work in each count: drilled_plates(20) on the lab laptop took 235 s on 4 processes (2 physical cores) and 211 s on 8 (4 cores; the stage table: measure 58 / 60 s, assembly 33 / 33 s, counting 116 / 89 s), so about 60 s of its counting is serial and 16 cores would bring it to about 165 s; 12 processes did not fit the laptop's 9 GB. The naming task then ends and its queue slot is free; a watch on the same queue looks every 20 s (a moment of a slot each) and stores what lands exactly as the worker stores it - the same size choice on the check and the same plans beside it (the run reuses them) - but only when the VM sized it with this code (`planning_code`), so the answer is the worker's to the bit (TIME-12: the same whatever the process count). The stage keeps "Sizing the mesh... You can go on"; while it is away (`where`: vm, then after_vm) only the marker's clock ends it (TIME-11). Nothing gets worse when no VM can be had: no route here, no quota or none free on either machine, a start never confirmed (its VM deleted by name), the check not recordable, the watch not queueable - the worker sizes it as before; a VM that never reports (15 minutes), stops answering (10 minutes), stops, sends back nothing usable, runs other code or whose look breaks is released and the watch sizes it on the worker with a whole new clock. No VM is left: it is released (deleted with its exchange objects) when its result is collected or ran out of time, when it fails, when the check moved on (a new naming or unit, the session gone), when its clock has passed, and when the run starts first (the run sizes the mesh itself, as when it starts before the worker's sizing ends; the size choice says "The run started before the sizing ended"); its own one-run end, its lifetime limit and the VM sweep (RUN-4) are the backstops. Every stored size choice says where it was sized and why (`sized_on`: where, machine, predicted worker seconds, the line, hot spots, triangles, seconds), and the run cites it in its why record (stage fea_sizing). The fit (the lab laptop held to 4 cores, this code; the site's worker is slower still - the customer assembly took 282 s on 8 laptop cores and 1,012 s on the worker - so it is taken as about twice the laptop's 4 cores): sizing seconds measured on the laptop, then predicted on the worker - ONE SIZE OPTION (one count): bracket, plate with a hole, v-notched bar, stepped shaft 2-3 s / 1 s; transition 4 s / 10 s; touching blocks 5 s / 0 s; coil spring 13 s / 13 s; drilled_plates(4) 13 s / 21 s; 3 plates with 50 holes 23 s / 33 s; drilled_plates(10) 27 s / 131 s; folding chair (20 bodies) 37 s / 36 s; hinge (7 bodies) 45 s / 58 s; the knuckle on the site about 20 s. TWO OPTIONS (9-19 counts): 3 plates with 450 holes 211 s / 295 s; drilled_plates(20) 213 s / 524 s; Helmholtz coil (775k triangles) 367 s / 306 s; grooved_shaft(40,12,30) 586 s / 348 s; the customer assembly 1,012 s on the site / 1,014 s. Every part lands on its side of the line: the one-option parts predict at most 131 s (about 90 s at most on the worker), the two-option ones at least 295 s (about 420 s at least on the worker); the prediction is a sorter more than a stopwatch - it is low by up to about 3 times on the longest fixtures, which only means they go to the VM anyway. The line sits in the gap between them: a VM costs about 90 s to boot, as much again to pull its image and hand back, and sizes about three times faster (its whole cores and more counting processes against 4 vCPUs), so it pays from about 4 minutes - and every part it sends frees a worker slot for that long. |
| TIME-22 | in force | **A stage stopped by its clock keeps what it had finished, and says so** (rulebook 132; `engines/gmsh/gmsh_runner._stage_guarded`, `_kept_past_clock`, `STAGE_CLOCK_RULE`, `STAGE_NOTE_FILE`, `stage_clock_words`; cited by `engines/fea_driver.drive`). The `full` stage writes the staged solid and gmsh's B-rep in about a second and then draws the report's picture under STAGE_EXTRAS_TIMEOUT_S (300 s). When that clock stops it after the process said the staged solid (and the B-rep) were written, those files are kept, the half-drawn picture is removed (the OCC tessellator draws it), and the stage writes one plain line the run cites: the preview was drawn the quicker way, the geometry was read in full and the mesh goes ahead. Before, both files were deleted and the part staged again without the B-rep (a public 0.16 m blade, one B-spline face: 300 s, then no B-rep). Stopped before the staged solid was written, the part is staged again as before. Test: `tests/unit/engines/test_between_routes.py::test_a_stage_stopped_by_its_clock_keeps_what_it_had_finished`. |
| TIME-23 | in force | **An optional extra's clock never costs the result** (rulebook 135; `contracts/work_limits.TIME_OPTIONAL_STEP`, `NOT_ATTEMPT_ENDING`; recorded by `engines/shell_run._wait`, `_watched`, `_kept_start`). Work made beside the result whose stop leaves the result as it would be without it - a shell's thick regions kept as solid (MIXED-12) - is stopped at its own limit; the result is delivered and the user is told in one plain sentence that the extra was not done this time. Its trip is recorded (rule TIME-23, `by`: the rule that set the stop - MIXED-12, MIXED-16, MIXED-25 or MIXED-26) and never ends the attempt: the run is neither failed nor run again. Before: the kept-solid stop was recorded as an attempt-ending trip, so the executor threw the delivered all-shell mesh away, ran the same attempt again unchanged, and ended the run as "ran out of time" (the tank with a cone roof, cen_s0195, on the website: 1,803 s, nothing delivered; main delivers it). |

### What each clock became

| Where | Before D3 | Now |
|---|---|---|
| snappy pass (`engines/snappy/drivers.py`) | a fixed 2,400 s cap; a pass that timed out made every later pass, and the next attempt, coarser | watched on its machine (RUN-5): its guard is the machine's wall, its planned cells recorded beside it; a stall or the wall is a trip, which stops the passes and the attempt is replayed (TIME-3). A pass that came out broken still makes the next pass coarser: that is measured, not a clock. A trip after an earlier pass of the attempt kept a sound mesh aside ends the passes on that mesh (TIME-8). |
| native run caps (`engines/dispatch.py`) | fixed seconds, a stopped run noted as a decision | the same caps as each engine's guard floor; a stopped run is a guard trip |
| cfMesh run (`engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner.py`) | 1.25 x the estimate at 2,000 cells/s | a guard of 3 x the planned cells at 1,200 cells/s, counting the near-edge rescue (3 runs' worth), within 1,200 to 3,000 s. Whether a plan fits one run is still decided from the plan (`fits_one_run`), before it runs. |
| cfMesh rescue (`engines/cfmesh/rescue.py`) | skipped steps "with no time left", stopped the optimiser at its own share of the clock, dropped an unmeasured candidate | both steps always run when triggered and their cell limits allow. The optimiser's loops are planned from the cells (TIME-7), and each step has its own guard from its planned work, within the run's. A trip stops the rescue: the best valid mesh measured so far is kept (TIME-8), and with none the attempt is replayed (TIME-3). |
| VMTK ladder and surface (`engines/vmtk/vmtk_runner.py`) | steps not started under 60 s or 10% left; a layered step got 60% of the clock, then the layer-free fill; folded-wall remeshes skipped near the end; the OpenFOAM export skipped under 60 s | every ladder step and every remesh runs when the step before it measurably failed; each gets what is left of the run's guard; a trip stops the run. The export always runs, with at least its own 600 s guard. |
| passage measure (240 s), passage flow (180 s), sealed spaces (120 s; no clock since INS-27) | abandoned: the mesh shipped, or the floor was judged, without the reading | each reads a fixed number of samples or voxels; the clock is a guard; a trip is a guard trip. The passage measure's guard is planned from the mesh's boundary faces (`engines/passage.measure_guard`: 120 faces a second, the 10th percentile of the benchmark's 306 measures, 3 times over, between 240 s and 3,600 s): 114 of those 306 measures took longer than the old fixed 240 s (up to 1,622 s). The benchmark could not see it, because there the measure ran in a worker thread, where its clock never arms; on the mesh job it runs in the main thread, and on dev-areen (2 Nov) the FDA pump housing and the gyroid phantom tripped at 240 s twice each and their valid meshes ended as "ran out of time". The passage flow reading is taken only beside a mesh with no fatal defect. A trip of either keeps the mesh beside it, checked without the reading (TIME-8). |
| engine fallback (`pipeline/engine_fallback.py`) | "too little time left" kept the run on its engine (SW-13) | removed: the time left never decides a switch |
| engine order wait (60 s), intake recommendation (15 s) | the declared order, or no recommendation that turn | waits for the measurement (TIME-4, guards 900 s and 300 s) |
| review rerun (`pipeline/graph.py`) | not started again with too little time left | started again by its count alone (TIME-1); the run's deadline is a guard (TIME-5) |
| geometry check naming and vision | naming skipped | a guard (TIME-4): the same path as a model outage; the user confirms the proposal |
| builder run_mesh with too little loop budget left | refused, with a hint to reduce the work | a guard trip of the attempt (TIME-5), replayed as it was |
| a run that timed out still repairing broken cells | treated as a broken mesh, so the run moved to the next engine | a guard trip; what it was doing at the time decides nothing |
| CAD inspection (25 s), CAD repair (120 s) | as now | unchanged (TIME-6), left for the repair work |

### The interface for any driver (FEA step 4 included)

```python
from meshpipeline.contracts.work_limits import PlannedWork, guard_for
from meshpipeline.engines.determinism import note_guard_trip

work = guard_for("snappy", cells=planned_cells)       # or PlannedWork(where="fea.gmsh",
                                                      #   unit="elements", amount=n, rate=r,
                                                      #   floor_s=..., ceiling_s=...)
run(..., timeout=work.guard_seconds)                  # the only clock
if timed_out:
    note_guard_trip(work.where, workspace=ws, **work.as_dict())   # the executor ends the
                                                                  # attempt; the graph replays it
```

## PLAN-cfd: the flow mesh's sizing, on every flow engine

The sizing rules every flow engine (snappyHexMesh, cfMesh, VMTK, gmsh) follows for a CFD mesh,
from the part's own measured passages - never from a file or a part name
(`engines/resolution_policy.py`, `engines/gap_sizing.py`).

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| PLAN-cfd-1 | in force | **Cells from the measured narrowest gap, locally.** Every passage the flow goes through gets at least 13 cells across its own width, refined only where it is narrow: snappyHexMesh and cfMesh refine boxes and distance bands round the narrow spots of the staged wall (`engines/gap_sizing`, the cost checked against the cell limit before meshing); VMTK sizes every edge from the local radius (2 / `edge_length_factor` across); gmsh sizes from the local radius field. The plan is written beside the case (`gap_sizing.json`) and cited in the why record. |
| PLAN-cfd-2 | in force | **Every opening, at least 8 cells across its own width.** A port narrower than 8 wall cells gets its own finer cells at the port only (snappyHexMesh: the port's refinement level; cfMesh: a local refinement on the port's surface). Under about 4 the octree seals a port over and it comes back with no faces. |
| PLAN-cfd-3 | in force | **The big-machine hook.** When the cells the sizing rule needs are over the run's cell limit (the standard machine's, or the user's own count), the plan records `route_big` (and the why record cites it), the run is meshed as the limit holds, and the delivered note says so in plain numbers. NOTHING IS ASKED (Areen, 14 Nov: "the person is specifying how many cells they want - why does the system ask 8 M or 21 M?"): the "Keep 8.0 M, or try full detail?" question and its buttons are gone. The bigger machine is the user's to ask for, by a count in their words (RUN-9); the note says how many cells to ask for. |
| PLAN-cfd-4 | in force | **A size the user asks for on a part becomes that part's own level (snappyHexMesh)** (`engines/snappy/part_levels.py`).
