On a surface of several named parts, the notes are tied to the parts by NOTE-4 (the reading the user checked before approval first, NOTE-5). A size becomes a level against the background cell the dict really uses: the coarsest level whose cell (base / 2^level) lies inside the user's range, else round(log2(base / size)) at the range's middle; "finer" is one level over the surface's own, "coarser" one under. A part that is its own region in the staged surface gets its level there (finer or coarser). A part merged into one wall with others is refined near its own surface (a distance region on its own file, 3 cells deep) and cannot be made coarser: that is said, not applied. What the notes asked and what each part got is said once per run (in cell sizes, not levels) and kept in the why record. Unclear words, one part, or no answer: nothing applied - never a failed run. |
| PLAN-cfd-6 | in force | **The far field is wide enough for the body: at most 3% blockage** (`engines/far_field.grow_for_blockage`, `projected_areas`, `blockage_words`; `engines/snappy/snappy_runner.domain_from_strategy`, `engines/snappy/drivers._say_blockage`, `engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner._configure_external_3d`). The margins asked or proposed (5 / 10 / 5 body lengths up, down, to the side) are the minimum. The body's frontal area along the flow (half the summed absolute projection of its staged wall, the measure the CFD board uses) must be at most BLOCKAGE_MAX (3%) of the box's section across the flow (COST Action 732, Franke et al. 2007; the usual wind-tunnel correction limit): when it is not, every open cross-flow face moves out by the same distance until it is (aimed at 95% of the limit). A floor under a grounded body and a symmetry plane never move; the streamwise margins are untouched. Wide, flat and rotating bodies thus get their box from their frontal size, not only their length (the board's external sweep, 22 Nov: 13 of 120 at 3.05-12.6% with the box always exactly 5 / 10 / 5: 5 propellers or rotors, a wind turbine, 4 road vehicles, a rocket, a bridge, a bluff body). Said in the run log ("the far field was widened by 3.4 body lengths on each open side so the body blocks 2.9% of its cross-section (it was 9.8%; at most 3%)") and kept beside the case (`far_field_blockage.json`). The extent gate reads more room to the side than asked as no defect (the typed path already did; the prose path's lateral check is one-sided). cfMesh widens a free body's box the same way (a box with a floor or a symmetry plane is left as planned there). |
| PLAN-cfd-7 | in force | **The near wake and the flow beside the body are resolved for every body** (`engines/wake_region.plan_wake`, `level_for_cells`, `plan_near_body`; `engines/snappy/snappy_runner.render_snappy_case`). On the wake length (the reference length, the length along the flow, or a disc's smaller cross size, whichever is largest): the finest wake box keeps at least NEAR_WAKE_CELLS (25) cells per wake length - finer than one level under the wall where the wall's own cells are coarse for the body, never finer than the wall - and reaches NEAR_WAKE_REACH (1.5) wake lengths behind the body. A budget cut takes the far wake first (shorter down to that reach, then the outer boxes, then the near wake one level coarser down to its floor) and never past the floor; what the protected near wake costs over the wake's share is added to the budget, within one job's cell limit. Before, the cuts halved every box's reach first, so the near wake ended short of one body length (the board's external sweep: 29 of 120 under 25 cells per body length 1 L behind, 4.8-24). A box round the body's own box, NEAR_BODY_PAD (0.1) wake lengths past it, holds NEAR_BODY_CELLS (50) cells per wake length beside the body (never finer than the wall; the board: 29 of 120 under 50 there), its cells added to the budget the same way. A wake level the plan or the user asked coarser keeps the old cuts; a cell count of the user's is never raised. Recorded in the wake record (`near_wake`, `raised_cells`, `near_body`). |
| PLAN-cfd-8 | in force | **A cfMesh internal plan over its budget is made coarser only where that buys cells, its narrow-gap boxes are held as they are priced, and a plan still over runs where it fits** (`engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner.coarser_wall`, `machine_for_plan`; `engines/passage.narrow_passage_regions(whole_box=)`). A level coarser at the wall is taken only when it cuts the estimate by a quarter or more (`MIN_LEVEL_CUT`) and leaves at least two cells across the typical passage (`USABLE_WALL_CELLS_ACROSS`); a plan line never says "about 0 cells". When the gap plan cannot fit even its fewest cells across, the fallback boxes are held to half the job's cells (the run's budget, or the standard limit when none is set) and costed at their whole volume, as cfMesh refines a box and as the plan prices it. An internal plan still over the standard machine once nothing more can be made coarser says what it comes to and what carries it, and runs on the VM whose memory holds it (RUN-9's route). Why: abc 00063401 priced 210 M cells, 209.3 M of them in 114 narrow-gap boxes about 250 times their fluid, held by no budget (none was set) and costed at their fluid; eight wall levels (0.85 mm to 218 mm) cut it to 209 M, left 0.06 cells across the typical passage, and the run was sent to a 64 GiB VM for files alone. Tests: tests/unit/engines/test_cfmesh_plan_coarsening.py. |

## LAD-cfd: the resolution ladder, and what a sound mesh becomes

A mesh is SOUND when every blocking gate passed and the trial solve converged: no fatal checkMesh
defect, no negative volume, every port with faces, the right region, the domain asked for. The
resolution floor (12 cells across the narrowest 5% of the wall), the share of skewed faces and the
one-job cell limit are our own bars, not validity: on a sound mesh they are notes, declared as
non-blocking gates (`GateSpec.blocking=False`). `engines/resolution_policy.py` holds the rules.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| LAD-cfd-1 | in force | **The ladder's next rung, never an identical re-run.** A sound mesh short of the resolution floor sends the next attempt up one rung on the same engine: the cells-across target at the narrow spots is multiplied by 1.5 (rung 1), then 2 (rung 2), or by what the measured shortfall needs ((floor + 1) / measured) when that is more, never past 3. snappyHexMesh also refines where the mesh measured short (its narrow retry); cfMesh detects and sizes its narrow-spot boxes that much finer; VMTK divides its edge factor by it, and its budget fit may coarsen the edge back only to the rung's own cells across (a rung that cannot fit one job is not started, and the sound mesh stands); gmsh sizes its passage field by it. The rung is written into the attempt's workspace (`resolution_ladder.json`), so the case the mesher gets is never the one it already ran (the rerun guard hashes it). No rung when there is no room left to refine: the mesh already has 90% of the cell limit, or the attempt's gap plan was already held by the limit (`limited`, `over_limit`, `accepted_fewer`) with its budget the whole one-job limit - the next plan has the same cells to share and shares them the same way (lab: 7 of 7 such rungs rebuilt the same mesh). A held plan under a smaller budget keeps its rung: the next plan may raise the budget. |
| LAD-cfd-2 | in force | **A switch does not spend the ladder.** When the run moved to another engine and that engine's first mesh is sound but short of the floor, it still gets one rung - inside the run's ceiling of 3 attempts. The time left never decides it (TIME-1, since D3): the run's deadlines are guards only (TIME-5), and a rung that runs past them is a guard trip (TIME-3); the earlier sound mesh is still delivered (LAD-cfd-3). |
| LAD-cfd-3 | in force | **Ladder spent, mesh sound: delivered with a note.** The note names the narrowest gap, where it is, its cells across and the floor ("the gap at (x, y, z) has 8 cells across; 12 is our floor"). The share of skewed faces is a production limit (VER-2), not a failure. And a later attempt that comes out broken never throws an earlier sound mesh away: the run delivers its best sound mesh instead of the failure (`node_deliver_sound`). The best is the one with the most cells across its narrowest gap (one that meets the floor beats any that does not), the newest on a tie; and when the last attempt is sound but a rung left it coarser than an earlier sound mesh, the earlier one is delivered too. The same holds between snappyHexMesh's own passes: a sound pass is kept aside while the next pass tries for a cleaner one, and put back if that pass comes out broken. |
| LAD-cfd-5 | in force | **The last rung: a wrapped surface, only when every rung before it failed** (`pipeline/cfd_wrap.py`, `cad/surface_wrap.py`; STAR-CCM+'s surface wrapper, Fluent Meshing's wrap). When a flow run (external or internal) is about to end with NO MESH - every regular attempt and every switch of the fallback ladder spent, a failure no retry of these engines changes, or the run's time spent - and no earlier attempt is sound (LAD-cfd-3 comes first), the surface the engines staged is wrapped into a clean skin and meshed again, as any attempt is: same boundaries, same plan rules, layers where they hold, the same gates. **The rung has its own engines, in order**: the engine the user named when it is snappyHexMesh or cfMesh, then those two in the run's ranked order (ENG-5), then gmsh's tetrahedra filled against the skin's own triangles with no remesh and no layers - each only when it admits the approved request. A step that fails on the wrapped surface hands the same skin to the next engine (a user-named engine too: the skin is clean, so the engine is all that is left to change), and the delivery says which engine failed and which took over; a mesh from gmsh's step is labelled DEGRADED (tetrahedra, no wall layers). **The rung has its own time**: each step starts with at least `CFD_WRAP_TIME_S` (2 hours) ahead of it, whatever the earlier attempts spent, so a run that timed out still reaches it. Never for a failure that is not the geometry's (our service, the domain asked for, a boundary type or name, a region split, the review), a file the engines refuse by design, or a dispute rebuild; a failure of ours ends the rung. The wrap is for the mesh only: the customer's file is never changed, and INS-7's stop at the stage stands. A run that delivers a mesh today never reaches this rung. |

The flow's side is every point farther than r from the surface that the flow reaches - from the grid's border round a body; through a part, from the point inside the flow, and when that does not reach every opening, every pocket of free space the staged boundary encloses (its generalised winding number at the pocket's deepest point at least one half), so shells drawn through each other merge and the staged boundary's own topology never decides - and the wrapped fluid is that space grown back by r: gaps narrower than 2r are closed, and faces that cross, doubled faces and the inside of overlapping shells, which the flow never reaches, are left out. r is the surface's diagonal over 500 (about half a standard wall cell); through a part it is never more than 0.15 of the narrowest way's half-width from the point inside the flow to every opening (measured on the grid), so a real passage keeps at least 70% of its width, and the flow must reach every opening in ONE connected fluid, else r is cut to 0.6 of itself, down to half a grid cube; still not, the whole is drawn once more on a GRADED grid of up to 128,000,000 cubes, so holes and vessels the openings need are resolved. A staged boundary left open (the flood gets out to the open air) is wrapped again at two, four and eight times the distance, never past the diagonal over 60. The fluid may be several separate passages (cfMesh meshes them side by side), each holding two openings at least. Holes finer than the graded grid are below any mesh's resolution and are closed by definition: they are listed with their size as bridged spots. When no try keeps every opening, the try that lost the fewest openings (then the least opening area) is delivered with a concern, never refused: only the passages still holding an opening are kept, and each stranded opening (cut off, or alone in a dead end) is placed on the wrap's wall nearest where it is drawn, with its drawn area, the delivery saying how far it moved. The rung stops only when the wrap finds no fluid at all. A grid the cap holds coarser raises r with it, to 2.5 cubes - or, where a passage asks for less (the narrowest way, or a flood that no longer reaches an opening), to one cube, so small vessels stay open on a coarse grid. Round a body the flow must stay outside it: when the space a wrap four times as wide (never past the diagonal over 60) seals off from the open air, and the flow at r still reaches, is more than a quarter of the body at that wider wrap, the flow went inside through a gap, and r doubles (at most three times); still inside, the rung stops rather than mesh the body hollow. A sheet the flow wets on both sides seals nothing off, so it is never taken for a hollow body: it is kept as a slab about 1.5 cubes thick. The skin is the zero level of the distance to the wrapped fluid's edge, which equals minus the exact distance to the surface wherever the surface was sound, so it lies on the CAD to well under a cube there. It is closed and manifold (every edge on two faces, run opposite ways), its pieces enclosing fewer than 8 cubes dropped, every face turned out of the fluid (out of the body, round one), and coarsened (topology-preserving decimation) to about the triangles of edge 0.7 r its area holds, 20,000 to 600,000, only when the coarsened skin is still closed. Each face is named after the nearest face of the original, so every boundary keeps its name; a wrap that loses an opening stops the rung. |
| LAD-cfd-7 | in force | **The delivery says how far the wrap lies from the CAD.** Measured at every corner of the wrap against the original's exact surface: the largest and the 95th-percentile distance where the wrap lies on the CAD (within 1.5 cubes), and over all of it; every BRIDGED spot (a connected stretch farther than 1.5 cubes: a gap it closed, a sharp re-entrant corner it rounded) with its place, area, widest distance and the patch it was named after, the widest first (20 listed, the rest counted); the wetted CAD's distance from the wrap and the CAD faces the flow cannot reach. ## INS: the fluid's inside (where the fluid is, how it gets in and out)

One map per part drives every step below, so the steps cannot disagree (`cad/inside_space.py`).
The stage's openings, the stage's question, the staging's seals and seed, and the checks before
meshing all read it. Nothing here reads a file name, and no model decides any of it.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| INS-1 | in force | **The inside-space map.** The part's surface is drawn on a grid of at most 6,000,000 cubes (400 a triangle, never fewer than 1,500,000; a cube never finer than 1/5,000 of the part's diagonal). Every loop of the surface that could edge an opening - an open rim, or the outline of a smooth patch no face lies across - gets a cap, and the free cubes are flooded from the grid's border. A **pocket** is space the open air reached with no cap but reaches now only through caps; space no flood reaches even without caps is a closed shell's inside (the metal, or a body that is the fluid). A cap with open air on one side and a pocket on the other is a **mouth**. A cap with a pocket on both sides (a step in a bore, a barb's ridge) is taken away and the space flooded again, until none is left. A cap whose pocket runs less than 1/10 of the cap's width behind it (under 9 in 10 of its points) closes a dent in the outside and is taken away too. Of two mouth caps of one pocket that share most cubes, the tighter loop is the mouth; of two facing one way on one axis, one just behind the other (within its width, at about its size, or within two cubes at any size), the one in front is. Pockets lined on the same side by one piece of the surface cut along the mouths (at least 1/4 of each one's lining) are one pocket. A pocket holding at least 5% of the largest one's space is a **passage**; a smaller one is a side pocket. Surfaces of more than 2,000,000 triangles are not mapped (the shape rules stand). |
| INS-2 | in force | **Openings are the mouths.** Every mouth of a passage is an opening, of any shape or size; a mouth of a side pocket (a bolt hole, a blind dimple) is shown as not an opening, for the user to click. A loop too small for the grid (under 4 cubes across) is read by the hole finder's shape rules (`cad/open_ends`), so no small opening is dropped - unless the map reads the space ahead of it as the part's own inside (a port from a tube into the chamber round it). The STL upload, the stage's click and the staging read this one definition (`open_ends.find_holes`). A CAD part's face-read openings whose outside is the part's own inside (a step in the bore) are proposed as not openings, and a CAD part whose faces give fewer than two gets the map's mouths. When the map's openings differ from the shape rules' and only the shape rules' close the fluid in, the shape rules' are proposed, with a note. |
| INS-3 | in force | **The flow kind by rule.** The user says the fluid flows through the part: when the map finds a passage with two mouths or more, or two openings are confirmed (a body that is the fluid, with its lids), it is internal flow. When it finds neither, the stage asks one plain question - "This part has no openings or inside for fluid. Did you mean flow around it?" (or, for an inside with one mouth, that the fluid has no way through it) - with flowing around it the one-click way out. Never a job that can only fail. A part the map could not read is never asked (the intake asks). |
| INS-4 | in force | **The point inside the flow is the deepest point of the passage**: the cube farthest from every wall and cap (the first in grid order on a tie) of the passage most confirmed openings open, taken when the closed boundary's winding number there is one, at least 4 of 6 rays cross it an odd number of times, and it stands at least 2% of the smallest opening's radius from every face. For a body that is the fluid, the deepest point inside it. Otherwise the point is stepped in from an opening, as before. Never a model's guess, never a point in a wall. |
| INS-5 | in force | **Caps fit each mouth's own loop; nothing leaks.** Every mouth no confirmed opening claims is sealed into the wall before the fluid's side is read (a mouth just behind a confirmed opening, inside its footprint and no wider, is that opening's own throat and stays open). When the loop-by-loop vote cannot settle which side is the fluid's, the pieces that line the passage are the fluid's. When the map's own seals leave the boundary unclosable, the same openings are staged once more as the shape rules alone read the surface, kept only if that closes, and recorded. A cap is the lid of the loop's own points (flat, or pulled toward the smallest area when the loop is not flat); a ring-shaped mouth (a centre body in it) is stitched round its centre, or, when a loop does not turn once round the centre, joined to the outer loop by one bridge and clipped whole. Then the sealed check: every edge of the fluid boundary on exactly two faces, and a flood of the boundary from the point inside the flow stays inside it. A leak is located and named. A wide one - no thickening of the wall by up to four cubes closes it, so any mesher would fill the outside too - on a boundary that is open or crossed by its own lids stops the staging before meshing; a narrower gap (a mesher's own cells close it), or any leak of a closed, uncrossed boundary (a grid artefact at a gap finer than its cubes), is recorded only. The file is never patched: a gap that is not an opening, and that the user did not ask to close for the mesh (INS-8), is named as a CAD defect ("this needs CAD repair") for the CAD repair stage. A passage the confirmed openings do not close is refused with the place of the opening left open. |
| INS-6 | in force | **Every confirmed opening is its own patch.** Each one comes out of the staging as a patch with faces on the closed fluid boundary, or the staging stops before meshing and names it. |
| INS-7 | in force | **A broken surface is said, never healed.** The check counts the drawn surface's open edges that edge no opening (an open pipe end is an opening; a tear is not) and its loose pieces (each holding at least 1/200 of the skin); a CAD solid's B-rep is closed, so its skin's unwelded seams are not counted. When the user says the fluid flows through a part with no way through, and its surface is open or in loose pieces, the stage does not ask about flow around it: it says the part needs CAD repair first, and what it found. Nothing here changes the file - no gap is closed in it, no piece joined, no face turned, no wrap: that is the CAD repair stage's work. Leaks narrow enough to close for the mesh alone are INS-8's proposal, not this stop. |
| INS-8 | in force | **Leaks up to a size the user confirms are closed for the mesh, never in the file** (Fluent's "close leaks up to X"; `cad/inside_space.read_leaks`, `cad/internal_surface._close_leaks`). A triangle surface whose map as drawn finds no way through it, and that is open (an edge on one face, or a second piece), is read again with its leaks closed: the wall is thickened by r cubes (r = 1, 2, 3, 4 in turn; a gap up to 2r cubes across closes) only within r + 1 cubes of an edge not on two faces or of where two pieces of the surface meet - never anywhere else, so a narrow channel of a closed surface is never taken for a leak. The first r that finds a passage with two mouths - or, for a body that is the fluid, the inside of its closed shell, when the map as drawn read no pocket at all and the inside regained holds at least 5% of the part's box - is the reading, when every leak it closed is narrower than that inside (one as wide as the passage is a missing piece, not a leak); else the map as drawn stands. A **leak** is a piece of the closed cubes (outside the caps left standing) between a passage and space that is not it - the open air, the part's material, another pocket; its width is the widest way through it, twice the clearance from the drawn surface that every step of that way keeps, and its place is where that way is narrowest. The openings are that reading's mouths, and every hole too small for the grid to see (under 4 cubes across) is a leak of that reading, listed with its own size, not an opening: the inside was found only once gaps of that scale were closed (a small real port is the user's to click). The stage lists every leak (the widest first, up to 20, with width and place) and proposes one switch, on by default: close every leak up to the widest found plus one cube, at two figures, rounded up; the user can change the size or switch it off (then the surface is meshed as drawn, and a leak it cannot mesh across stops the staging, named). The confirmed size goes with the run to every route's staging (`leak_closing.json` beside the run's surface): the map is read with every gap up to that size closed (r = size / two cubes, at most 6), a leak at a loop the staging seals anyway (a pinhole's rim) or at a confirmed opening is the seal's or the opening's, and only when the flood from the point inside the flow still gets out does each other leak get a **cover** in the wall - the faces of its closed cubes the passage touches, its rim laid on the drawn surface and its inside smoothed - before the flood is tried again. The why record names every leak, its width, place and what closed it, and says the uploaded file is unchanged. |
| INS-9 | in force | **The ground follows the up axis.** A body on the ground stands on the far-field box face under the up axis the user confirmed on the stage (`engines/ground_plane.floor_of`): z-min on a +z-up file, y-min on +y up, y-max on -y up, and so on; nothing confirmed is +z up. The floor's place, the contact under the body (measured with the up axis as height), the wake's floor, the extent gate's seated face and the intake's check all read that one face. A flow along the ground is never refused; only a flow along the up axis (into the ground) is, and the chat asks one plain question - which way along the ground the flow goes, or which way is up (the stage's Up control, with its picture). |

Read one by one, each region's interface face hides the part's far end (nothing is clear ahead of the true end, and the body behind it stops at the interface), so no flat end read as a mouth, and INS-7 called the three closed regions "3 loose pieces" for CAD repair. Otherwise the parts' own reading stands, unchanged. Touching solids are never loose pieces (INS-7). The file is never changed. The reading of an external body's sealed spaces (the voxel copy that keeps a hollow shell's inside out of the mesh) had a 120 s clock of its own besides its planned work. An external marine body (abc_ext 3962, 546 triangles) read in 1.1 s on a quiet lab machine and ran past the 120 s on a loaded one (251 s): the trip ended the attempt as an infrastructure failure and threw away the production-grade mesh (1.93 M cells) it then made. Now the reading has no clock: its work is planned up front - at most 8 M voxels (the voxel coarsened to fit, as before), at most 150 M samples on the triangles (a surface that needs more is read at a coarser voxel instead of not at all), at most 128 spaces tested for being sealed and 32 M voxels measured for depth (largest first; smaller ones are left unnamed) - so its result never depends on the machine's speed, and the attempt's own guards still stand (TIME-5). Measured: 3962 meshes end to end; the sealed-spaces unit tests unchanged. |
| INS-29 | in force | **Every opening of a separate passage lands in the mesh, or the attempt says so at once** (rulebook 76; `engines/cfmesh/native.mesh_passages`, `ports_without_faces`, `finer_mesh_dict`, `PASSAGE_REFINES`, `PASSAGE_REFINE_CELLS`). cartesianMesh keeps one closed region; a further passage meshed at the case's cells splits at a neck under a cell across, and the region holding its opening was dropped (abc 63401 passage 2: 3 regions, outlet_3 no faces; abc 99768: 9 regions in 4,350 cells), which the manifest gate refused only after the rescue and the exports, and the same plan then ran again. Now each further passage's openings are read on its own mesh; one with no faces meshes that passage again at half every cell size (its own resolution, up to 3 times, while 8x its cells stay under 2 M); still none, or the first passage's own opening with none, fails the attempt there, before the rescue, saying the same plan drops it again; the builder ends its turn on it and the refusal (PATCH_NOT_CAPTURED, deterministic, ladder exhausted; `agents/builder/tools/meshing._run_and_judge`) is one no further attempt repeats. A merged mesh that lacks a passage's opening fails the attempt the same way (abc 99768: merged, outlet_3 no patch); a cfMesh owner file with no cell note is counted from its faces. |
| INS-30 | in force | **A rescued mesh keeps every opening the mesh as built held** (rulebook 77; `engines/cfmesh/rescue.rescue`, `faced_patches`, `RESCUE_PORTS_RULE`). The protective re-mesh reads the case's own surface, which on a case of several passages is the first passage only: abc 99768's merged passage 2 (outlet_3) passed INS-29's merged-mesh check, then the rescue kept a 33 s re-mesh with no outlet_3 (after a 2,063 s optimiser run). Now a re-mesh of such a case has the further passages meshed and merged into it again (`engines/cfmesh/native.mesh_passages`) before it is measured, and a re-mesh whose passages do not merge, or any candidate that lost a patch the mesh as built had faces on, is no candidate (recorded as `lost_openings` / `passages`); the mesh as built, or the best candidate that kept them all, is delivered. |
| INS-31 | in force | **Each further passage is checked, and rescued at its validity edge, before it is merged** (`engines/cfmesh/native.rescue_passage`, `PASSAGE_RESCUE_RULE`). A case of several passages meshes each further passage in a case of its own; that sub-mesh is now measured with checkMesh and, when it has a fatal defect or is past the near-edge angle, given the cfMesh rescue (TIME-19 order, every opening kept, INS-30) in its own case before mergeMeshes takes it. abc 99768 (c2e8_p30a): the merged mesh read 171 degrees with negative volumes where passage 1 alone re-meshed at 73.5 degrees - passage 2 carried the bad cells, and the merged mesh's rescue could not clear them. A passage mesh that cannot be measured is merged as built, as before. |
| INS-32 | in force | **The narrow spots an opening reaches its passage only through are sized first** (`cad/inside_space.opening_necks`, `engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner.neck_plan`, `NECK_CELLS_ACROSS`). When gap sizing cannot afford every narrow spot within the cell limit (OVER_LIMIT), the spots that an opening - or a piece of its passage at least 5% the size of the largest - joins the rest of the passage only through are found on each passage's surface: the inside is flooded on a grid, each cube keeps its clearance from the wall and a narrow spot's box its measured half width, and the widest path from the opening to the largest piece clear by 3 wall cells is read again with each needed spot meshed across. Those spots are boxed at their own width / 3, padded by two of their cells, before the other spots get what is left of the limit (held to half of it, as before). If even those do not fit, the plan stops before meshing as a dropped opening (PATCH_NOT_CAPTURED with port_region_dropped, so no wrap rung: LAD-cfd-12), naming the opening, the gap width and the way out: widen or close the gap in the CAD, allow more cells, or drop that opening. The user's result says that stop in its own words and those three ways out (`engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner.neck_stop_refusal`, `NECK_WAYS_OUT`; `contracts/failure_cause.describe` reads its `plan_stop`) - before, it read "A boundary you declared came out of the mesher with no faces ... run it again" from a plan that never started a mesher. abc 63401: the half-budget fallback gave its 0.25 mm slit 0.425 mm cells, cfMesh kept the larger region and two outlets came back with no faces. |
| INS-33 | in force | **A needed narrow spot is boxed round its own gap, and coarsened to 2 across before the plan stops** (rulebook 82; `cad/inside_space._gap_extent`, `NECK_EXTENT_SLACK`; `engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner._neck_boxes`, `NECK_CELLS_ACROSS_MIN`, `NECK_COARSEN_STEP`, `NECK_TIGHT_RULE`). INS-32 boxed each needed spot as gap sizing grouped it - the whole box, at width / 3 - and priced it whole: abc 63401's outlet_3 slit (0.1 mm, 8 boxes at y 156-169 mm) came to 15.9 M cells against the 8 M limit and the plan stopped. Now the spot's box is held tight round the gap itself: of its cubes, those on a way through the narrow cubes from one side to the other at most 2 cubes longer than the shortest, and no clearer than the gap's own half width (a drawn-shut gap: its own cubes; a wider one: one cube more) - the slit, not the near-wall cubes of the chambers either side nor their approach - inside the spot's box, padded by two of its cells. A gap whose way does not read near its box keeps the box. When the needed spots still do not fit, they are coarsened 0.25 cells across at a time from 3 towards 2 across, never fewer than 2 across the gap; only then does the plan stop, saying it needs that many cells even at 2 across. Synthetic chamber + 0.3 mm slit in a 10 x 6 x 10 mm grouped box: 600 k cells whole, under 200 k tight. abc 63401 (plan only, plain mesh not counted): the grouped boxes there are already small (80-234 grid cubes), the tight box did not read, and coarsening to 2.25 across (0.044 mm cells) took the needed spots from 15.9 M to 6.93 M, under the 8 M limit. |
| INS-34 | in force | **The walk from an opening to its passage reads every narrow spot it needs, not a fixed number** (rulebook 83; `cad/inside_space.opening_necks`, `NECK_RULE_WALK`). INS-32 read at most 8 narrow spots in a row on the way from one opening to the rest of its passage and then stopped as if the opening were joined. abc 63401's outlet_3 needs nine: eight 0.1 mm slits and a 1.6 mm gap at y 152 mm; the ninth was left unboxed at 1.9 cells across (0.85 mm cells), cfMesh cut it and dropped outlet_3's piece (passage 2, 6.9 M cells, run c2e11_p33a). The walk now goes on until the opening joins at the needed size or the narrowest step is no measured spot; each step names a spot not named before (a named spot is meshed across), so it ends within one step per measured spot. 63401 plan only: 10 needed spots (the ninth for outlet_3 read tight round its gap), 6.93 M cells at 2.25 across, the same as before within 0.1%. |
| INS-35 | in force | **A passage that lost an opening still brings the openings it kept into the mesh, and the refusal names what was never meshed** (rulebook 83; `engines/cfmesh/native.mesh_passages`, `_not_meshed`, `PASSAGE_KEPT_RULE`). A further passage whose opening is still missing after its finer re-meshes is merged into the case's mesh when it holds any other opening with faces, so the mesh the gate judges holds every passage built and only the opening really lost reads zero faces. abc 63401 (c2e11_p33a): passage 2's own mesh had outlet_2 at 6,392 faces and outlet_3 at 0, was never merged, and the gate read outlet_2 AND outlet_3 at zero. The PORT_REGION_DROPPED line now also says which openings are in the mesh with their faces, and names the passages the run stopped before meshing (a lost opening on passage 1 stops the run before the further passages). Whether a mesh that keeps some openings is delivered is not changed here (owner decision). |
| INS-36 | in force | **A mesh built that lost an opening ends in the plan-time stop's own words, never a manifest error** (rulebook 86; `engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner.after_build_refusal`, `neck_words`, `NECK_SPOTS_FILE`, `neck_stop_refusal(after_build=True)`, `pipeline/executor.refusal_stands_over_mesh`, `contracts/failure_cause._describe`). The plan writes its needed narrow spots (INS-32: width, where, the openings that hang on each) beside the case. When a passage's mesh is built and an opening has no faces (INS-29 / INS-35), the builder's refusal is the same sentence INS-32's plan-time stop says (one function, `neck_words`): the opening, the narrowest gap on its way (width and where), what WAS built (the openings in the mesh with faces) and the same three ways out; the user reads "The mesh was not delivered: ..." in place of "The plan stopped before meshing: ...". That refusal stands over the mesh on disk (it is not judged again into "zero faces"); no wrap rung follows (LAD-cfd-12) and no attempt repeats it. Partial delivery is NOT changed (owner decision, still open). abc 63401 (c2e13_p34c): 8.19 M cells built, outlet_3 at zero faces, ended "[MANIFEST_VALIDATION_FAILED] mesh_manifest.json: these patches have zero faces: ['outlet_3']" with no gap named and no way out. No plan-time rule predicts this loss yet: 63401's needed spots fit the 8 M limit (6.93 M planned) and cfMesh honoured the 0.05 mm boxes (level 14), yet still dropped outlet_3; one part is too few to set a "will not open" ratio. |
| INS-37 | in force | **A single-passage cfMesh mesh that lost an opening is told in INS-36's words** (rulebook 89; `engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner.lost_opening_words`, `lost_opening_facts`, `LOST_OPENING_WAYS_OUT`, `engines/cfmesh/flow_gates._lost_openings_said`). The manifest check's refusal of a cfMesh mesh whose OPENINGS (never a wall) came out with no faces says what INS-36 says for a case of several passages, from the same functions: the narrowest gap the plan measured on their way (`neck_words`, width and where) when there is one; else the openings and that the mesher kept no cells where they open - no gap is named - and what IS in the mesh, with the ways out (allow more cells, check that opening on the geometry stage, or drop it). The refusal's cause and facts are the zero-face check's own, so the next attempt still refines the missed opening (LAD-cfd-8) and the wrap rung follows as before; the old text stays in the builder's feedback. INS-36's own fallback (a passages file but no measured spot) uses the same no-gap words. |
| INS-38 | in force | **Two narrow spots the walk reads as one chain are meshed as one chain** (rulebook 90; `cad/inside_space.opening_necks` `walk_min` / `walk_max`, `engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner._neck_bridges`, `NECK_RULE_WALKED`). INS-32's walk reads a measured spot as the whole grid cubes its box touches (cubes of a quarter of the needed size), so two spots of one gap whose boxes stop short of each other inside a cube - a spot ends at its last measured surface point, about a point's spacing short of the next one - read as joined, while the mesh boxes left a stretch of plain cells across the gap between them. Each needed spot now carries the cubes it stands for (a tight box, INS-33: its gap's own cubes); for every two needed spots whose cubes touch and whose mesh boxes do not meet, a box across the hole between them (each side's overlap where they overlap, the hole where they do not, padded two cells into both) at the finer of the two cells. Its cells count in INS-32's cost like any needed spot, so the plan-time stop still speaks when they do not fit. abc 63401 (outlet_3 behind eight 0.1 mm slit spots): boxes 0.5-0.7 mm apart along y at y 164.8-165.5 mm (one cube is 0.64 mm); the walk joined outlet_3 at the needed size, so the walk's own "no measured spot" stop never fired there. Plan replay: one bridge, 7.06 M cells at 2.25 across (6.93 M before). Rounding every box out to whole cubes instead cost 8.71 M at 2 across and stopped the plan (run c2e16_p37a). Cloud c2e16_p37c (same job line as c2e14_p35a): the plan sized 11 spots (one bridge) and did not stop; passage 2 meshed at 10,165,529 cells with no opening lost (it was refused 8.3 min in for outlet_3 at zero faces), merged 11,377,307 cells (over the 8 M limit the plan priced: the plan under-reads this mesh); the run had not finished 50 min in (no log line for 35 min after the merge), so no product verdict yet. |
| INS-39 | in force | **A case meshed as several passages says which cell region holds which opening, and a passage cut in two is refused** (rulebook 94; `engines/cfmesh/native.mesh_passages`, `opening_regions`, `regions_judged`, `PASSAGE_REGIONS_FILE`). mergeMeshes joins nothing: it puts the passages' meshes side by side. After the last passage is merged the merged mesh's connected cell regions are counted (owner/neighbour) with the region each opening's faces lie in, and written to `passage_regions.json`. A passage whose openings lie in more than one region is cut in two inside the mesh: the run fails in words naming the pieces and their openings, with INS-29's way out. Otherwise the run log says the passages lie side by side, not joined, and which region holds which openings; a region holding no opening is named. abc 63401: passage 1 (inlet, outlet_1) and passage 2 (outlet_2, outlet_3) are two solids of a 7-solid assembly, 100 mm apart (passage 1 to y = 50 mm, passage 2 from y = 150.5 mm), so the two regions are the CAD's own; no join can be made. A mesh that cannot be read (binary) is not judged. |
| INS-40 | in force | **A separate passage no inlet opens into is not meshed, and is told before meshing** (rulebook 95; `cad/fluid_passages.plan_passages`, `no_inlet_words`, `openings_not_meshed`; Areen's decision, 24 Nov). When the confirmed openings lie on several separate passages and at least one of them carries a declared inlet and another opening, every passage with no declared inlet is taken off the staged surface (cfMesh `_stage_passages`, snappy `_one_passage`): no flow can enter it, so its cells only cost time (abc 63401: about 10 M cells and 11 minutes for a 34 cm3 tube of an assembly that held two outlets and nothing else). The user is told, in the plan's notes, which openings sit in a cavity no inlet opens into, its size, that it was not meshed, and the way out: mark one of them as an inlet, or remove them, then run again. The told openings are recorded beside the case (`passages_read.json`, `not_meshed`); the gates hold the mesh to the openings it was built for (the executor's gate context, the manifest's patch roles and the launch check `engines/case_contract.check` leave the told ones out, and the CAD check `engines/cad_fidelity.cad_reference` holds the mesh to the passage meshed, `_passages/p0` or cfMesh's `geom.stl`, not the whole staged boundary), and the verdict tells them (VER-13). When no passage has both an inlet and an outlet the plan is unchanged: the existing words and refusals decide. The engine's passage count before a run (`passage_count`, no declaration) is unchanged. |

## FLOW: through the inside or around the outside

One authority decides whether a flow part is internal (the fluid flows through its inside) or external
(around its outside): `application/flow_kind.decide`. The check stores its verdict on the facts as
`flow_kind` - `kind` (internal, external or undecided), `flow` (the kind, or the best guess the stage
draws while undecided), `best_guess`, `confidence`, `by` (file, words, geometry, pictures or guess),
`rule`, `reasons` (plain words), `question` and the `input_kind` that goes with it - and the proposal,
the stage and the intake read that one value. The measuring step's own reading is kept as `flow_scout`.
Nothing here reads a file name.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| FLOW-1 | in force | **The user's words win.** Words that state where the fluid goes ("flows through it", "internal flow", "pressure drop", "the fluid volume"; "around it", "external aero", "drag", "wind tunnel") decide, whatever the shape says, at 0.95; the shape's disagreement is kept as a reason. With no statement, the object the words name decides at 0.8 (a pipe, duct, manifold, valve, nozzle, artery: through it; a car, wing, aircraft, hull, building, propeller: around it). Words that state both, or take a statement back ("not internal"), decide nothing. The answer to FLOW-4's question is read the same way. |
| FLOW-2 | in force | **The measured shape decides only where it can tell.** From the measuring step's faces and the inside-space map (INS-1), each situation with the share it gets right on the labelled development corpus: the parts close off a space with openings where the measuring step reads flow through them (0.85, internal); several closed solids whose flat ends read as mouths (0.65, internal); open ends that lead into a way through it the map finds (0.9, internal); a closed solid shaped like a bent or branched passage, its flat ends the mouths (0.8, internal); a straight closed solid whose widest flat end is at least 0.8 of its cross-section (0.7, internal); many short holes through a thin plate, a perforated plate or a grille (INS-23; 0.7, internal, the stage asks); blades round a hub (0.75), a closed solid with no passage mouths (0.8; 0.7 when it has flat ends that do not read as mouths), a scene of many pieces (0.7), surfaces with no openings and no inside (0.65): external. Two flat ends are "straight" when they face apart and their centres lie off each other's axis by under a quarter of the wider one. **Undecided** (under 0.6): a surface with nothing inside it (it encloses under 0.5% of its box, with open edges, or a flat patch); an open skin with open ends; faces that read a body where the map finds a way through; ring ends with no way through; a straight convex bar; a straight solid that bulges past its two flat ends. |
| FLOW-3 | in force | **The pictures decide only where clearly surer.** The picture model's kind replaces the measured one only when it is surer by 0.15 (and at least 0.6), and never when it contradicts what was measured: flow through a part needs two openings and, for a wall, a way through the map finds; flow around never fits a part the map shows a way through. The user's words are never overruled by the pictures. |
| FLOW-4 | in force | **Undecided is said, never guessed silently.** A part no rule above decides carries `kind` "undecided", its best guess as `flow` (what the stage draws), and the one fixed question "Is the flow through the inside or around the outside?" for the intake to ask once. It is asked only once the naming has landed (the verdict then carries the user's first words and the pictures), beside the open stage, with two buttons, and never when `by` is "words" (`agents/intake/flow.flow_kind_question_due`, asked by the stage's read of the check). The user's answer decides by FLOW-1 and sets the stage's "The fluid flows" and "The file is" before Proceed; so does a flow the user states in the chat at any point before Proceed - the latest statement wins over the stored verdict (`agents/intake/flow.stated_flow_kind`). |
| FLOW-5 | in force | **A file that declares its regions decides itself.** A thermal model (ECXML) names its parts and the air among them: internal, at 0.95, whatever else is said. |
| FLOW-6 | in force | **First what the part is** (`application/part_understanding.understand`; the proposal's and the stored facts' `understanding`: object in plain words, class, mode, confidence, by, reasons, measured). The user's words name it first (0.9: "exhaust manifold" is a collecting manifold, "intake manifold" a distributing one, "mixing tee" mixes, "dividing tee" splits); the measured openings give the class from general geometry - two equal ends on one line (straight), with a waist (venturi), unequal on one line (reducer; a diffuser where the whole-shape reading names its narrow end), at an angle (bend), side by side facing one way (return bend), facing apart off one line (offset duct), a passage that opens into a chamber behind its ends (chamber: a valve body, a housing, a plenum), an eye on the part's axis with an outlet off to the side and tangential to it (volute), a run with a branch square to it (tee) or at an angle (wye), a stem with two equal arms (wye), a run or one larger opening with smaller branches (manifold), two or more separate passages (circuits), else a branching part - and say so when they disagree with the words, which win. The picture model's name is used only where the words name nothing (agreeing raises the sureness; it names the part alone only when the measured class is under 0.6). |
| FLOW-7 | in force | **Then which opening is which** (`part_understanding.assign`; each opening's `role_reason`: confidence, reason, guess). Sure only where engineering says: a pump or fan draws in at its eye (0.75), a diffuser is fed at its narrow end (0.75), a collecting manifold takes in at its runners and a distributing one at its larger opening (0.85, from the words), a mixing wye joins its arms into its stem and a dividing one splits from it (0.8, from the words). Everywhere else - two ends alike, a reducer, a tee that may split or mix, a manifold no words name - the measuring step's order stands and is marked a guess (0.4 to 0.55), which the stage shows as "a guess - check it" for the user to confirm or flip. The pictures may change a role only where they are surer than this; a role they set stays marked a guess. An opening the file declares is never reassigned. |
| FLOW-8 | in force | **A sticker means an opening.** The pictures carry stickers only on proposed openings, numbered with the stage's own ids; a face proposed as not an opening (a side pocket's mouth, a step in the bore) wears none. An undecided part (FLOW-4) is pictured with its openings stickered, so the pictures can help decide. |
| FLOW-9 | in force | **A body the map finds a way through is asked as flow through it, with the way's mouths** (`application/flow_kind._decide`, `THROUGH_CASE`; `application/geometry_check.through_part_openings`, `_is_mouth`; the holes' `passage_mouths`). A part whose faces read as a body in a flow while its inside-space map (INS-1) finds a passage with two mouths or more (FLOW-2's "faces that read a body where the map finds a way through") stays undecided and is still asked FLOW-4's one question with its two buttons, but its best guess - what the stage draws until the user answers - is flow through it (before: the faces' "flow around it"), its input kind a wall round a passage. Its openings are the mouths of those passages, each the flat face's hole of its bore measured exactly (INS-13), else the mouth as the map reads it on the drawn skin: a face-read opening that is a mouth (a hole, not a flat end's disc of metal; its centre within half the wider one's width of the mouth's, facing the same way within a dot of 0.9, its size within 1.25x) stays as read; a flat end's disc of metal (an end plug's 54 mm end faces round its 16 mm bore) and the mouth of a blind side pocket of the map (a bolt hole) are kept as not openings for the stage's click; any other hole the faces read stays proposed after the mouths (the map may not see it: a branch it reads with one mouth); openings that already are the mouths are left as they are. A body with no way through (blind holes only, one mouth) and every part another FLOW-2 case decides are read as before. The answer decides by FLOW-1 either way. Measured: built parts - a 56 mm plug with a 16 mm bore (asked, flow through it, the bore's two 16 mm holes; the end discs not openings), a block with a bore turning a corner (asked, flow through it, its two 12 mm mouths unchanged), a block with two blind holes and a block with one deep pocket (flow around it, decided, as before). The check step on the lab's 222 parts (STEP and triangle files, flow and not-flow; 824637a0 against this rule): 38 proposals changed, all by this rule, none otherwise; on 32 only the drawn guess changed (flow around it to flow through it, still asked), on 6 the openings too - two end plugs (their 54-60 mm end discs set aside for the 16 mm bore's two holes, and six small bores), a honeycomb plate and a cell end plate (end discs set aside for the mouths the map finds), an end cap (11 blind bolt holes set aside), an elbow of three solids (its two 47.6 mm ends added beside two 5 mm holes) and a chip plate (11 more mouths). On the eye-labelled parts: of 13 flow parts proposed as flow around it before, 11 are now guessed flow through it (the 2 left: a cooling plate the map finds no way through, a mis-scaled aorta); of 25 not-flow parts proposed as flow around it, 5 are now guessed flow through it (a sieve end cap, a lid, a chip holder, a stabiliser plate, a discharge ring): all are asked, the answer decides. The first pass of this rule also set aside every face-read hole the map did not read as a through mouth (a manifold's two side ports, a plate's 9.6 / 7.2 / 4.6 / 3.4 mm holes); hence only discs and blind holes are set aside. |

## PLAN-fea: the FEA meshing plan

A structural solid (purpose Structural / FEA, 3D, no face named by the user) is meshed by the
deterministic FEA driver (`engines/fea_*.py`), not the builder loop. The plan is computed from the part's
measurements (FEA build step 3: walls, hot spots, bodies) and is the same for every engine; each
engine is one adapter that meshes it (gmsh first, `engines/gmsh/fea_adapter.py`). The plan is
written to `fea_plan.json` beside the mesh, with these sentences in plain words. No model is asked
anything: AI changes to the plan are off (Areen's decision 4, 20 Oct).

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| PLAN-fea-1 | in force | **Quadratic tetrahedra, solid only.** Version 1 meshes the solid with 10-node tetrahedra; thin walls are meshed as solid too (shells are version 1.1). |
| PLAN-fea-2 | retired | **The detail level set the element count** (Quick / Standard / Fine). Retired 21 Oct: one rules-based plan, sized by PLAN-fea-14 and held under the element limit by PLAN-fea-8. |
| PLAN-fea-3 | in force | **At least 2 elements through every wall.** Every face's measured wall gets elements of at most half its thickness, held through the wall. A wall is measured by rays from the face along its inward normal to where they leave the material: a face of another body, a surface closer than 1/100,000 of the part (or twice the CAD face's own tolerance), a face met at a graze, and a face meeting the ray's own at an acute edge (a taper, whose thickness runs to nothing at that edge) are not the far side of a wall, and a face LYING ON the ray's own (26 Oct: two faces of one body that share no edge and are read under that floor somewhere lie on each other - a self-overlapping solid, a zero-thickness membrane on a wall - so every reading of that pair closer than the two faces' own tessellation error, each triangulation's measured deflection added, is the same coincidence and is read through; job bf863215's tube read 0.0055 mm "walls" that way on its 1.2 mm wall, 138M elements) are not the far side of a wall; a face's wall is the thinnest reading at least 3 of its rays agree on within 25%, and a face its rays do not agree on has no wall of its own (`cad/fea_features.wall_reach`). **A wall thin only in places is refined only there** (22 Oct): when a face's thinnest agreed reading is under half its median reading, the readings under half the median are thin spots, grouped where they lie within 2 sample spacings of each other. The rest of the face keeps the wall its other readings agree on. Each spot gets its own zone: the wall rule held off the face, inside a ball round its readings plus one sample spacing (`cad/fea_features.thin_spots`; a sliver between a counterbore and a bore no longer sets the whole bore's size). **A taper is read on the CAD's own edges too** (27 Oct): two faces sharing a CAD edge whose outward normals there turn by more than 92 degrees are a taper whatever their triangles (`cad/fea_measure._brep_tapers`); a whole assembly's coarser tessellation left a cryostat cylinder and the shallow cone it runs into unwelded, read as a 0.0068 mm "wall" - 1.3 billion elements. |
| PLAN-fea-4 | in force | **Hot spots: 6 elements per quarter-turn** of every hole, fillet and notch or groove (quadratic tetrahedra: their curved sides follow the arc), and 3 through the wall beside it, held out to half a radius off the feature, then growing at 1.3. Was 10 per quarter-turn held out to one radius, which made small assemblies 1.7 to 2.8M elements; the textbook Kt cases stay within tolerance at 6 (lab, 24 Oct). **A bore is not a hole** (27 Oct): a concave cylinder sharing its axis with a wider convex cylinder of the same part, all the way round, over at least half the bore's length, and at least 5 times as long as the wall between them, is the inside of a tube, sleeve, ring or hollow shaft - its main wall, sized by PLAN-fea-3 - and gets no hot spot; it is named (`cad/fea_features.is_bore`). A tube's 533 mm bore sized as a hole (3 elements through its 1.2 mm wall, its whole length) was 2.6M of job bf863215's 3.9M elements. **A channel along a long body is not a hole either** (27 Oct): a concave cylinder that runs at least 90% of its body's length along its own axis, in a body at least 5 times as long along that axis as it is across it, is part of the section of a bar, an extrusion or a drilled shaft - the load it carries along its length runs past it, not round it - so the wall rule sizes it and it is named (`cad/fea_features.is_channel`). A hole across such a body, or a short one along it, stays a hole. A 10 mm slotted extrusion's 2.5 mm centre channel, 1.5 m long, sized as a hole was 87% of a 57M plan (now 23M: its 0.25 mm walls and capped corners). |
| PLAN-fea-5 | in force | **Sharp inside corners are capped** at a quarter of the wall beside them, and refined no further: their stress never settles under refinement, so more elements there buy nothing. A corner whose faces read no wall of their own is capped against the thinnest wall of its own part in an assembly (PLAN-fea-17), never another part's; a single part's, or a part with no wall read, against the thinnest wall measured. |
| PLAN-fea-6 | in force | **Gentle growth:** element sizes grow by at most 1.3 from one element to the next away from every refined zone. |
| PLAN-fea-7 | in force | **Every curved face keeps its shape:** at least 4 elements per quarter-turn (about 16 round a full circle; quadratic elements) of every round face that is not a hot spot - a cylinder or torus by its CAD radius, and any other curved face (a swept wire, a spring's coil, a free-form blend) by its own tightest bend, read from its principal curvatures sampled over the face (`cad/fea_measure.face_radius`). A bend gentler than a quarter of the part's diagonal is left to the base size. A round bar or wire so gets at least 3 elements across its diameter (a coil spring's wire came out a 4- to 6-sided ribbon, 22 Oct). |
| PLAN-fea-8 | in force | **Under the element limit, never a stop before meshing.** The element estimate is checked before meshing against the limit: 8,000,000 elements, or fewer when the mesher could not build that many inside its time guard (PLAN-fea-13). Over it, the finest zones are made coarser - a floor size doubled from the finest until the estimate fits - but never past what keeps elements valid: at least 1 element through every wall and 3 per quarter-turn of every round. What that leaves short of the rules is named (`resolution_short`) as a note on the delivery. When even those minimums are over the limit, the part is meshed at them anyway and told so (Areen, 21 Oct); the search stops as soon as a coarser floor changes no zone. An estimate the size field stopped counting short is never zero: the volume it had not resolved is counted at the size its zones ask for (fea0221 read 0 elements, and its time guard fell to the floor). **A count is finished before it decides** (27 Oct): a count of the zones that ran out of cubes while still under the limit is counted again with four times the cubes, up to 12M, and counted to 1.5 times the limit; a count still short there is a floor, and under the limit it fits (a plan short of its machine steps up when it runs, RUN-3) - before, an unfinished count never said a plan fits, so plans that fitted were coarsened (18 of the corpus; job bf863215's bolt fillets at 3 elements per quarter-turn instead of 6). **Over the limit, the low-stress bulk is coarsened first**, kind by kind and each only as far as it must: rounds and relief (their shape only), capped corners, walls and the parts' own sizes, and only then holes, fillets and notches; kinds coarsened to their minimums before a later kind fitted are given back as far as the plan still fits. The plan records each kind's floor and the kinds kept at their rules' sizes. |
| PLAN-fea-16 | in force | **The plan is sized by the accuracy rules, not by a machine.** The element limit is what the LARGEST machine allowed holds (RUN-2: an m1-ultramem-40, 961 GB, 90% usable), by the measured model peak = 450 MB + 5,100 bytes per element with 1.25 times to spare (lab VMs, 22 Oct) - about 145M elements (`engines/fea_driver.element_limit`). The Cloud Run job's memory and the old 8M cap no longer coarsen a plan (Areen, 24 Oct): a plan they cannot hold runs on a bigger machine (RUN-1). Only past the largest machine is the plan coarsened (PLAN-fea-8), never past what keeps elements valid; when even the least valid mesh is over it, the part is meshed anyway with a plain note. The model, the machine and the limit are in the why record. |
| PLAN-fea-14 | in force | **The base size is the coarsest the part allows** - its diagonal over 10 - and every rule refines below it where it asks to (walls, hot spots, corner caps, rounds, growth). That is the mesh the rules recommend; there is no detail level. |
| PLAN-fea-9 | retired | **One part only** (version 1): a file of several bodies stopped before meshing. Retired 23 Oct by PLAN-fea-17: an assembly is meshed. |
| PLAN-fea-17 | in force | **An assembly is meshed, its parts joined as the user said** (`cad/fea_assembly.py`, `engines/fea_assembly_deck.py`). Each solid body is a part, Part_k in OpenCASCADE's order, named from the CAD product (else by its number); several files uploaded together are joined into one such file first (`cad/ingest/assembly_files.py`), each part where its file puts it. Every face is Part_k_Face_j (the j-th face of part k). OpenCASCADE's general fuse finds where parts TOUCH (a face both share: a contact candidate) and where they PASS INTO each other (a solid both share: interference); the stage marks both and asks one question, how the parts are connected - the answer every pair takes unless it is set on its own (PLAN-fea-19). **Bonded** (welded or glued, the default): the fuse itself is meshed, so the parts share their nodes where they touch (one conformal mesh), and a shared piece of interference is one piece of material kept by the smaller body (PLAN-fea-20), said in a note; should the fuse fail, the parts are meshed apart and tied (*TIE / BCTABLE IGLUE / CONTA174 bonded), never a stop. **Contact** (they can slide or separate): each part is meshed on its own and every touching or overlapping pair is a named contact pair, the smaller surface the slave (*SURFACE + *CONTACT PAIR, hard and frictionless as a default to change; Nastran BCPROP + BCBODY + BCTABLE + BCPARA with the surfaces as BSURFS; Ansys TARGE170 / CONTA174). Each part is its own element set (and PSOLID / material number), sized by its own rules - its base a tenth of the part (PLAN-fea-14 per part), held all through the part, not only on its faces - and the element limit (PLAN-fea-8) covers the whole assembly. A part touching no other is meshed and said; a part wholly inside another, which has no surface to pair with, is said, never dropped quietly. A bonded mesh killed for memory is not tried again tied (LAD-fea-7). **Parts that meet tangentially are tied from the start** (27 Oct): where, in the fuse, outer faces of two parts meet at an edge with outward normals within 5 degrees of opposite - a round of one running onto the other's face, a crack of no angle - no mesher can fill the fused parts (job bf863215: each bolt's head fillet onto its washer, "PLC Error: A segment and a facet intersect"), so the assembly is meshed apart and tied, said in a note (`cad/fea_assembly.tangent_meets`). **Every zone has its own id** (rulebook 43): a face zone of an assembly is named with its part and face, W2.9 for Part_2_Face_9 (a single part keeps W9), so a lever, a check row or an AI choice that names a zone names one part's zone - before, every part's Face_9 was "W9" (44 of 46 assembly plans in the FEA hunt, 27 Oct). |
| PLAN-fea-10 | in force | **A cusp is set aside, and named.** Where two CAD edges of one face leave a vertex within 5 degrees of each other (a bore or boss just touching a straight face; a nut's chamfer meeting its flats), or where a face's boundary collapses to a point (a degenerated edge: a fillet or blend running out against a bore or pin; a sphere's, cone's or revolved face's pole is not one), the material runs to nothing and no element there can be well shaped at any size. The plan finds every cusp before meshing (`cad/fea_measure.cad_cusps`); the elements within 3 of their own longest edges of one that are under the SICN floor or miss the verdict's aspect or dihedral limits (`engines/fea_quality.LIMITS`) get straight sides (always valid: a positive Jacobian), are set aside from the quality floor, and the mesh is delivered with a concern naming each place: the material runs to nothing here, so stress at this point is not meaningful; round it off in CAD for a clean mesh. Every other element must meet the floor (Areen's decision, 21 Oct). |
| PLAN-fea-12 | in force | **A micro detail is set aside, and named.** A CAD edge shorter, or a face narrower, than 1/20 of the finest element the plan asks for (a sprocket's teeth drawn with 0.001 mm flat strips, a socket's 0.004 mm edges) pins the elements on it into bad shapes at any size the level allows. The plan finds them before meshing (`cad/fea_measure.cad_micro`); the elements on them get straight sides (always valid), are set aside from the quality floor, and the delivery names each detail and says that stress right at it is not resolved - on a gear, the contact stress at the tooth tips. Every other element must meet the floor (Areen's decision, 21 Oct). **A round under the measure's own floor is a micro detail** (27 Oct): a fillet, hole, notch or round whose radius is less than the floor the wall readings stop at (1/100,000 of the part) is set aside and named, never a zone - a kart frame's 0.0016 mm "fillets" asked for 0.4 um elements. **A round kept only for its shape (PLAN-fea-7) under 1/1,000 of its part** is an edge break the elements' curved sides follow, not a shape: no zone of its own, named (a 127 mm coil's 0.06 mm rounds asked for 31.6M elements). A round that is a face of a sharp corner is still such a micro round (the corner's zone is along its edge, not on the round; rulebook 49). **Raised or sunk detail under 1/1,000 of its part is relief** (27 Oct): faces standing off a flat face by no more than 1/1,000 of their body's diagonal, on one side of it, touching nothing but that face and each other - lettering, a logo, a shallow texture - carry no load of their own. Their walls, corners, fillets and rounds get no zone and are none of the part's hot spots, nor its thinnest wall; one zone as fine as the relief is high keeps their shape, and each piece is named (`cad/fea_features.relief_groups`). A chamfer or a step at a face's edge touches the next face, and a thin sheet's other side faces away, so neither is relief. A 15 mm printer part's 0.05 mm lettering asked for 0.016 mm corners and 2 elements through its 0.06 mm strokes: 27.1M elements, now 1.54M. |
| PLAN-fea-18 | in force | **Where parts meet is measured on their surfaces, and parts across a small gap are joined** (`cad/fea_joins.py`, `cad/fea_assembly.joined_interfaces`). Every pair of parts whose surfaces come near is read pair by pair on the surfaces themselves, never by asking a solid what is inside it (a part whose surface crosses itself answers that wrongly - 26 Oct: a tube whose hole cuts left a skin of no thickness made the fuse put a bracket "100% inside" a tube it does not reach and miss the same joints on the mirrored side): faces whose outward normals face each other (within 45 degrees of opposite) TOUCH within the fuse's own fuzzy distance, or face each other across a SMALL GAP of up to 0.5 mm, or 2% of the smaller part's box diagonal when that is less - the bonded-contact tolerance a solver user sets for a weld or a thread the CAD does not draw (a boss sleeved into a clearance hole, a bolt in its bore); a wider gap (a bolt in a clearance hole) stays a clearance. Parts whose surfaces come within the gap limit only along a LINE, an EDGE or at a POINT (no faces facing each other over the least joint area: a tube on a plate, a plate's edge on a frame, a ring in a countersink, corners a hair apart; the industrial hunt, 27 Oct) are joined there too, the faces that come near tied with the gap limit as the solver's reach; faces that face each other across a band that closes to nothing touch along a line, and are said so. A joint needs (1% of the smaller part)^2 of facing area. A face another part passes through that also crosses, or lies on, another face of its own part is a SKIN: its crossing joins nothing, and its faces are named for the sizing. The fuse's interfaces are checked against the surfaces (the JOIN CHECK): a pair it joins whose surfaces are apart or meet only on skins, or a part it puts wholly inside another whose box it is not inside, sets the fuse aside - the measured joints are used, and a bonded assembly is meshed apart and tied there; otherwise the fuse's interfaces stand exactly as before, and each small-gap pair, and each touch the fuse missed (two coincident faces it did not merge, parts meeting along a line - the public assembly corpus has them, 27 Oct), is added after them and tied. The stage proposes each gap joint in words with one switch, on by default ("Join parts across small gaps"); bonded, a gap joint is tied, contact makes it a contact pair. Every gap pair carries its measured gap and its REACH (the widest gap on its faces and a quarter more). EVERY TIE IS SETTLED before it is written, the same in every solver (`engines/fea_assembly_deck.settle_ties`; the deep test of #334, 28 Oct: a fused joint also tied stopped CalculiX with "zero coefficient on the dependent side", and ties that moved their nodes gave 8,804 "nonpositive jacobian"): its TOLERANCE is the gap pair's reach, or 5% of the master's typical element edge where the parts touch - and, where they pass into each other (a press fit), the slave's nodes inside the master part down to one master element edge; its SLAVE is the side with more nodes within that tolerance of the other - the finer mesh there - and the smaller surface when they are even, unless that side is another tie's master (a master is never a slave too); a slave node is tied only when it is not the master's own node (where the parts are one mesh they are joined already), lies within the tolerance of the master surface, is not held by a nearer tie (a node follows one master) and lies on no tie's master surface (no chains of ties: one comes back round where the parts are also fused, and CalculiX loops on "zero coefficient" without end); a tie whose slave nodes are all the master's own is a bonded joint, its faces still named. Each solver gets the same pair, slave and tolerance and ties the nodes where they stand: *TIE on a node surface of exactly the nodes tied, POSITION TOLERANCE, ADJUST=NO; Nastran BCTABLE ERROR; Ansys CONTA174 pinball (real constant 6) with KEYOPT(9) = 1, its contact elements only on the slave faces that hold a tied node. `assembly.json` names the gap faces and the skins (`joins.gap_faces`, `joins.skin_faces`), for the sizing to read. The stage reads the joints within 150 s, the run's staging within 600 s; a measurement cut short keeps the fuse's answer, said. The faces that come near are found by a nearest-point tree on the two parts' surface points (never all pairs of faces); a pair of them whose boxes (OpenCASCADE's optimal boxes of the exact faces) lie further apart than the gap limit and twice the touch tolerance is not measured point by point (TIME-12): no distance between them can be within either. |
| PLAN-fea-19 | in force | **Each pair of parts is joined as its own choice says** (`cad/fea_pairs.py`, `cad/fea_assembly.stage`, `engines/fea_assembly_deck.py`). Every pair of parts that meet (touching, across a small gap, or passing into each other; one pair per two parts, keyed by the two part ids, "Part_1\|Part_3") is **Bonded** (welded or glued), **Contact** (a contact pair: they can slide or separate) or **Off** (not joined: the mesh leaves them unconnected there). By rule a pair takes the stage's whole-assembly answer; a small-gap pair takes it while the small-gap switch is on and is Off when it is off. The stage lists every pair with its two parts, what was measured (the gap, the facing area, how deep they pass into each other) and its type, and the user may set any one pair; a pair the user set stands over the rule (source `user` in the why record), one left alone follows the rule when the run stages it. The type reaches every deck: Bonded is a shared mesh or a tie (*TIE; Nastran BCTABLE IGLUE=1; Ansys CONTA174 KEYOPT(12)=5), Contact a contact pair (*CONTACT PAIR; BCTABLE IGLUE=0; CONTA174 KEYOPT(12)=0), Off nothing. A bonded assembly stays one fused mesh while every touching or overlapping pair is bonded (a small-gap pair may be any type there: its sides are not one surface); once such a pair is set to Contact or Off, each part is meshed on its own and the bonded pairs are tied, said in a note. A pair may carry a reach the user set (millimetres, up to 1000), written in place of the measured one. Each pair's type and reason is recorded in the why record (the first 200 pairs one by one, the rest in one entry). |
| PLAN-fea-20 | in force | **Where two parts pass into each other, the smaller body keeps its full shape** (`cad/fea_pairs.default_keep`, `cad/fea_assembly.stage`, `engines/fea_assembly_deck.prepare`). A bonded mesh of one piece makes the shared piece one piece of material, and that piece belongs to the SMALLER body by volume - the larger gives way there, the rule SpaceClaim's interference tool publishes (it cuts the overlap out of the larger body) - so a pin pressed into a bracket keeps its whole section; two bodies the same size (within 0.1%: a body drawn twice) leave it with the lower-numbered. Before this the lower-numbered part kept it, so the file's order decided. The stage says per pair who keeps it and why, and the user may name the other part. A piece three or more parts share goes to the smallest of them. Meshed apart (tied or contact), each part keeps its full shape as drawn, as Ansys bonded contact does. A part left with no elements of its own because other parts keep every piece of it (a body drawn once whole and once in pieces; lab, 28 Oct) is said by name, with the parts that keep it, never dropped quietly. |
| PLAN-fea-21 | in force | **An open bonded pair is named, never a clean pass** (`engines/fea_assembly_deck.open_joints`, `engines/verdict_limits.fea_limits`). After meshing, every bonded pair meshed apart (a tie) is checked face by face: each of its CAD faces, on either side, must have mesh nodes within the reach the files give the solver of the other part's surface (the measured reach, the one set on the stage, or for a touching tie each solver's default - read as Abaqus's published 5% of a typical master facet). A face with none is not joined at all - Abaqus and Ansys both leave nodes outside the reach untied - so the pair is OPEN: the result shows it in red with the pair, its faces and how far their nearest nodes are, the mesh report says it, and the verdict row "Bonded pairs left open" makes the run delivered with concerns (VER-2), never a clean pass. The way on is said with it: switch the pair to Contact or Off on the stage, or set its reach to at least the distance found. For two parts that pass into each other and are tied apart, the tie joins them only where their surfaces cross, so no reach is offered: the way on is to remove the overlap in the CAD, switch the pair to Contact or Off, or leave every touching pair Bonded so the two are meshed as one piece (lab, 28 Oct). A check that could not run is not measured, never taken as closed. A pair of one shared mesh shares its nodes and is closed by construction. A tie the writer settles (PLAN-fea-18) is judged by what it ties: one that ties no node - its near nodes all beside the other part's faces, none over them - is open, and its way on is Contact, Off or the CAD, never a wider reach (lab, 29 Oct). |
| PLAN-fea-22 | in force | **The size choice: the full-rules mesh, or a quicker one - recommended by what it gives up, picked by the user** (`engines/fea_plan_options.py`). Once an FEA CAD part is shown on the stage, the check plans its mesh as the run plans it, in a child given its own time after the stage is shown (30 minutes, not what the naming has left), and keeps the plans it made: the run reuses them when they were made for its own staged solid and the same planning code, and the AI changed nothing in them (planned once). When the full-rules mesh fits the standard mesh job there is nothing to choose. When it needs a bigger machine (RUN-1), the stage shows two meshes, each with its elements, machine and time - what such a run usually takes (the whole run's measured rate) and its upper end (the part's slow fill rate), and that a retry can take longer: the FULL-RULES mesh, and a QUICKER one whose limit is what keeps it on the standard job (the mesher and its solver files in the job's usable memory, over PLAN-fea-8's aim), coarsened in PLAN-fea-8's order with every zone it leaves short named. The quicker mesh is recommended when it coarsens only rounds and relief kept for their shape and capped sharp corners (whose stress never settles): the walls keep their elements through them and every fillet, hole and notch its rule size. When it would coarsen a wall, a part's own size or a stress raiser, the full mesh is recommended. The recommended one is preselected; the user picks, the pick is said in the declaration and read into the run (`fea_plan_choice`), and THE CHOICE IS SETTLED BEFORE THE RUN, WHICH NEVER WAITS (Areen, 28 Oct): with nothing picked - the stage had not sized it yet when the user went on, its sizing stopped (TIME-11: past its clock or with its worker gone, said so on the stage), or no answer came - the run plans both meshes itself and takes the recommended one, said in the why record and in the plan's words on the result. Each pass also records the mesher's measured peak per element against PLAN-fea-16's model, the evidence its cautious margin is revisited on. When even its validity minimums need a bigger machine there is no quicker mesh - and that is told BEFORE the quicker mesh is planned (TIME-12): only when the full-rules mesh needs a bigger machine, the plan with every kind at its validity minimum is counted under the standard machine's limit, only as far as it takes to tell whether it fits; when it does not, the quicker mesh is not planned (it would not be offered) and the stage says the same words as when it was. |
| PLAN-fea-23 | in force | **An assembly's general fuse runs in millimetres** (`cad/fea_assembly.fuse`). The staging writes the assembly in metres, and OpenCASCADE's own tolerances are absolute (1e-7), fitted to CAD drawn in millimetres. Fused in metres, the 406 parts of a laser cutter grew past 26 GB over 33 minutes and the 32 GiB mesh job was killed there twice (o_ox_mc_laser, and a biped robot the same way); the same parts in millimetres took 3.2 GB and 2 minutes (lab, 27 Oct). So parts in another unit are scaled to millimetres for the fuse (its fuzzy distance with them) and the result is scaled back, every face and solid in the same order, so the sidecar names them as before. The pair-by-pair fuse and the check's assembly record do the same. The sidecar says the unit the fuse ran in (`fuse_unit`). The scaled fuse is never finer than the fuse in the parts' own unit: OpenCASCADE takes points closer than 1e-7 as one, in any unit, so its fuzzy value is at least 1e-7 of the parts' unit, scaled with them (fused in millimetres without that floor, fc_zcoupling kept near-coincident edges that cross in the 1D mesh, its bonded fill failed twice and its parts were tied; lab, 28 Oct). The staged file and every length the mesher reads stay in metres. The fused shape itself is worked out at the new scale, so a bonded assembly's mesh can differ slightly from the metre fuse's: the same parts, joints and shared faces, a few tenths of a percent of its elements laid out differently (regression set, 28 Oct). |
| PLAN-fea-24 | in force | **A cosmetic fillet is meshed at the size around it** (`engines/fea_plan.cosmetic_fillets`, rulebook 49). A fillet hot spot whose radius is under 1/20 of the wall it sits in AND under 1/1,000 of its part (the PLAN-fea-12 share) gets no zone of its own: resolving it would ask for elements under 1/150 of that wall (6 per quarter-turn is 0.26 r), and its stress peak stays within about one radius of its root.
