| SHMESH-100 | in force | **The time left goes to quality: extra passes at the elements past a limit** (rulebook 133, `engines/gmsh/shell_quality_passes.py`: `RULE`, `QUALITY_PASSES`, `PASS_SHARE` = 0.5, `PASS_MAX` = 500 (a backstop only), `ON_CAD`, `ON_TOL` = 1e-3, `STEPS`, `TURNS` = 8, `FINE_STEPS`, `PAIR_STEPS`, `PAIRS`, `REMESH`, `REMESH_RINGS` = (1, 2), `REMESH_MAX` = 400, `REMESH_ON` = 0.25; `shell_cad_projector.PatchMesher`; `TARGET_MAX` = 4000, `BISECT_MAX` = 14, `STALL_MAX` = 3, `PROJECT_MAX` = 4; `shell_adapter._quality_passes`, `QUALITY_PASSES_RULE`; `engines/gmsh/shell_cad_projector.py`). Since the shell mesh's limit follows its work (SHMESH-21e), what a run does not need of it goes to the mesh's quality, never to an earlier delivery. When the mesh is made with a measured figure past its limit in force - the lines the verdict judges: the user's own quality lines while they set any (SHMESH-26), our usual shell targets (`shell_quality.LIMITS`) while they set none, Nastran's own GEOMCHECK limits always - and time is left under the run's limit (its start plus the limit in force, less TIME_MARGIN_S), the elements past it are worked on, the furthest past first: each free node of such an element (inside one flat face, in no place the user sized, no node of a ring, band, grid cell or mapped strip - SHMESH-83's pinned nodes, carried on the mesh as `held`) is tried at a few steps round where it stands in the face's plane, and a node on a straight stretch of its faces' boundary (two boundary edges in one line, every face round it flat: `SLIDE_SIN`) along that line, where it stays on every face round it; a corner never moves and goes where that element's figure improves most while no element round it comes out past a line in force it was inside, further past one it was past, folded, or with an edge newly past the user's shortest or longest; a triangle past such a line and the triangle across one of its sides on the same flat face have their diagonal turned under the same guard. Element kinds, numbers, faces, walls and groups are never changed. Each pass is measured whole as the verdict measures it and kept only when a figure past its limit improves (its worst, or fewer past with the worst no worse), no line of the user's that was met goes unmet, no line counts more elements outside it, no figure past its limit is worse, and no more elements are past Nastran's limits or invalid. On the CAD (rulebook 133): `_quality_passes` runs in a forked child of its own that reads the mid-surface B-rep again (gmsh OCC, its own session: the gmsh model the mesh was made in is closed by then, in another process); a free node of a curved face whose nodes all stand on one CAD surface (within `ON_TOL` of their shortest edge; a face made again stands on none and is left as made) is tried in its tangent plane and put back on that surface (`getClosestPoint`), a node on a curved stretch of the boundary (a hole's rim: it and its two boundary neighbours on one CAD curve) along the curve and put back on it, and two triangles of a curved face have their diagonal turned, all under the same guard. Passes go on while each one is kept: they stop at the first pass not kept, at the run's clock, or once they have spent `PASS_SHARE` (half) of the time left under the run's limit when they began, the rest staying for what follows; the best mesh is kept and the result says in one line what was done ("Improved the worst element shape (Jacobian) from 0.54 to 0.63 in 2 extra passes"), or that nothing improved. A mesh inside its limits, a run with no clock, and a mesh delivered at its stop are untouched. Recorded in `quality.quality_passes`. Measured on the public shell board (testlab-2, 25 Nov, criteria typed at each part's size, fd0a5bc1 and this rule side by side): of the 106 parts with a figure past a limit, 62 kept passes (31 with a worst figure better, 31 with fewer elements past it), 3 now meet every limit (passed with notes -> passed), 43 kept none (30: no node or diagonal could change without another figure getting worse; 12: not better measured whole; 1: no free node), 1 had no time left; the passes took a median 0.5 s, at most 27 s; the 67 parts inside their limits came out identical (counts, every figure, the deck's body). The perforated panel with no notes: worst Jacobian 0.547 -> 0.596 (7 -> 1 elements under 0.6) in 6 passes, 6 s. Rulebook 88, measured the same way (testlab-2, fd0a5bc1 and 4341ab53 side by side): of the 106 parts past a limit, 77 kept passes (was 62), 4 now meet every limit (was 3), 0 rows worse; per row past a limit: min angle 7 now met, 21 worst better, 57 fewer outside, 27 same; max angle 7 / 10 / 33 / 24; aspect ratio 2 / 14 / 17 / 23; Jacobian 4 now met; Nastran T3_SKEW (14), T3_IAMAX (13) and warpage (3) unchanged. On the same mesh before the passes, 41 parts came out better than with this rule's first version (before the CAD reading) and none worse. The cap of 6 had stopped only 3 parts; 9 now keep more passes (at most 8). Pass seconds: median 1.5 s, at most 196 s, 566 s in all; the 67 parts inside their limits came out identical. The panel with no notes: 0.547 -> 0.596 in 4 passes, 5 s (still 1 element under 0.6). Rulebook 89: a pass is kept patch by patch (a patch: the moves and the turn round one element past a line). When the pass measured whole is worse on a row (something the local guard cannot see), the longest run of its patches that is no worse is kept, found by halving (at most `BISECT_MAX` whole measures), and the patch after it is set aside: its element is not worked on again; one bad move no longer drops the good ones. A pass no worse but with no gain is kept too (the next pass starts from it); the passes stop after `STALL_MAX` such passes in a row. At the end the mesh is measured whole against the mesh as made and handed back only when it is better and no row of it worse, else the last mesh that was, else the mesh as made. On the CAD, a node's candidates are judged first in its tangent plane (along its curve's tangent) and only the `PROJECT_MAX` best that pass there are put on the CAD and judged again (a node with none costs no CAD call); a face's check reads a sample of its nodes (`shell_cad_projector.SAMPLE`) and each node of a curved face is checked by itself, once, when it is first a candidate to move (a node off its surface never moves). Recorded: `set_aside`, `probes`, `stalled`, `cad_s`. Rulebook 89, measured the same way (testlab-2, 3ade7251 against fd0a5bc1's results): of the 106 parts past a limit, 80 kept passes (was 77), 4 meet every limit (the same 4); per row past a limit (now met / worst better / fewer outside / same): min angle 7 / 23 / 56 / 23, max angle 7 / 9 / 35 / 21, aspect ratio 2 / 15 / 18 / 20, Jacobian 4 now met, Nastran T3_SKEW and T3_IAMAX 1 fewer each, warpage unchanged; 0 rows worse where the passes ran on base's mesh (3 parts made another mesh before the passes under another load; run again side by side, 2 came out no worse on any row and the third ran no passes, stopped at its clock). No pass measured whole was worse anywhere (0 patches set aside): what stopped passes before was a pass with no gain, which now goes on. The 67 parts inside their limits came out identical. Pass seconds: median 1.3 s, 90th percentile 11.4 s, at most 58 s (was 196 s: that part now 2 s, 1 s of it on the CAD). The panel with no notes: 0.547 -> 0.596 (1 element still under 0.6). Rulebook 89, round 4 (same id): a node none of `STEPS` helps is tried again at `FINE_STEPS` (shares of its shortest edge down to 0.002), and an element none of whose nodes can move alone, nor its diagonal turn, has two neighbouring nodes of it moved together (`PAIR_STEPS`, each in its own plane, along its line or on its CAD curve or surface; recorded `paired`), both under the same guard as one node's move: a node of a corner, a CAD edge's corner, a place the user sized, a structure or off its CAD surface never moves. Then the local re-mesh: where an element is still past a line once the passes end, the small patch round it (the elements sharing a node with it, then one ring more) is made again on its face (gmsh, a model of its own in the passes' CAD session; a curved face's new nodes put back on its CAD surface, at most `REMESH_ON` of the patch's edge size away) with the patch's outline fixed: every node on it, so every neighbour, CAD edge and typed seed, stays as it was. A patch is only tried on one known face, in no place the user sized, with one wall, body, group set and offset, and no node of a structure inside it; it is kept only when its worst figure on the line betters with no figure in it worse, no fold, no edge newly past the user's shortest or longest, and (a patch with quads) no more triangles. The element count changes only inside a kept patch. The mesh with its patches is measured whole and kept only when better with no row worse (else the longest run of patches that is, by halving), all inside the passes' share of the time. The result's line says how many patches were re-made; recorded `remeshed`, `remesh_tried`. Not without the CAD read again (no forked child): there it is not tried. Round 4, measured the same way (testlab-2, fd0a5bc1 and 880f61eb side by side): of the 106 parts past a limit, 83 kept passes (was 80), 5 meet every limit (was 4); per row past a limit (now met / worst better / fewer outside / same): min angle 8 / 30 / 54 / 20, max angle 8 / 11 / 34 / 21, aspect ratio 3 / 14 / 17 / 22, Jacobian 4 now met; Nastran and warpage as before; 0 rows worse. Two nodes moved together on 55 parts; 13 patches re-made on 3 parts (407 tried). The 67 parts inside their limits came out identical. Pass seconds: median 3.1 s, 90th percentile 42.7 s, at most 295 s (inside its share). The panel with no notes: 0.547 -> 0.596 (2 patches re-made; 1 element still under 0.6). **Amended 94 (round 13): the passes stop when they no longer help** (`GAIN_SHARE` = 0.05, `GAIN_OF` = "gained", `GAIN_RUN` = 4, `_pass_helps`): a pass helps when, on some line past its limit as it began, it brings the line inside or takes away at least 5% of all the passes have gained on that line so far, in elements past it or in its worst figure; 4 passes in a row that do not help (a pass with no gain is one) stop them, the best mesh so far kept as before; recorded `gain_stop`, and `stopped` says so. Chosen on a pass log of the public board (98 parts, 589 passes): this is the rule that saves the most seconds (74) while leaving no line worse than the full passes; 10% and 3 in a row would have saved 532 s and left 10 parts worse (17 more elements past, 6 worst figures worse), 10% and 1 pass 1,595 s and 43 parts worse - the passes' tail is small real gains, not waste. |
| SHMESH-101 | candidate | **A narrow mapped strip aims its elements inside the user's aspect target** (**OFF since rulebook 133**: `STRIP_AIM` = False, `STRIP_OPEN` = False. Measured on the public shell board (173 parts, base 4c2c9122 side by side with the round 10 tree): the finer counts stayed in the delivered mesh on 12 parts - 3 better on some row, 2 worse, no part to pass - and were safe only through the keep check, a whole second mesh on 42 parts that cost 2,622 of the branch's 3,919 extra seconds; the open-strip pass acted on 0 parts. The text below is what it did when on. Rulebook 90, amended 91, 92 and 93, `engines/gmsh/shell_adapter.py`: `STRIP_AIM_KEEP`, `AIM_OFF_NAME`, `_rows_worse`, `_aim_check_classic`, `STRIP_AIM_RULE`, `STRIP_AIM`, `STRIP_AIM_SHARE` = 0.6, `STRIP_AIM_ADD_MAX` = 20000, `STRIP_OPEN`, `STRIP_OPEN_PASSES` = 1, `STRIP_FLOOR_SHARE` = 0.2; `_aspect_target`, `_strip_edge_floor`, `_time_for_pass`, `_Reopen`; `engines/shell_requirements.py`: `low_typed`). **Amended 93 (round 9): the re-count is taken out; the keep check covers every mesh a part can keep.** The round 8 re-count of strips joined by shared long sides acted on one part of 173 on the public board and there made it worse (aspect elements past the target 1,400 -> 2,868): it is removed, not left as a switch. Its keep check never ran on that part, because the part kept SHMESH-36's mesh (#435's steps), which the finer counts reach too but which never passes SHMESH-76's candidates. Now that mesh, when SHMESH-36 keeps it and the finer counts acted on it, is made again with them off; the finer counts stay only where no quality row is worse with them (a row met that is then missed, more elements past it, more past the solver's own limits), else that mesh as planned is set against this PR's mesh again in SHMESH-36's own order, and the notes say the counts were left as planned and on which rows. A keep check that could not be made is recorded (no time left, or the mesh failed), never silent. **Amended 92 (round 8): kept only where they help.** When time allows, the part is meshed again as planned (the finer counts off) and the finer counts are kept only where no quality row of the part is worse with them; else the part keeps the plan and the notes say so and which rows. This is judged per part, not per strip. **Amended 91 (round 7): the strips left open.** A strip narrower than the shortest edge is planned to be closed (SHMESH-60) and so is counted along at the neighbours' size; when its closing is refused (SHMESH-68/94) it stays open with elements about the global size long and its width wide. As measured in round 6 this branch is where the strips that hold the past elements sit, and the 90 text below never reached it. Now, when a pass ends with such strips refused and time is left (another pass costs at most what the run spent so far, with 30 s spare), the pass is made once more with those strips meshed as open strips: one row across, along max(edge floor, 0.6 x the aspect target x its width). The aspect target is the user's (their aspect line), else the usual quad aspect target in force. The edge floor: **a shortest edge the user typed holds** - where it keeps a strip past the aspect target the notes say how many elements, the narrowest width, and the shortest edge that would clear them (target x narrowest width). **When the user typed none** (our own default, 0.5 x the global size), the floor is 0.2 x the global size, and the notes say how many strips went under our default and to what floor. The same guards: no face sized in a named place, at most 20,000 elements added a mesh, one extra pass at most. 90 text, still in force for strips wider than the shortest edge: Under the user's aspect target, a mapped strip one element across (narrower than its size, SHMESH-16/24) is stretched along it to 0.6 of that target times its width, not 0.9 of it: on skewed and tapered strips quads made at 0.9 measured past the target, and these strips held 99.5% of the elements a structure pins (round 6 census). Never under the user's shortest edge (SHMESH-63 still caps the count), never on a face the user sized in a named place (SHMESH-52); at most 20,000 elements added a mesh, the strips past that keep the plan. Told in the notes: how many strips, the finest length along, the elements added; and how many kept the plan for the cap. |

SHMESH-100's passes held every node of a structure fixed (SHMESH-83's pinned nodes), a mapped strip's too. Once the plan card stands, the chat box is where mesh requirements are typed (there is no separate notes box). THE REST (short words with no requirement in them) is asked of the intake's own model, one small question with a strict answer set - go / wait / question / requirement / other - given our last line and the message, within 3 s. **go** starts the run (exactly as the Proceed button does) only when the model says go AND code found no requirement content, no question mark, no negation or condition ("not", "don't", "but", "first") - a no that only says nothing more is wanted and then a clear go is no negation ("nope, nothing else. go", "no, that's all, mesh it": every clause before the last a no to more or a nothing-more, the last a go that acts; never "no go", "don't go yet", "nothing else to go on"; `plan_talk._done_then_go`, rulebook 91) and no plain "ok" to another question of ours; the model's "wait" is a hold, its "question" goes to the intake, anything else is asked "Start the mesh now?" once - but only when the words could ask to start (a yes or a hedge to the consent reader, a go asked back, filler only, or a go word: `plan_talk.goish`, rulebook 97); any other statement the model is unsure of is kept as a requirement and answered as kept (NOTE-25). CONTEXT ABOUT THE PART is kept in code before any model is asked (`plan_talk.context_statement`, rulebook 97): a statement, no question, no engine and no other plan word, that names the material ("the part is cast aluminium"), the loads or supports, the solver ("we'll run this in Abaqus" is no go for its "run"), or the analysis the plan already has ("it's for a stress analysis of the bracket" on a static stress plan, read from the user's words as `flow.analysis_said`) - never a go, never "Start the mesh now?", never a re-plan; another analysis than the plan's ("make it a modal analysis") is still the intake's (before rulebook 97, live: "the part is cast aluminium" was asked "Start the mesh now?", "it's for a stress analysis of the bracket" threw the plan away, and a model's "go" on "we'll run this in Abaqus" started the run). The model failing or slower than 3 s: the phrase reading decides (every clause a go clause or filler - "ok I'm good to mesh", "no more changes", "that's all from me, mesh it" - or the intake's consent words), and where it is unsure the go question is asked once; a go-like sentence is never answered "couldn't read that as a mesh requirement". **ask** - a go asked back ("ready to mesh?", "can we proceed?") or a hedge ("maybe"): "Start the mesh now?" is asked once; asked already, the message is kept as a requirement. **hold** - "wait", "not yet", "don't proceed yet", "hold on": nothing starts and nothing is kept, and the reply says so ("OK, I won't start yet. That's my guess from your words; click it on the model if you meant another spot.", a phrase it could not place, the flow reader's own lines on a flow mesh; nothing taken: the words are still kept, and the reply says so and what it can take (NOTE-25; before rulebook 94 "I couldn't find a mesh setting in that.") - only for words that state something to set: words that ask a question are answered as one (NOTE-20); a picture: kept with the note and shown, but not read itself, so the place comes from the words or a clicked face (said once, only when a picture came); a message with picked faces and one plain size in mm and nothing else is answered at once from its known row, without waiting for the reading (rulebook 85); a model with no checklist before the run: kept, read when the mesh is built. |

| NOTE-24 | in force | **An element or cell count typed at the plan step is a row of the checklist, and the reply says it** (rulebook 93, `agents/intake/plan_talk.count_asks`, `plan_talk.count_rows`, `application/part_matches.with_counts`, `agents/intake/plan_reply.facts`, `plan_reply._count_check`). Before, the plan-step checklist on an FEA mesh came only from the parts reader (NOTE-5), which reads sizes in places and never a count, so "keep it under 1.5 million elements" got "I couldn't find a mesh setting in that" (deployed 91926483, FEA solid). Now a count the words state for the whole mesh - a number with its multiplier ("1.5 million", "800k", "500,000", "2M") before "elements" / "cells" / "tets" / "hexes", or after "element count", at least 1,000 - is read in code, with its bound: under / at most / max / up to / no more than (and "max" or "or fewer" after it) is a ceiling, at least / over / min (and "or more") a floor, anything else about. A range ("700k-1.5M", "between 1 and 2 million") and a number being replaced ("instead of 8 million") are left to the run's reader; a size ("3 mm elements"), a count under 1,000 ("2 elements through the wall") and a question with no number (NOTE-20) are no count. The newest note that states one gives the row: what (an element count, the whole mesh), the bound and the number as typed ("at most 1.5 million elements"; "cells" on a flow mesh), stamped with that note's id. It is shown only where the run's own reader applies it: every flow mesh (CFDREQ-3), and the solid and shell meshes while their AI step reads the notes (AI-fea-4 -> PLAN-fea-27 / SHMESH-23; with that step off the run applies no count and no row is shown). The reply is written by the model under NOTE-19's guard, from facts that hold the count (its bound and number as typed, no place needed), what is already on the checklist, and - when sizes the user typed are on the checklist too - which one wins on this route: on the solid and shell meshes the sizes typed are built and the count moves only the rest (PLAN-fea-29, PLAN-fea-33, SHMESH-24); on a flow mesh the count comes first (CFDREQ-3); either way the result says what could not be held. The guard also refuses a reply that does not say the count's number as typed, says it with another bound, or - beside such sizes - promises it or gives no condition. Its fallback is said in code: "I'll keep it under 1.5 million elements." ("I'll make it at least 2 million cells.", "I'll aim for about 800k elements."), then, beside typed sizes, "If that can't hold with the sizes you typed, your sizes win and the result will say so." (flow: "... the count comes first and the result will say where the sizes gave way."). The plan step does not settle a count against a size; the run does, and tells it. On a flow mesh the CFD reader reads the same count again, so its line ("From your notes: about 2 million cells.") and a count of the same bound saved in the team's settings ("From your settings: ...") are not listed beside the row (`part_matches.listed_once`): one setting is listed once, the typed value winning (before, 26 Nov: the typed count showed twice on a CFD checklist). |
| NOTE-25 | in force | **Words the checklist takes nothing from are kept, and the reply says so truthfully** (rulebook 94, `agents/intake/plan_talk.took_nothing`, `plan_talk.NOT_READ`, `plan_talk.kept_reply`, `plan_talk.geometry_edit`, `agents/intake/plan_reply._kept_facts`, `plan_reply.check`). A statement at the plan step is kept as the user's note before it is read (NOTE-18), every route's planning step is handed the notes as guidance (NOTE-2: `user_notes.txt`, the builder, the snappy planner, the FEA judgement calls, the requirements reader), and the result tells each ask back as applied, the nearest, not applied with the reason, or not a mesh setting (NOTE-8, NOTE-12). So a statement the checklist took nothing from ("make it look nice", "it's for a stress analysis") is no longer answered "I couldn't find a mesh setting in that" (Areen, 25 Nov: what the user types gets built; acknowledged and kept, never turned away). The reply reads like a person talking back (Areen, 25 Nov: "make it reply like an LLM is talking back to us"; the first wording, "That isn't something I can lock in on the checklist, but I've kept ... for mesh planning. ... You can set a size, refinement, quality limit, element count, or click a face.", read as a form and was dropped): written by the model under NOTE-19's guard, ONE short sentence, two at most, that says what they told it in its own words and that it will be kept in mind when the mesh is planned ("Got it, cast aluminium. I'll keep that in mind when the mesh is planned."). It adds that the result will say what came of it only when the words ask something of the mesh (`plan_talk.asks_of_the_mesh`: a requirement word or "mesh", as in "make it a good quality mesh please"), never for plain context such as a material. Words that ask for a change to the CAD itself - removing or filling holes, fillets, chamfers, slots and the like - are told that meshing cannot do that and the mesh follows the part as it is, that they are kept, and that the result will say it was not applied. The guard refuses a reply to either that claims anything was set, applied, done or guaranteed (unless negated: "it wasn't applied", or said as a condition: "the result will say if it was applied", "whether this mesher could apply it", "it may be applied"), that does not say the words were kept (in any natural words: "noted", "I'll keep that in mind", "I'll remember that for the planning"), or that says "mesh setting" - the same claim test answers a flow model's requirement kept for the run (the real model's "I've noted ... The result will show whether this mesher could apply it." was refused as a claim before); to words kept for the planning also one that says "checklist" or "lock in", lists what can be set (or asks for a click), runs past two sentences (three with a picture), quotes their sentence back in quotation marks, or speaks of the result for plain context. Its fallback, said in code (`plan_talk.kept_reply`): "Noted. I'll keep that in mind when the mesh is planned." (words that ask something of the mesh: "Noted. I'll keep that in mind when the mesh is planned, and the result will say what came of it."; the CAD change: "Removing or filling features changes the CAD itself, which meshing can't do, so the mesh follows the part as it is. I've kept your words, and the result will say that wasn't applied."). Unchanged: questions (NOTE-20), a hold, a go, the one-place asks and their lines (NOTE-21, NOTE-22, NOTE-23), and a flow model with no checklist before the run (kept, read when the mesh is built). |
| NOTE-26 | in force | **What a typed requirement costs is said at the plan step of an FEA solid** (rulebook 104, amended 123; `application/plan_cost.py`, called from `application/part_matches.run_reading`; `part_matches.with_cost`, `part_matches.read_fea_notes`, `part_matches.whole_or_rest`; `engines/fea_plan_options.requirements_plan`; `engines/fea_driver.requirement_inputs`, `fea_driver.requirements_section`; `engines/meshing_standard.decide_and_base`; `agents/intake/plan_talk.cost_mine`, `plan_talk.cost_line`; `agents/intake/plan_reply._cost_facts`, `plan_reply._cost_check`; `api/v1/chat._requirement_reply`). A cast knuckle, 25 Nov: "finer at the fillets" took a plan of about 1 million elements to 10 million on the 128 GB machine, a 96-minute run, and nothing said so before the run. Now, when the checklist's reading of the user's words lands on the geometry worker and anything typed sets the mesh (a size, a finer or coarser place, a count - NOTE-24 - or a requirement the notes' own reading holds, such as a size on a picked face), the plan the run would make with every requirement so far is planned BY THE RUN'S OWN STEPS WITH THE RUN'S OWN INPUTS: the notes read with the team's saved standard as the run reads them (`meshing_standard.decide` then `fea_driver.requirements_section`; the standard the run will be dispatched with goes with the reading), the parts reading, the faces picked on the model (NOTE-17), the count the checklist shows (else the standard's, STD-1), the size picked on the stage and the stage's kept plans - through `fea_driver.requirement_inputs` and `fea_driver.size_choice`, which the run itself calls before its first planning. The C5 knuckle, 26 Nov: said "about 0.4 million", built 0.65-0.74 million - the cost had planned without the standard and without the picked face (a test part of the same shape: 10,669 costed against the run's 13,485; now equal). What only the run adds is its AI step's own refinements (AI-fea-1), decided when it starts. BEFORE IS THE PLAN AS IT STOOD JUST BEFORE THIS MESSAGE: the reading before this one's "after" (kept with it), so the replies chain; with no reading before, or one that had nothing to cost (it keeps a marker), it is the plan the team's standard alone makes; after a reading whose costing failed it is not known, and nothing is compared. (The knuckle's third reply, "finer in/around the holes", said "from about 0.9 million to about 0.4 million" - the first reply's numbers - because "before" was always the stage's plan with nothing typed.) Notes that could not be read are no cost at all. Nothing is costed when the stage did not size the part. THE ELEMENT COUNT ONLY, NO MINUTES: the time models held (`fea_driver.FEA_USUAL_FILL_RATES`, the lab's whole runs; RUN-11 records each run's stages but holds no model of them) do not cover the queue, the machine starting, the quality passes the ladder repeats or the reviewer - the knuckle's "3 minutes" took 8.5 - so no time is kept or said. It is said when the element count moves by more than a quarter (`plan_cost.WORTH_SAYING` = 0.25; smaller moves are inside the estimate's own error) or the plan needs a bigger machine, and only in the reply to a message that set something. A size typed with a face picked is no longer answered at once (the short way had no cost to say) on an FEA solid the stage sized: its reply waits for the reading like any other. The reply is written by the model under NOTE-19's guard from facts that hold the counts before and after and whether a bigger machine is needed; the guard refuses a reply that does not say the count after, says a bigger machine when none is needed, or says a number the facts do not hold (a time). Its fallback is said in code: "That takes it from about 0.7 million to about 2.9 million elements." (", and it needs a bigger machine" when it does). It is a fact, never a warning or an "are you sure": what the user typed gets built. The count row on the checklist shows the estimate too ("Planned: about 1.4 million elements"). On a single part, a size the reader put on everything else reads "the whole part" unless another row sizes a place of its own (then "the rest of the part"). Measured 26 Nov on the test parts: one planning took 0.06-2.2 s, so it runs inside the reading; the reply never waits past NOTE-19's limit for the reading. AMENDED 125 (the C5 knuckle on the site, 26 Nov, after the fix above: the chat's last count about 0.4 million, the run's "Estimated before meshing" 857,032; "finer in/around the holes" got no count line; replies of 47, 31 and 24 s): THE NUMBER SAID IS THE RUN'S "Estimated before meshing" for the same requirements (`plan_cost.planned`). The parts reading is costed AS THE RUN TAKES IT (`cad/part_words.as_run`, the one copy `part_words.decide` uses: each area with its faces) - the check keeps its reading without faces, so an area asked finer was costed as a part that does not exist and changed nothing (a part of the knuckle's shape, 11 holes in 4 sizes: 71,489 costed against the run's 235,846; now equal). The run's AI step (AI-fea-1..9) is asked when the words are costed, on the brief the run builds, and its answer goes to the run with the reading (`part_matches.for_run` `ai_args`; `fea_driver.plan_step_ai`; `fea_ai.decide` `kept`, checked again on the run's own part by the same rules, source "plan step"), so the run plans with the same decision; when the AI step gives no decision then, the count is said as a floor ("to at least about ..."). THE REPLY NEVER WAITS FOR THE COST: the reading is kept and answered first (`cost_pending` on the card's view); the cost lands after it (`part_matches.keep_cost`, only while the notes are the ones it read - a stale count is never kept) and its line is its own message after the reply (`agents/intake/plan_reply.count_owed`: with the send's `then` when it has already landed, else with the card's poll as `count_line`; once per reading, `part_matches.COUNT_SAID`); the reply itself no longer says the count. A size typed with a face picked is answered at once again. "Before" is the reading before this one's "after", or, when that cost had not landed, planned from what that reading was read with (kept with it as `fea_inputs`). AMENDED 126 (the knuckle, 27 Nov: the first message's count line was never said - its cost read "before": null - because the reading before it had been made before the stage sized the part and kept nothing it was read with, and "before" answered "not known"): EVERY reading of an FEA part keeps the notes it was read with (`fea_inputs`), sized or not, and a reading kept without them is planned from its parts reading with the team's standard alone - "before" is never "not known", so the first requirement's line is always said. AMENDED 127 (the knuckle, 27 Nov: the stage card said "about 883,835 elements" and the first count line "from about 1.1 million"): ONE COUNT FOR "GO NOW". The card's count was the stage's sizing without the team's standard and the AI step's decision, while a run started with nothing typed plans with both (a part of the knuckle's shape: the sizing 100,346, such a run's "Estimated before meshing" 126,575). Once the stage has sized the part, the count for nothing typed is planned on the geometry worker by the same function as the first line's "from" (`plan_cost.stand` -> `plan_cost.planned`; asked for by the check's own route, once a while, `part_matches.enqueue_stand`), kept beside the stage's plans (`fea_plan_options.STAND_FILE`, for that sizing and that team standard), and shown on the card's full-rules option (`fea_check.plan_view`, the facts' `stand`); the first line's "from" is that count; a run started with nothing typed uses the AI step's answer it was planned with (`fea_driver.plan_step_ai`, checked again), so it prints it as "Estimated before meshing". The part as measured is kept on the worker between costings of the same upload (`plan_cost._measured`): the first line came 74 s after its reply. AMENDED 128 (the knuckle, 27 Nov: the card updated to "about 866,295" but the first line said "from about 1.1 million"): the card's count is the first line's "from" WHENEVER THE PLAN BEFORE THE MESSAGE HAD NOTHING TYPED (`plan_cost.nothing_typed`: no reading before, or one whose words set nothing to cost) - not only with no reading before. On the site a reading made before the stage was sized came first, so "before" was planned again and its AI step asked anew, on an intake request that by then held the typed words: a different question, a different answer. A run started with nothing typed plans with the card's AI answer, so the card's count is what it prints. With nothing to choose, the card's reason follows the machine its count names (`fea_plan_options.nothing_to_choose`: "fits in the standard machine's memory ... it runs on a faster machine (16 cores) to finish sooner"). |

## STD: the team's meshing standard (settings)

Areen, 11 Nov: a team meshes a standard way - a quality criteria list it always uses, a default element size, units,
a solver - and should not have to type it every run. The console's Meshing settings hold ONE standard per
organisation, written as the notes are written (`engines/meshing_standard.py`; storage `organization_meshing_standards`,
migration 0020; routes GET/PUT `/api/v1/organization/meshing-standard`). Settings are the team's standing
requirements; the notes are this run's; the notes win, field by field.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| STD-1 | in force | **One reader, read once.** The standard is read by NOTE-7's own prompt, tool and checks, shown to the model as a note of its own ("the team's meshing standard, from settings") with no route in the question, so the reading made when it is SAVED serves every run: the model's answer and the key of the question are kept with the words, and a run re-checks that answer by the rules with no model call. Changed words or a changed question (a new prompt or tool): the run reads it again through the builder route and the decision cache, with NOTE-9's fallbacks. A model down at save time still saves the words (each run reads them). The run is dispatched with the standard as it stood at approval (a dispute re-run keeps its parent's). A standard that names a solver file every FEA run delivers (Nastran, Abaqus, CalculiX, Ansys) is told as delivered, never "not applied"; its words for one part (a size in one named place: a place belongs to one part) or shell or solid are told as not applied ("put ... in a run's notes"). **Its element or cell count is the default count of every run** (rulebook 95): on a solid and a shell it aims the plan through the path a run's notes use (`engines/fea_driver.element_target` / `settings_count` -> PLAN-fea-27, SHMESH-23, the surface file's SURF-11), on the flow route the engines' own count (CFDREQ-3), with the same meaning (about, at most, at least, a range) and the same tell-back ("You asked for at most 2M elements (from your team's settings); ..."). A count in the run's own notes, of any kind, takes every count of the standard out for that run (STD-2). Where an engine cannot take a count it says so, as for the notes (gmsh on a flow mesh). |
| STD-2 | in force | **Rules < settings < notes, field by field** (`engines/meshing_standard.merge`, the one merge every route calls where it read the notes). A field is a size of one kind, one element-kind choice (quads or triangles or a share of triangles; the order), a quality limit per measure, element shape and direction, and on the flow route each of its own fields and each named place. What the run's notes set wins for that run; every other field of the standard stays. The notes' lengths with no unit take the unit the standard names; an edge length written as a multiple of the global size follows the global size in force; where a value of the standard contradicts one of the notes, the notes' is kept and the standard's told as not applied. Every row of the set says where it came from: said before meshing ("From your settings: ..." / "From your notes: ..."), on each asked-against-delivered row ("Your settings ask: ...", "(from your settings)"), and in the closing line ("From your settings and notes: ..."). A part the notes size keeps its size; the standard's global size covers the rest (PLAN-fea-33). On the flow route (NOTE-10, CFDREQ-1) the CFD reading step (`cfd_requirements.read_for_attempt`) makes the same call: every CFD engine's set is the merged one, the flow report's rows carry where each came from and its closing line is led the same way; the flow fields' own rules hold across the two sources (no boundary layers against a layer setting, a first layer not thinner than the stack, a cell floor not under the ceiling, a count outside the range: the notes' kept, the standard's told), and the standard's unit reaches the flow lengths (the size at the walls, a layer's first height and stack, a size in one named place). |
| STD-3 | in force | **Nothing in either, nothing changes.** With no standard the notes' own section is returned untouched: every plan, mesh and record is byte for byte the run before the standard existed (NOTE-9 then holds as before). |
| STD-4 | in force | **The settings switch and the stage on a shell's elements.** The Meshing settings page has one switch beside its box, "Shell elements: Quads | Triangles" (Quads by default; `organization_meshing_standards.shell_elements`, 0021), the settings' own value of the shell element kind - **once the user SET it** (rulebook 95: `organization_meshing_standards.shell_elements_set`, 0024, set when the switch is changed on the page; Triangles is never the default, so always set): Triangles is triangles only; Quads chosen sets aside a "triangles only" the standard's words gave (told) and keeps a "quads only" or a share of triangles (`engines/meshing_standard.switched`, `switch_chosen`). A switch left at its default sets nothing aside: "tria mesh only" typed in the box is built, and the stage starts on the kind the words name, said as the settings'. **A row the route cannot take is never said before meshing as applied**: boundary layers and a size at the walls on a solid or a shell, a control every lane of the route declares not here (files in millimetres) are said "Not for this mesh (your settings): 5 boundary layers - a solid or shell mesh has no boundary layers or wall cells; they are a CFD setting." - the reason the result's row gives (`mesh_requirements.not_on_route`). Then STD-2 as before - the notes win - and last the stage (`engines/meshing_standard.stage`): a click on the stage's Quads | Triangles is the last word for that run, recorded with source "stage" (Triangles: triangles only; Quads: quad-dominant - only a "triangles only" gives way). One field, kind.shell; the result says which and where from: "Triangles, from your settings.", "Triangles, from your notes.", "Quads, chosen on the stage.", "Quads, the default." A switch on Quads with no words is no standard (STD-3). |
| STD-5 | in force | **A saved standard applies to the kinds of mesh it is written for** (rulebook 73, `engines/meshing_standard.merge`, `_kinds_of`, `_fits`; the reader's `applies_to`). When the standard is read, the AI tags the list as a whole and each line that differs from it with the kinds of mesh it speaks to - shell, solid, cfd, any - and the rules check each line's quote is the user's. A structural quality list (warpage, quad and tria angles, Jacobian, element length rules) is shell and solid, its global size too: on a flow mesh none of it applies, and the run is the run with no standard - every engine's dictionaries byte for byte - told once before meshing and with the result: "Your saved meshing standard is written for shell and solid meshes; it was not applied to this flow mesh." A list written for CFD is likewise not applied to a solid or shell mesh. A general line ("export in mm", "at most 2 million cells or elements") applies to all; a mixed list splits line by line, and the lines left out are named. The job's own notes always apply as typed. A standard read before the tag existed applies as it always did. (m483 with the saved list on: a 10-line structural list set CFD cell sizes - wall level 3 to 1 - and judged their aspect ratio.)  Each saved line is listed once in that line (amended 123: the C5 knuckle's result listed "3 boundary layers, growth 1.2" twice, as a setting set aside and as words for flow meshes; lines the same but for spacing, case, quote marks and a closing full stop are one). |
| STD-6 | in force | **The standard's design intent reaches the planning steps as guidance** (rulebook 95, `engines/meshing_standard.guidance_notes`, `guidance_split`, `contracts/user_notes.SOURCE_TEAM`). A line of the saved standard that is no setting - what the reader files as no mesh setting of ours ("we care about fatigue at the fillets") or as not a mesh setting at all ("this goes into a crash model", "keep run time low") - is handed to the run's AI steps the way the run's notes are (NOTE-2): the FEA plan's AI step (`engines/fea_ai.brief`), the builder (`agents/builder/attempt`: its prompt and the attempt's `user_notes.txt`, which the snappy planner reads) and the reviewer as context only (NOTE-3: never a check). It is one note of its own, labelled "your team's saved meshing settings - guidance for every run; where this run's own notes below say otherwise, the run's notes win", put BEFORE the run's notes. Only the lines for the run's kind of mesh (STD-5): a "For CFD meshes:" line is not handed to an FEA run and is named in STD-5's line. The lines come from the reading kept at save time, else the decision cache's saved answer; no model is asked for them. Told in the three places lines are told: before meshing and with the result `From your settings, kept as guidance for the mesh planning: "...".` (with the reader's hint when it has one, "(a time limit is not a setting yet ...)"), on the settings page `Kept as guidance for the mesh planning: "...".` - never "applied", never "no mesh setting of ours matches it yet". A standard with no such line, or none: every prompt, plan and record byte for byte as before (STD-3). The standard's own reader never sees this note (its question, and so the key of a kept reading, is unchanged). Test: `tests/unit/engines/test_standard_guidance.py`. |

## CFDREQ: the user's requirements on a flow (CFD) mesh

The notes' typed set (NOTE-7, NOTE-10) on every CFD engine (`engines/cfd_requirements.py`, rulebook 63).

| ID | State | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| CFDREQ-1 | in force | **One seam.** The builder reads the notes once per flow attempt, before any engine plans (`agents/builder/invoke._read_flow_requirements` -> `cfd_requirements.read_for_attempt`, route "flow"), keeps the reading in the workspace and says it ("From your notes: ..."). Every CFD engine reads the set through `cfd_requirements.requirements_for(workspace)` (an `Ask`) where its own plan is made: snappyHexMesh's external and internal drivers, cfMesh's meshDict writer, the multi-region mesher's configure step, VMTK's configure step, the gmsh flow driver and the snap-grid driver. Each engine records what it did with each requirement beside the case (`cfd_requirements_plan.json`) and says it before meshing ("On this mesher, ..."); a requirement it has no setting for is "not applied on this mesher", told, never silently dropped. **Netgen and the wrap rung read it too** (rulebook 68): Netgen's plan (`engines/netgen/driver.plan_case` -> `cfd_requirements.netgen_inputs`) takes a size at the walls, a global size as a band, the smallest and largest edge as clamps on its passage-sized edges, a cell count as its budget (a target also refines toward it, a count under the floor coarsens below it, said), the layer count, first layer or y+ (the widest opening as the reference length), stack and growth; a growth ratio and a size in one named place are not settings it has, said. A mesh built on a thinner rung of its ladder says so on the layer rows (`netgen_built`). On the CFD wrap rung (LAD-cfd-5) each engine applies the notes to the wrapped surface as to any surface, and every size row says so with the wrap's distance from the CAD (`wrap_rows`); on its last step (gmsh filling the wrap's own triangles) a size at the wall is the nearest - the wall keeps the wrap's triangles - and no wall layers are built, said. The wrap's own distance is set by the geometry, never by the notes. |
| CFDREQ-2 | in force | **Sizes are snapped to what the engine builds, and the size built is said.** snappyHexMesh: a global (or largest) size is the background cell, the box cut in whole cubic cells and the levels re-counted on it so the wall keeps its absolute size; a size at the walls (or the body's own size in a named place) is the wall's level, round(log2(background / size)) - with no background size of the user's, the background is nudged (under 1.42 times either way) to the wall size times a whole power of two, so the walls land on the size asked; the smallest cell caps every level; a size on a named region or opening is that region's level; the wake's size or "finer" is its levels under the wall; a growth rate is nCellsBetweenLevels = ceil(ln 2 / ln rate). cfMesh: maxCellSize, the wall's localRefinement cellSize (cfMesh lands on max / 2^n: a wall size alone nudges the background so the walls land on it; with a background size too, the nearest halving, said), a wake box held to a quarter of the budget, a named patch's own cellSize, a wake box along +x, the smallest cell as a floor on every refinement. The multi-region mesher: the background cell and the regions' levels. VMTK: clamps on its radius-adaptive edge length. gmsh flow: the body / fill element size, its smallest and largest. The snap grid: its background cell and growth. VALIDITY IS NEVER TRADED: snappy's body-sealing level, an internal passage's 8 cells across, an opening's level that keeps it open, a thin region's level that keeps its zone - a size past them is the nearest that holds, said. Our accuracy rules give way (a coarser size than our rule is built, and said "results may be less exact"). A background past half the largest machine is the nearest that fits, said. |
| CFDREQ-3 | in force | **A cell count the user sets wins over ours, up to the largest machine** (Areen, 14 Nov: "they specify whatever they want, it's up to them completely"): on snappyHexMesh and cfMesh a count past the standard machine's `settings/policy.CELL_HARD_LIMIT` is planned as asked and runs on a bigger machine (RUN-9), said in its row ("... more than our standard machine is sized for, so it is meshed on a bigger one"); the other engines hold it to the standard machine, said. The user's count is the plan's limit too: our gap boxes (PLAN-cfd-1) never raise a budget past it. A ceiling or a target is the engine's budget (snappy's maxGlobalCells and the size brake's plan, the multi-region mesher's, VMTK's, the snap grid's; cfMesh coarsens the wall one octree level at a time until the case's own estimate fits); a floor raises ours. Past the largest machine it is the nearest, said. A budget of ours gives way to the user's sizes (snappy raises its own to what they need, up to the largest machine, with a note); the user's own count comes first over their sizes, said. gmsh's flow mesher sizes by element size only: a count there is measured and told. |
| CFDREQ-4 | in force | **Boundary layers as asked.** The count replaces the planner's (its 3-5 / 3-6 is ours); a tight non-orthogonality of the user's is held while layers are added, and when that takes layers back the layers row says so; snappyHexMesh takes a first layer height, a stack thickness or both in absolute sizes (relativeSizes false; minThickness a tenth of the thinnest layer, minVol under the thinnest layer cell), a growth as expansionRatio; cfMesh takes the count, thicknessRatio and maxFirstLayerThickness (no stack thickness: told); VMTK the count and the growth (sublayer ratio; absolute thicknesses: told); gmsh flow and the snap grid have no prism layers: told. A y+ is a first layer height when the flow's numbers are known: Re = U L / nu (air 1.516e-5, water 1.004e-6 m²/s at 20 °C, or the user's), Cf = 0.026 Re^(-1/7), y = y+ nu / (U sqrt(Cf / 2)) - or from a Reynolds number and a length alone; L is the notes' length, else the reference length the user confirmed, else the body's (or bore's) length, said. With a number missing nothing new is asked: the missing one is named and the layers keep our sizing. A first layer the user gave is used as given. |
| CFDREQ-5 | in force | **Quality limits tighter than ours go to the mesher where it has the setting.** snappyHexMesh's meshQualityControls take a tighter maxNonOrtho (the layer stage's relaxed bar 2 degrees under it), maxInternalSkewness (and maxBoundarySkewness, and the relaxed one), minDeterminant and minVol; ours stay where they are tighter (said). cfMesh: a non-orthogonality under its own edge (75) sends a mesh past it through its optimiser and a protective re-mesh (`engines/cfmesh/rescue.near_edge`). Every other limit is measured on the mesh, never promised. |

The closing message carries one line ("From your notes: ..."). |

The plan now carries the answer as ONE fixed sentence ("Fluid: water at 20 °C at 1 m/s at the inlet. Near the walls: a resolved wall (y⁺ ≈ 1); the first layer is sized for y⁺ ≈ 1 at that speed."), written and read back by the same module; the run reads it (`cfd_requirements.with_conditions`, in `read_for_attempt`) into the same set a y+ typed in the notes makes: a resolved wall is y+ 1, wall functions y+ 50 (inside their 30-300 band, with room for slower regions); the speed in m/s (km/h, mph, knots; a Mach number for air at 343.2 m/s; a volume or mass flow through the one inlet whose size is known); air or water give the viscosity (CFDREQ-4). | CFDREQ-13 | in force | **The wall treatment sizes the first layer, fitted to the wall cells** (rulebook 75; `engines/cfd_requirements.wall_fit`, `friction`, `y_plus_height`, `cfmesh_layer_fit`, `_vmtk_wall`). The wall treatment the user accepted ("OK" on the proposed conditions: wall functions) or chose (a resolved wall) is a y+ target. **Which y+:** from y+ 5 up (wall functions' 50, a typed 30) it is the first cell's CENTRE - the y+ the solver reports (OpenFOAM `yPlus`, Fluent) - so the first layer is twice y+ nu / u_tau high: a height at y+ 30 would put the centre at 15, in the buffer layer. Under 5 (a resolved wall's y+ 1, typed or chosen) it is the first cell's full height, as before, so its centre sits at about half: the safe side for a resolved wall on an estimate good to a factor of two. Every told line gives the first layer's height and the y+ expected at the cell centre. u_tau comes from the run's own conditions (fluid, speed) and a length the code measures: a flow through a part takes its Reynolds number on the passage's hydraulic diameter (the inlet the user confirmed) and the smooth-pipe friction factor (Blasius below Re 1e5, Haaland above); a flow round a body takes the body's length and the flat plate (Cf = 0.026 Re^-1/7). A y+, first layer height or layer count the user typed wins over all of it (CFDREQ-4, 8). The y+ row says which wall treatment was built, its first layer, the layers and the y+ expected for the fluid and speed assumed - applied when it is the one asked, the nearest with why when the wall was resolved instead. It is sized on its boundary layer a fifth of the way along the body (5 x / sqrt(Re_x) at x = 0.2 L, 0.45 of the thickness at its end; the layer only thickens behind it, so the cells across it there are across it, or more, on the 80% of the length behind). On snappyHexMesh, with the wall cells known: the fewest layers, from the thickest first layer, that put BL_CELLS (10) across that layer, the layers under its edge and the core cells between the stack and the edge counted - up to LAMINAR_LAYERS_KEPT (8) layers while the core cells make up the rest, past it a stack of at most BL_CELLS layers that fills the layer; growth LAMINAR_GROWTH (1.2) unless the user gave one (or the plan's is lower); the top layer never past FIRST_CAP_REL of the wall cell, the stack never past a tight wall's cap or the layer budget; a count of the user's stands (the cells it reaches are said). Elsewhere (cfMesh, Netgen) the first layer is that of BL_CELLS layers filling it. The bridge (CFDREQ-9) and the thick-side fit do not resize this stack. The y+ row says it in plain words (the flow is laminar at this speed, wall functions need a turbulent one, so the wall is resolved; the layer's thickness, the layers, the first layer and the cells across) and is judged on the built mesh: from every face of the body's walls (never the ground plane the domain lays, CFDREQ-19; rulebook 93) cell after cell straight out, layer and core cells alike, the cells under that thickness - met when 10 or more (to the nearest cell, 9.5) stand on BL_WALL_SHARE (80%) of the wall, else missed with the share and the median; uncounted (no polyMesh or past the census limit): judged as before (CFDREQ-6/14). Before, a y+ 1 first layer (0.026-0.034 mm) took 8-16 layers; snappyHexMesh cut the stacks of 9 or more short on 32 of 46 bodies (layer coverage 9 -> 38 misses, CFD board batch 5 -> 6), and the row judged the first cell against 1.5x a 0.03 mm plan, not the boundary layer the rule promises. Wall cells thicker than the layer (most bodies at 10 m/s) leave the core no room inside it, so those take 10 layers: more than the 8 the board shows snappyHexMesh keeping, from first layers 1-3x thicker than before - the cloud sample says whether it holds. A turbulent body (CFDREQ-17's thick case) keeps y+ 1. |
| CFDREQ-23 | in force | **A laminar stack gets finer wall cells when the mesh can afford them** (rulebook 84; `engines/cfd_requirements.laminar_band`, `band_regions`, `LAMINAR_BAND_LEVELS`, `LAMINAR_BAND_LAYERS`, `LAMINAR_BAND_GRADING`, `_snappy_layers`, `_bl_cells_row`; `engines/snappy/snappy_runner.render_snappy_case`). On snappyHexMesh a CFDREQ-22 stack keeps the case's nBufferCellsNoExtrude 0 (rulebook 93). The -1 tried for it ("the stack ends in one step"; the default steps the layer count down one layer per ring of faces round every point not extruded, and the first cloud sample extlam_ef6c20fc extruded 97-99% of each wall but stood 10 cells across the layer on 61-79% of it) made it worse on the 12-part external sample (extlam2_s12_8f2765ba): the wall with 10 across fell on 6 parts (79 -> 2%, 78 -> 35%, 61 -> 0%, 73 -> 0%, 65 -> 0%, 75 -> 53%), rose about a point on 2, and the runs were 2-10x slower (8 -> 83 min, 10 -> 144, 18 -> 250 min with no mesh). On the external body, when the plan on the wall cells needs more than LAMINAR_BAND_LAYERS (6) layers and no count is the user's: a distance band as deep as the layer, 1 or LAMINAR_BAND_LEVELS (2) levels finer than the wall cells (the coarsest that does it), with a stack of at most 6 layers that reaches BL_CELLS counting the band's core cells (first layer never thinner than the plan without the band). It is built only when its cells (band volume over its cells times LAMINAR_BAND_GRADING, plus its layer cells, less the cells and layers there now) are no more than the room the plan gives a resolved wall's layers (LAYER_BRIDGE_ROOM, half the pass's budget); then the castellation ceiling and the brake's plan are raised by them. Else the stack on the wall cells stands and, if the row is missed, it says what finer cells would cost against that room (or that two levels finer would not do it). Under a tight wall's cap (CFDREQ-9) the laminar stack's first layer may thin to LAMINAR_CAP_THIN (0.25) of its floor before the cap cuts layers (6b8a01ca cut layers at the floor itself and left abc 00021335 6 layers and 10 cells across on 7% of its wall, extlam2_r3c_6b8a01ca; 9 layers from 0.48 of the floor had held them on all of it); a cap under one such layer is still set aside. The bar (10 across on 80% of the wall) is unchanged. On the sample the band is priced at about 30-35 M cells on the two bodies with the finest wall cells and is out of reach within two levels on the other seven laminar bodies, so it is built on none of them. |
| CFDREQ-24 | in force | **An internal flow's wall-function layer stands on cells that suit it, and the y+ row reads the first cell it built** (rulebook 85; `engines/cfd_requirements.wall_function_keep_cell`, `WALL_FUNCTION_JUMP`, `_y_plus_row`; `cad/thin_features.thin_refinement_boxes` `keep_cell_m`; `engines/snappy/drivers` internal pass; `engines/layer_census.census` `area_first_at_plan`, `area_under_jump`, `next_over_first`). In wall-function mode (the conditions' y+, accepted or chosen, or a y+ of 30 or more typed) on snappyHexMesh, a thin feature's local refinement no longer puts the wall's cells far under the first layer: where two cells across the thinnest feature would make them thinner than the layer over WALL_FUNCTION_JUMP (2), the feature gets the fewest levels that still put one cell across it (castellation keeps a feature at least a cell thick) or as many as keep the cells at that size, whichever is more; a feature thinner than that keeps the cells it needs, and the run's thin-feature note says the layer there stands under thinner cells. Before, a 2.2-3.1 mm feature under 3.0-3.4 mm wall cells took +2 levels over boxes covering most of the part, so the 1.92 mm y+ 50 layer stood under 0.35 mm cells (next cell / first cell 0.18, a five-times jump going away from the wall) on 00122027, 00145914 and 00172105 (yplab_e11_7821cb5b): now +1, 1.5-1.7 mm cells. The census, with a planned first layer, reads every wall face whose first cell is at the planned height (within PLAN_FIRST_TOL either way, far face parallel) with the cell next out: under a cell more than GROWTH_MAX (2) times thinner it stands under a jump. The y+ row's wall share is CFDREQ-21's layers at plan plus the first cell at the planned height with no such jump, whatever its shape (a layer taller than its wall faces are wide read 'layers on 0% of the wall' while its y+ was 47); the share under a jump is said with the ratio, and a first cell taller than its faces are wide under a jump is no part of the share, so such a wall is still a miss, for its true reason. The count of layers by shape (CFDREQ-14, 21) is unchanged. |

Typed (the conditions step's choice of wall functions, or a y+ of 30 or more in the notes): CFDREQ-18 may lower the cells across the bore under WALL_FUNCTION_MIN_CELLS_ACROSS (13), as far as the asked first layer needs, or - where that layer is past a fifth of the bore's radius - the thickest that is not, when its centre is still at y+ 30 or more; never under wall_function_hard_cells (snappyHexMesh 6, cfMesh 5: the count at which the engine's thickest first layer is a fifth of the radius, the edge of the log layer a wall function models). | CFDREQ-28 | in force | **An external body's layer stack kept on too little of the wall moves the run to the next engine, and cfMesh refines the external wake** (rulebook 112, amended from 94; `engines/cfd_requirements.stack_lost`, `STACK_KEPT_PCT`; `engines/snappy/flow_gates` and `engines/cfmesh/flow_gates` gate `layer_stack`; `pipeline/engine_fallback` `GATE_LEVERS`, `DELIVERABLE_GATES`, `deliverable_refusal`, `gate_distance`; `engines/cfmesh/cfmesh_runner._external_wake_request`, `render_cfmesh_case` `wake`; `engines/cfmesh/spec` `wake_refinement`). On an external flow (the workspace's `flow_topology`), when the plan's y+ row lays a stack (any wall treatment) and the built wall carries it on less than the layer row's own share (80%, `_layers_row`), the mesh is refused with no lever on that engine: snappyHexMesh drops a layer wherever it would fold a cell, and its layer-stage levers (CFDREQ-22's band, CFDREQ-23's cap, the buffer cells) did not keep the stack - CFD board batch 7, external, by the product's own verdict: 50 of the 62 misses had the layer row missed, 45 of them laminar bodies (10 layers on 0-80% of the wall). The ladder moves the run to the next engine that delivers the same mesh; cfMesh splits its wall cell into the layers, and its external case now refines the wake from the shared wake plan (`engines/wake_region.plan_wake`, PLAN-cfd-7, on cfMesh's own background cell and wall level, as objectRefinements costed with the case, recorded in `wake_refinement.json` and the quality record as snappyHexMesh's is), so it is the same mesh as an external snappyHexMesh plan. cfMesh judges the same gate in the same place; a refused mesh is delivered as concerns with its share said, and of two refused meshes the one nearer the bar (80% less its coverage, LAD-cfd-9) is delivered. Internal flow is not judged here (CFDREQ-27; cfMesh is its home engine). A ground plane cfMesh cannot lay stays with snappyHexMesh (cfMesh's admission refuses it), as before. **Amended in rulebook 112: the run ends on the better mesh, and a kept mesh is always handed back** (`engines/resolution_policy` `ENGINE_BETTER_OF_TWO`, `verdict_standing`, `standing_order`, `MISS_TIE`, `engine_choice`, `choice_line`, `near_misses`, `GATES_THAT_ARE_ROWS`, `_beats`/`deliverable_sound`; `pipeline/graph.route_after_executor`, `node_deliver_sound`; `contracts/native_time` `HANDBACK_RULE`, `HANDBACK_RESERVE_S`, `hold_for_handback`, `handback_cap`, `wall_for`, `run_off_the_wall`; `agents/builder/agent._hold_for_handback`; `application/final_result._near_misses`; `engines/cfd_requirements.STACK_KEPT_PCT` is read from the verdict's own layer row). (a) A mesh kept as a fallback is always handed back when the next engine fails, is refused or runs out of time, in any engine order and on internal flows too: an attempt that follows a kept mesh gets its native runs capped at the run's anchored deadline less HANDBACK_RESERVE_S (1,800 s; progress does not move this time on - a mesh is already in hand), a run stopped there is not moved to a bigger machine (RUN-5), and a guard trip with a kept mesh delivers it instead of a replay (TIME-3). Why: CFD board batch 8, abc 00025560 - the snappy mesh refused at 0% layer cover was kept, cfMesh ran into the lab's wall clock (251 min) on an inverted-faces rescue, and nothing was handed back. (b) When the run holds sound meshes from two engines it ends on the better by the product's own measured verdict (engines/verdict_limits rows, contracts/decision_record.verdict): pass beats concerns beats anything else; of two with concerns, the one missing fewer rows; then the one whose worst missed row is missed by less, as a share of that row's limit (MISS_TIE, 0.01 apart); a tie keeps the first mesh, which is cheaper. The mesh not chosen and why is told in one plain line on the result (the ladder's `delivered_from`). A layer-stack refusal is the layer row itself, never a second missed requirement (requirement_near_misses). Meshes of one engine keep LAD-cfd-3/8/9's order. Why: batch 8 moved 48 runs; 20 ended on cfMesh with 17 concerns (non-orthogonality 10) because a mesh no gate refused always beat the refused snappy one. The gate and the verdict read one measured cover (the manifest's layer_coverage_pct) against one bar: the four batch 7 passes it moved (00011034, 00039049, 00045950, 00106604) were refused on batch 8 snappy meshes whose cover measured under 80% (00045950 79.2%, 00106604 78.5%; batch 7 96.0% and 86.8%), so the verdict read them as concerns too - a mesh the verdict passes on its layers is never refused by this gate. |

Cell heights do not shrink into a jump going away from the wall. snappyHexMesh makes room for a layer by pushing the snapped wall into the cell the layer stands on, so the cell above is about that cell less the layer: the keep cell (the smallest cell a local refinement may put under a wall-function first layer) is now the layer over FIRST_CAP_REL (0.6, the share the wall cell is sized to give it; the cell above is then 0.67 of the layer), not the layer over WALL_FUNCTION_JUMP (2), which let a 1.57 mm layer stand on 1.31 mm cells. And a thin-spot box whose feature is still thinner than the cells the levels reach (not one cell across it, so the refinement captures nothing) is not refined where those cells would be under the keep cell; the run's thin-feature note says which spots were left and why. A box whose feature the levels capture is refined as before (CFDREQ-24). Measured on batch 7 (7d3a55dd): 00157704 (a 0.017 mm reading boxed over the whole part, +1) and 00137655 (0.0077 mm, +2) had their layers under a cell more than twice thinner on 61% and 63% of the wall. NOT covered: a real feature the levels do capture under a thicker layer (00140625: 2.24 mm, +1 to 1.65 mm cells under a 1.93 mm layer, 78%), and cfMesh's single wall-function layer, which is ~0.63 of its wall cell whatever maxFirstLayerThickness says, so the cell above is the rest of a cut boundary cell (00180172, 00188075: 14-25% of the wall). |

Every cfMesh meshDict with layers writes the layer optimiser's parameters in full: nSmoothNormals 3, maxNumIterations 5, featureSizeFactor 0.4, reCalculateNormals 1, relThicknessTol 0.02. Writing the optimiser's block at its documented defaults already moves the single layer (cfMesh's built-in defaults are not the documented ones); relThicknessTol is the setting that holds it to the plan. Tighter is not safe: the optimiser evens the layer towards its thinnest spot, and at 0.01 / 0.005 the first cell fell to 0.47 / 0.24 of the plan on 00142033 and 0.58 / 0.29 on 00178392 (a y+ under the band). featureSizeFactor 0.1-0.4, maxNumIterations 1-20 and nSmoothNormals 3-5 moved nothing. When LAMINAR_LAYERS_KEPT (8) layers from the floor do not put BL_CELLS across the laminar boundary layer, the first layer of an 8-layer stack is thinned, down to LAMINAR_CAP_THIN (0.25) of the floor as a tight wall's cap does, so the wall cells inside the layer make up the rest; the thickest such first layer is used. Where the halved stack was whole it still gave 10.3-11 cells across; the loss is the stacks snappyHexMesh cut short. Shallower stacks keep their absolute heights. **Amended in rulebook 113 (ENGINEER 36): in metres first, on each face's own cells on the retry** (`cfd_requirements.RELATIVE_ON_RETRY_KEY`, `_mark_relative_on_retry`, `relative_on_retry`, `snappy_dict(relative_now=)`; `engines/gate_facts.relative_stack_next`; `engines/snappy/flow_gates` `layer_stack` fact `relative_next`; `pipeline/engine_fallback.lever_bears`, `LAYER_STACK_RELATIVE_LEVER`). A stack this rule would write relative is written in metres on the run's first snappyHexMesh attempt (the batch 7 dictionary), marked /* relativeOnRetry <m>; */ (no reader takes it for a height). When CFDREQ-28's layer-stack gate refuses that mesh, the refusal says so (`relative_next`, kept in gate_facts.json) and snappyHexMesh's next attempt writes the same plan relative, as above - before the next engine, even when it is the run's last regular attempt (`engine_fallback.decide`); only that attempt's refusal moves the run to the next engine, when an attempt is left. Measured why the order (testlab-5, e36ext_c2e36_8f115573_v15, the next engine first): 00008746, 00039088 and 00058385 went from the refused metres stack (66.5 / 79.7 / 77.3%) straight to cfMesh and ended concerns, where the relative stack passed them on the 4f823f8e tree (85.6 / 80.6 / 88.2%). Of two refused meshes the one nearer the bar is kept (LAD-cfd-9), and against another engine's mesh CFDREQ-28 (b) chooses. A typed first height, y+ or stack is the same on the plain faces either way and is told as before; on the relative attempt its row also says what finer faces got. Measured why (kept meshes of 00011034 and 00039049, batch 7 tree in metres against the 4f823f8e tree relative; the dictionaries differ only in relativeSizes, firstLayerThickness as a share and minVol / 512; 10 layers, a stack 1.0 of the plain wall cell deep): layer cover 81.5 -> 79.8% and 85.2 -> 79.5% (snappyHexMesh's mean layers 8.15 -> 7.98 and 8.52 -> 7.95, at the same faces extruded, 98.2 -> 98.9% and 98.1 -> 98.6%). By face: the faces one level finer than the plain cell (8-13% of the wall) kept the full 10 layers on 57-59% of them in metres and on 27-35% relative; plain faces 94.5 / 89.4% -> 90.0 / 84.2%. Stack depth does not tell these parts from the ones relative helped (00008746, 00039088: also about 1.0 deep), so the built mesh decides; batch 8 lost four batch 7 passes this way (00045950 96.0 -> 79.2%, 00106604 86.8 -> 78.5%). **Amended in rulebook 116 (ENGINEER 39): a layers-only relative retry, built and switched off; the retry's cell budget priced once** (`engines/snapped_keep`: `LAYERS_ONLY_RETRY`, `wants_keep`, `snap_key`, `stage_dict`, `promote_phases`, `adopt`, `layers_only_ready`, `settle`, `drop`, `KEEP_DIR`, `LAYERS_ONLY_FILE`, `REUSE_FILE`; `engines/snappy/native._run_snappy_local`; `engines/snappy/drivers._say_layer_reuse`, `_native_payload_members`, `_PLAN_MAX_CELLS`, `_inherit_durable_plan_fields`; `engines/return_scope.scope_return`; `pipeline/executor` after the gates; `pipeline/graph.node_deliver_sound`). The relative retry rebuilds the whole mesh, as before (LAYERS_ONLY_RETRY False). Switched on, a parallel attempt marked relativeOnRetry runs its one snappyHexMesh without -overwrite and writing binary; the layered phase (processor*/3) is reconstructed as before and the snapped phase (processor*/2) is kept aside (.snapped_mesh, with its key and file list) until the attempt's gates are done; the relative attempt takes it over only when its castellation and snapping inputs (the background box, the dictionary without its stage switches, addLayersControls and minVol, the surfaces) and its rank count match, and runs snappyHexMesh with castellatedMesh and snap off; otherwise it rebuilds in full, and snapped_reuse.json, the log and the why record (stage snappy_layer_reuse) say which path ran. The kept mesh is dropped after the gates (unless they refused the stack with relative_next), from an attempt delivered as the sound mesh, and first when the archive home is over its ceiling. Measured why it is off (testlab-4 and -5, tree a6639f89, labels e39main_a6639f89_v15 / e39full_a6639f89_v15, the product's own verdict; the 24 relative-retry parts and 13 passes of engineer 37's external run): it is not the full retry's mesh. 00008746 kept its stack on 86.0% of the wall (full retry 85.6%) but at max non-orthogonality 76.7 (bar 70) and lost its pass; on 00087385 and 00054680 the layers-only pass folded cells (00087385: 12 wall spots, incorrectly oriented faces) and the run rebuilt in full after it (retry 32.4 and 20.1 min, against 21.9 and 15.4 rebuilt). Elsewhere it matched: 00058385 pass 87.8% (full 88.2%), 00039088 pass 80.5% (80.6%), 00067838 79.86% (79.84%), 00117623 the same mesh; and it saved time: on testlab-4 (4 jobs at once, as engineer 37) 17 parts 512 min against 622 with the same verdicts; the 6 side-by-side parts 161 min against 233 rebuilt. Splitting the first attempt instead (layers in a second run on the read-back snapped mesh) folded cells on parts the one run meshes cleanly (00058385: three passes layer-fatal against a clean pass), so the layers are added in the one run. Off, nothing is kept and the run is today's; the full-retry arm reproduced engineer 37's meshes exactly on its 6 parts (same cells, cover and verdict). A retry also inherits the plan's own cell budget (_PLAN_MAX_CELLS), so the near wake, the near-body box and the gap boxes are priced into it once: each attempt added them again on top of the raised budget (00058385: 4,000,000 -> 4,345,373 -> 4,690,746); on the 6 parts measured the retry mesh did not change with it. |

The values are the STORED ones - the plan's conditions sentence read back the way the run reads it (`flow_conditions.read`) - never the model's reading of the words. A part of the words that was not taken is said: a temperature the mesh is not sized for (it is sized for air or water at 20 °C), a number that is no condition we read ("40" with no unit), a second speed, and a fluid or speed the first layer cannot be sized from (CFDREQ-8's reason). A guard checks the model's sentence: the stored speed as stored, the fluid and its temperature, the wall treatment taken (never the other one), no number the facts do not hold, every part not taken said as not used, no question, no plan line, no rule id, no exclamation, at most 300 characters; refused, failed or slow, the code's own sentence is said. A plain OK (the button or the words) adds nothing: the plan line is the answer. I'll read it before the mesh starts." while the card kept the old speed. A summary the model redoes in its own words that keeps an old sentence against what the user said last is rewritten with the user's values; an internal plan redone takes each opening's size and place back from the stage's declaration (the stored patches keep only names and roles). A TYPED ANSWER TO OUR OWN QUESTIONS is said back too, in one short sentence the intake's model writes from the stored values (`step_ack`, its own prompt blocks, a 3 s cap inside the plan step's 6 s, the code's sentence when refused, failed or slow): the purpose ("Okay, a CFD mesh with the flow around the part.") and through-or-around ("Okay, flow through the part.") before the stage line, inside its drawing mark - the naming is already queued, so the stage never waits on it - and the FEA analysis ("Okay, a vibration (modal) analysis.") before the plan line. Its guard: the route, flow and analysis taken and never another one, no number, no question, no step line (Proceed, the plan, the panel), no rule id, no exclamation, at most 160 characters. Conditions named before the stage (a fluid and its speed) are not asked: the first plan says what was used. Asked before the plan only when the application cannot build the plan itself (`conditions_due`). A conversation stored with the question before the plan still reads (`flow.EARLIER_LEADS`).
