## AIG: the AI never fails or retries a run

The rules plan comes first. An AI change is used only when it is valid and inside the rules' bounds,
and every AI error falls back to the rules. These rules cover the AI steps outside the FEA builder
(AI-fea-n above) and the verdict (VER-5).

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| AIG-1 | in force | **The rules build when the AI builder gives no mesh.** On cfMesh, VMTK, gmsh (CFD) and snappy multi-region, an attempt whose builder model was down, busy, slow, raised, or ended its rounds without submitting a mesh is built in the same attempt by the rules plan (`engines/rules_plan.py`): the same tools, validation and gates, the layer count the request names, every size from the engine's own defaults. It is never an infrastructure retry and never a failed run. The why record and the run's notes say so. `BUILDER_AI=off` builds every such attempt by the rules plan. |
| AIG-2 | in force | **The AI plan is never worse than the rules plan.** Before an AI builder plan is used, its planned cells (the engine's own estimate of the case configure_mesh just wrote, at the sizes the mesher really makes) are checked against the one-job cell limit. When the AI plan is over the limit and the rules plan (`engines/rules_plan.py`) is not, the rules plan is configured instead (`engines/plan_guard.py`), the builder's tool result says so in plain words with both numbers, and the why record cites this rule. An estimate that cannot be read, an engine with no rules arguments to compare, or a rules plan that fails or is no better leaves the validated AI plan in force. cfMesh carries an estimate; VMTK fits every strategy to the limit itself, the snappy planner holds max_cells to it and the FEA AI is bounded by AI-fea-5. |
| AIG-4 | in force | **An attempt the engine handed on is not built again by the rules plan** (rulebook 77; `agents/builder/invoke._handed_on`, `HANDED_ON_RULE`). AIG-1's rules plan builds an attempt the AI gave no mesh for; but when run_mesh itself ended the AI's turn (tools/meshing HANDS_OFF: no mesh, and none this engine makes of this case with this plan) the rules plan ran the same plan into the same failure (abc 63401: a second 4.5 minute run for the same zero-face outlet_3). Now the attempt ends there and the ladder decides what comes next. |

## SHELL and SURF: the mid-surface extraction and surface files (rows from SHELL-50 and SURF-14)

The SHELL and SURF families' earlier rules are written in `docs/design/shell/README.md` and cited in `cad/midsurface.py`,
`cad/shell_pairs.py`, `cad/surface_model.py` and `engines/surface_run.py`; from SHELL-50 and SURF-14 each new one also
has its row here.

| ID | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| SHELL-50 | in force | **Two free-form skins a wall apart have the offset of one as their mid-face** (`cad/midsurface.offset_mid`, `offset_skins`, `OFFSET_SKINS`, `OFFSET_OFF_MIDDLE` = 0.10, `OFFSET_VARIES` = 0.15). A pair whose two faces are both open B-splines (or Beziers) - a stamped or drawn panel's skins - gets as its mid-face the smaller skin's offset by half their mean wall toward the other, bounded in that skin's parameters (its outline, or where its outline and the other's overlap, worked out flat as SHELL-9 does). Kept only when every point read on it lies within 5% of the wall of the middle (`abs(dA - dB) / (dA + dB)` at most 0.10: a shell's bending stiffness about it within 3%) and the wall read across it varies by at most 15%; an uneven wall's thickness is then carried point by point (SHELL-9b). An even pair tries it before the overlap of its two half-offsets (a Boolean common of two different free-form surfaces lying on each other: past the 1800 s extraction deadline on 153 x 103-pole skins); a tapered pair tries it after its fitted mid-face is off the middle (17 and 33 midpoints a side miss beads and swages: up to 65% of the wall off). |
| SHELL-50b | in force | **A big even skin pair that is not each other's exact offset is read like a fitted wall** (`cad/midsurface._extract`, `WALL_VARIES` = 0.01). When SHELL-50's offset mid-face of an even pair reads a wall varying by over 1%, the mid-face is marked fitted: bridged to the mid-faces it continues (SHELL-10) and its wall carried point by point (SHELL-9b). |
| SHELL-50c | in force | **A small even skin pair is read exactly as before SHELL-50** (`cad/midsurface.big_skins`, `OFFSET_EVEN_POLES` = 5,000). An even pair takes SHELL-50's offset only when one of its skins has at least 5,000 poles: the overlap of two half-offsets stalled past the 1800 s deadline on 270 x 170, 283 x 203 and 153 x 103-pole stamped skins, and works on small ones. Read as offsets, a hat rail's small bend and joggle skins joined with degenerated edges on the flat faces beside them (4e-6 to 5e-5 mm), which crashed the mesher (SHELL-54) and, removed, still stopped gmsh; read as before, the six hat rails and the shroud whose shell had changed are byte for byte the base's again. Tapered pairs (SHELL-9 then SHELL-50) are not affected. |
| SHELL-51 | in force | **A wall thinner than its tessellation's error is no coincidence** (`cad/shell_pairs.ray_partners`, `COINCIDENT_RESCUE`, `LYING_FLOOR_FRACTION`). The pairing rays read two faces that share no edge and meet under the floor somewhere as lying on each other, and read through them out to the two tessellations' errors added (cad/fea_features COINCIDENT_BAND). On a 1000 x 700 mm floor panel 0.9 mm thick the skins' tessellations were 4.2 and 3.2 mm off their surfaces: they crossed, and all but 20 of the 19,955 rays across its wall were read through. A pair the plain reading nominates that the lying-on reading loses is given back; the exact test on the B-rep then settles it, and a given-back pair whose exact wall is under the floor (`FLOOR_FRACTION` of the diagonal) is no wall ("the faces lie on each other"). A part with no faces read as lying on each other is read exactly as before. |
| SHELL-52 | in force | **No quick inside test past its triangle budget** (`cad/midsurface.quick_triangles`, `QUICK_MAX_TRIANGLES` = 2,000,000; `_Solid(quick=...)`). A body's quick triangulation (within 0.5% of its thinnest wall) is first estimated from the pairing's tessellation: each face's triangles times that tessellation's measured error over the quick deflection (a curved face's count grows as one over the deflection; a flat face's does not), never fewer than it has. A body whose estimate passes the budget is told by the exact classifier alone - the same answers, read slower: the 0.9 mm floor panel asked for a copy meshed within 0.0045 mm (about 200 million triangles; at 0.2 mm it already took 318,000 and 1.9 GB) and the extraction process died. Any other body is read as before. |
| SHELL-53 | in force | **The joins' distance readings skip what a box already settles** (`cad/midsurface.bridges`, `_free_edges`, `_geometry_box`, `_box_gap`, `BOX_PRUNE`). SHELL-10's bridges read every free edge of every mid-face against every other mid-face, and each candidate pair of edges at ten points, with an exact distance each; a mid-face or an edge whose box (read on the exact geometry) lies farther than the reach or the tolerance is not read, and a pair stops being read at its first point past the reach. No point of a shape lies nearer than its box, so every answer is the same; only the time changes (a 1,451-face antenna cover sat in the bridges for over 20 minutes). Also SHELL-53's: a face's area read once (kept by the face itself), and the box test in plain comparisons. |
| SHELL-54 | in force | **An edge with no 3D curve has no length** (`cad/midsurface._length`). A degenerated edge (a point a face's outline passes through) measures 0 instead of being handed to OpenCASCADE's linear properties, which read a curve that is not there: the mesher's edge census (SHMESH-35, `_interior_faces`) of a sewn mid-surface with such edges died in a segmentation fault (a hat rail, crit mode: the mesh child's EOFError at 13 to 21 s). Every edge with a curve measures as before. |
| SHELL-55 | in force | **A wall is read where its faces face each other** (`cad/midsurface._extract`, `EVEN_WHERE_FACING`). When the even-wall test (`shell_pairs.even_wall`, every grid point of the smaller face against the other face's whole surface) fails, the pair reads as a taper, its fitted mid-face cannot be made, and the tapered reading (`shell_pairs.tapered_wall`, only the points whose foot lies on the other face itself) found one thickness within EVEN_SHARE and the faces parallel within PAIR_DEG, the pair is an even wall and its mid-face is the overlap of the two half-offsets. A printed funnel's two coaxial 45-degree cones 7.8 mm apart read "turned 90 degrees" near the spout (feet past the other cone's apex), the taper fit through those points was 35% off its middle, and the funnel had no wall at all. A taper whose fitted mid-face is made, and every other pair, reads as before (applied before the fit, the rule changed the joins of a 1,451-face cover: 31 open edges became 115). |
| SHELL-56 | in force | **A thick region is thick metal, not a speck** (`cad/midsurface._drop_specks`, `SPECK_FLOOR`, `SPECK_WALLS2` = 4). A mixed body's region of thick or solid faces is a thick region only when its surface is at least 4 times the square of the thickest shell wall it touches (its faces' neighbours, else theirs, else the body's thickest shell wall): a boss two walls across and one wall high has about 9. A region under that (a counterbore's seat, a 4 mm land, a pin tip, a dimple) is counted in the body's row as `specks` and is not listed among the regions that stay solid; a body whose regions are all specks reads "shell". Reporting only: the faces' roles, the mid-surface, its joins and the record's checks are made exactly as before. On the board, any floor from 4 to 6 walls squared turns the same 8 of the 50 mixed parts to shell, each a plate with seats, lands or dimples in its render. |
| SHELL-57 | in force | **A wall region is as wide as its steps let it be** (`cad/midsurface._region_steps`, `REGION_STEPS`). (its shell elements were past Nastran's limits at the junction, and the all-shell mesh is inside them), so they are left out."); when it is past them too (or cannot be made) the two are level on that rank and the kept one is delivered over it (more of the part's structure), its words saying so. Should the two readings still disagree (the solid route made no solid and sent the file on, and the surface route finds its faces close), the part is not handed back: with a shell asked for, its faces are meshed as the part's skin and the user is told why; with none asked for, no shell is made (as before). ## Reserved families

These ID families are reserved. Their rules are written by the PRs that build them, and no code
may cite them before then.

| Family | For | Written by |
|---|---|---|
| `PLAN-<engine>-n` | the numbers of each engine's computed plan (`PLAN-fea` above is the FEA path's) | D4 to D8 |
| `LAD-<engine>-n` | each engine's fixed ladder of approved levers | D4 to D8 |
| `SHELL-n`, `SHMESH-n` | shell meshing: wall pairs, shell or solid, mid-surfaces and their sizing (`docs/design/shell/README.md`); since SHELL-23 every plate-like wall is a shell when the user asks for one (the 10 walls of SHELL-2 are one line of advice, not a gate) and SHELL-24 makes a short tapered plate (a rolled flange) one flat plate at its mean thickness; SHELL-25 fits a drafted wall two walls wide; SHELL-26 makes the shell without its short plates when the mesher cannot close them; SHMESH-51 (rulebook 71) decides the rings round a hole by what the hole is - a bolt hole (it lines up with a hole in another plate, it is one of a pattern of alike holes, or its plate is joined to another) gets two rings of quads where they fit inside the user's edges and the room round it, else one, with the reason recorded and told; SHMESH-51b to 51e (same rulebook) plan a gridded face's grid and its two rings together - the grid comes first, each box sized for its band, bands with unequal sides, one ring per hole that cannot have two, and (51e) no grid line ends on a two-ring box's side: it stops at the last line before the box, the box's sides hold the band's count and the cells beside take it; SHMESH-51f/51g (same rulebook): a pass gathers its faults before the model is meshed again (grid cells past the limits with a refusal), keeps the cells it frees by keys that outlive gmsh's tags across redraws and restarts that cannot touch them, counts bolt-hole rounds per face and never lets one face's refusal send another face's holes to one ring; a gridded face refused after meshing for elements past the solver's limits or over the user's longest edge, all in or beside two-ring boxes, sends only those holes to one ring; SHMESH-51h (same rulebook): such a round puts every fault it can on a hole at once (bands not made, elements past the limits or over the longest edge, and against the free mesh triangles and short edges, in or beside a two-ring box), and rounds go on while they send holes (up to 8 a face); SHMESH-51i (same rulebook): a line held back from a bolt box ends on a node of the box's side (whole segments either side, its last piece slanted at most one segment, the slanted cells checked against the solver's limits and the user's quad lines; else it stops one line earlier), so the cells beside the box map; SHMESH-51j (same rulebook): a bolt hole sent to one ring for a fault in its rings keeps its two-ring box (place, size, count; one row out to its two rings' outer circle, where that row is inside the solver's limits, the quad lines and the user's edges), so no line of its face moves; a fault beside a box is put on its hole (a box of its own) in a face's first 2 rounds only, then SHMESH-51b; a face gridded on an earlier pass that is not cut after a round of one ring is refused (SHMESH-51b), never left free without a word; SHMESH-51k/51l (same rulebook): a band's nodes move with the relaxing and mending, and go back to where the band put them wherever a quad of it would come out past the solver's limits or over the user's longest edge; SHMESH-51m/51n (same rulebook): where two joined plates' boxes stand right beside their joint, a line that would land on the joint inside the other plate's two-ring box column lands on a node of that box's side (its last piece slanted; a line the plate only carries moves whole), and a joint with a plate that has no holes is not split there; a chain facing a two-ring box's side takes its count from the box (each piece's class follows), and a 4-segment side is no longer given 6 (its band always failed); SHMESH-51o (same rulebook): a mesh whose two-ring faces carry triangles is made again with one ring on those faces (then on every bolt face) and the one with fewer triangles is kept unless it is worse on the solver's limits or the user's lines; SHMESH-51p (same rulebook): in a face with bolt holes two lines nearer than a tenth of a face size are one line even past their limits; SHMESH-51q (same rulebook): a CAD piece of a hole's own circle is no cell edge at planning (the hole is drawn again as one circle when its ring asks); SHMESH-51r (same rulebook): a side landing inside the other plate's two-ring box column first meets that box's corner, so both chains stay whole; the guard's one-ring trial keeps its faces' bolt boxes owned; SHMESH-51o3/51o4 (same rulebook): that trial is never kept when it loses grids; the first trial is the face with the most triangles of its own near its two-ring holes, alone, then (time allowing) the other such faces; SHMESH-51o5 (same rulebook): a trial is kept only when every face it switches has fewer triangles of its own (those that gain are tried alone once), triangles that move on other faces are told, never taken as the face's; SHMESH-51o7 (same rulebook): a four-sided straight-edged hole the square plan takes is a bolt hole (a carriage bolt's) when it lines up with a hole of about its size in another plate or its inscribed circle is within 15% of a round bolt hole on its plate, and its square ring then takes two rows where they fit; SHMESH-51o8/51o9 (same rulebook): in a face gridded with others along its joints every bolt box owns its sides and keeps its kites under the longest edge; SHMESH-51o10 (same rulebook): a round plate's band round its opening is meshed in rings of the ring's count where the opening's segments stay at half the size or more; SHMESH-51o11 (same rulebook): SHMESH-51b's one ring takes a face's square bolt holes back to one row too; SHMESH-51o12..51o15 (same rulebook): 51b names every face with two-ring bolt holes, the record gives triangles and past-limit elements per face, one ring held for a grid stays in the free face when the grid is refused, and a two-ring box side keeps its count against a class too long for it (the cells beside it go free); SHMESH-51o16/51o17 (same rulebook): a strip one cell from a two-ring box takes its count, and a free four-sided cell of n and n + 2k segments takes rows with three-to-one steps; SHMESH-51o18/51o19 (same rulebook): the guard takes one ring for triangles only where it takes off three quarters of a face's own, and a pass's bolt holes and its faces' 51b one ring go in one start again; SHMESH-51o20 (same rulebook): a class holding a two-ring box side and held elsewhere at another count splits at a transition cell beside the holding curve; SHMESH-51o21 (same rulebook): a grid with no triangle refused for short edges alone, on a face with holes, is kept without a free-mesh trial; SHMESH-51o22/51o23 (same rulebook): transition cells in the faces a grid cuts too, pinned and guarded as bands; SHMESH-48x3 (same rulebook, the user's choice): a grid with no triangle refused for its share under the user's shortest edge comes back when its free mesh has triangles and that line is no worse with it, told in the record; the numbers in the note are placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | the shell PRs 1 to 5 |
| `MIXED-n`, `SHLINK-n` | mixed shell and solid parts: the thick cores, the cut into the walls, the shell-to-solid join, Auto's Mixed, and walls short for their thickness (MIXED-8: a wall reaching under 10 of its walls from its nearest joint or thick region; kept solid until SHELL-23, since then meshed as shell and named in one line of advice) (`docs/design/shell/README.md` 2.6); MIXED-11 (`cad/mixed_split.thickness`, `CORE_THICKNESS`): a left-over piece MIXED-1b turns down (twice its volume over its area under half the thinnest wall) is measured by its deepest point - twice the largest distance from a point inside it to its skin - and is a core when that reaches the floor; a compact lump reads s / 3 by MIXED-1b (cen_s0143's two 10 x 10 x 2 mm pads read 1.05 mm against 1.25), a 0.6 mm ring round a hole still is no core; a piece MIXED-1b takes is taken as before; MIXED-10 (`engines/mixed_run.build`, `WHY_SPLIT`): when the thin walls and the thick regions cannot be cut apart and no wall was kept solid (MIXED-8 advice only), the stop gives the split's own reason, never "walls too thick for shells" (34 of 51 board parts were told that); placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | shell PR 3 |
| `SURF-n` | a CAD file that is a surface, not a solid (rulebook 59; `cad/surface_model.py`, `engines/surface_run.py`, `contracts/shell_thickness.py`, `application/surface_check.py`; `docs/design/shell/README.md` 11). **SURF-1**: an FEA file with no solid whose faces do not close after the clean check's own sewing is a surface part, meshed as a shell without the user saying so, unless its openings span at most 1% of its surface (a closed skin with a gap: stopped as before, with both ways on); the user's words (shell, a thickness) settle a file under the 1% as a surface. **SURF-2**: sewn within the clean check's tolerance only, every free edge left free; a rib's foot is joined to the plate it stands on (three faces at one edge sewn; a foot inside a face joined by scoring that face along it, no face moved); any other edge lying along a neighbouring face within 5 tolerances is counted as not meeting and said, never closed. **SURF-3**: the surface is the mid-surface - the extractor's own record is built from it (a part and a wall per sheet, a mid-face and group per CAD face) and the existing shell path meshes, writes and delivers it as the result; there is no solid to fall back to. **SURF-4**: the thickness is the user's words (a fixed word table; a bare number is millimetres; the latest word stands; one thickness for the file), never guessed; a bare gauge number, a range and a second thickness are said, not applied. **SURF-5**: with no thickness given the shell is still delivered - sized as for a thin sheet unless the notes give a global size (SURF-7) (its size over 300; again at twice and four times that wall while elements are left past Nastran's own limits), every property carrying 0 under a "WALL THICKNESS NOT SET" head - never asked for, never refused. **SURF-6**: what the user reads: one line on the stage and the result ("This file is a surface, not a solid. It is meshed as a shell, 1.2 mm thick."), and no "not clean" for a sheet's own open edges; **SURF-7** (rulebook 61, the combined test branch; `engines/surface_requirements.py`): the mesh requirements in the user's notes (NOTE-7) reach a surface file - the driver reads them BEFORE the surface is meshed, once per run; their sizes, quality limits and element kind are the shell mesher's as on any shell (SHMESH-24..26) and each is told asked against delivered. ONE thickness comes out of the two places one can be read: the word table (SURF-4: the notes, else the stage, else the request) wins whenever it reads one - it is the thickness the stage showed the user before Proceed, and fixed rules read it the same every run, model or no model; the notes' typed set (NOTE-7's thickness) is used only when the word table read nothing and refused nothing (a bare gauge number or a range stays refused); when both read one and they differ, the word table's is used and the other is said as a second thickness the notes name. On a surface file the thickness row reads "met - every shell property carries 1.2 mm" (a solid part's reads "measured from your CAD"). With no notes nothing is asked, cited, said or written (NOTE-9). With a global size and NO thickness the sheet is worked at the size asked - a working wall of the global size, so the shell rules' own floor (half a wall, SHMESH-2) is half the size, the shortest edge the usual criteria list allows - never at SURF-5's thin-sheet guess, and it is meshed once; no thickness is written. **SURF-8** (input stream; SURF-7 is the land branch's): a surface part's gaps are closed up a measured sewing ladder (2, 5 sew tolerances; by rule never past 5 = 1/2000 of the part, the reach SURF-2 already called "does not meet"), every pair of joined edges judged before a rung is kept (two different faces, not lying on each other, the gap within a tenth of the narrower face, no face or other free edge lost); a join that fails is left and said while the others are taken one at a time (pieces of an edge cut at a T-meet traced to their face by where they lie); a stated tolerance never leaves more open than the rule's (the rule's result is kept, said); a larger tolerance is offered only once tried on the file and found to close the gaps; a stitch tolerance in the notes ("stitch gaps up to 0.5 mm") replaces the rule's limit and the face cap, "do not stitch" turns it off; what is open is ONE count whatever tolerance was asked - places where free edges lie along another face within 1/100 of the part, a gap counted once (its pieces and its two sides together) - used by the record, the words and the stated-tolerance check; the words add up ("Closed K of the N places", then N - K left); places within what was stitched are said with why, wider ones with a tolerance only when it was tried on the file and closed them. **SURF-9**: a face written twice (same area, same box, each lying on the other within the sew tolerance) is left out once before anything is sewn. **SURF-10**: an inner loop of a face no bigger across than the gaps being closed (or than the size the notes name: "ignore holes under 5 mm") is filled with the face's own surface. **SURF-12**: on the surface route only, the places SURF-8 counts are made to meet by changing the surfaces, one at a time, nearest first - an edge stopping short extended along its own surface (a natural patch over more of its own surface, a B-spline continued with its own derivatives and kept within a tenth of the sew tolerance of the old, a trimmed face by a strip of its own surface whose sides continue its own edges) and cut where it meets its neighbour, an overlap trimmed back along the neighbour's edge, a step moved onto its neighbour; each change valid, its normal side kept, its area within a strip of the reach, crossing no other face, kept only when less is left open; both faces of a place tried; the cheapest change first (extended or trimmed along its own surface, and only then the edge moved onto its neighbour); a gap is closed when it is narrower than one element - the size the user asked for, else the run's own (its largest sheet's base size) - never past 1/100 of the part, or up to the notes' "close gaps up to 4 mm"; "do not change the surfaces" turns it off; every change told, one sentence per place (what, by how much, where). **SURF-13**: what is still open after SURF-12 and stitching, narrower than the same reach, is closed in the shell mesh (`engines/surface_mesh_close.py`): the free nodes facing each other across the gap joined half way, a node with no partner put into the edge it faces (its element cut in two or three), no node moved farther than the gap; kept only when no element turns or folds and none is past Nastran's own limits that was not before, else the mesh there is as meshed and the place told open with why; "do not stitch" / "do not close gaps" and "do not change the surfaces" turn it off. **SURF-11**: the element count the notes name reaches a surface file (the FEA plan's own reader, asked only when there are words), as it reaches a solid; placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | the surface-files PR |
| `SHSTAGE-n` | the FEA stage's "Mesh as: Auto / Shell / Solid" and the run it settles (rulebook 58): the shell check after the part is measured, Auto's recommendation with its reason (Shell when every body is a shell with no open edge and 90% of the surface thin; Shell + solid only when MIXED-4 and MIXED-8 pass with every wall's reach measured; Solid otherwise), the user's pick or words never overridden, the choice settled at Proceed, and the shell or mixed files made beside the solid deck, which is always delivered (`docs/design/shell/README.md` 7); placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | the shell land PR |
| `SHWRITE-n` | the shell solver files' parts (`engines/shell_writers.py`, `docs/design/shell/README.md` 4). **SHWRITE-1**: every element of a shell (or mixed, or surface-file) deck is in the CAD part (body or sheet) it came from, named as the CAD names it - the STEP names the assembly staging reads into assembly.json (`cad/fea_assembly.read_body_names`, the same names the result viewer lists, `engines/shell_delivery.part_names`), else Part_k. The name is the instance the assembly places when it is the product's own name with an instance number ("PEDESTAL FOOTPAD GUSSET.57", eight gussets of one product keep eight names), else the product's; a name that starts with an assembly's name above it or the file's own name loses that prefix when a real name is left. One property per part and wall, numbered part by part: a part of one property gives it the part's name, a part of several walls one per wall, "<part> t=<wall>mm". Nastran: the part's elements follow its `$HMNAME COMP k"<part>"` line (HyperMesh's component, k its MAT1), each PSHELL is named on the two lines before it (`$ Femap Property i : <name>`, Femap's title; `$HMNAME PROP i"<name>"`, HyperMesh's); Abaqus / CalculiX: an ELSET per part named after it (letters, digits and _, a letter first, unique without regard to case) and a *SHELL SECTION per property; Ansys: an element component per part named after it and a SECTYPE per property named after it. A mixed mesh's solid elements are in their part's ELSET, component and HyperMesh component. The face-pair groups stay as they were; the mesh is not touched (node and element numbers as before); placeholders until the rulebook numbers them | the shell part-names PR |
